# Changelog

Agent Durbar `v0.1.0` is the first stable release. Builds are unsigned and not notarized (see "Honest limits" in the README for where each OS has been run). Each entry names the spec that describes the behavior and how it was checked.

## Unreleased (on `main`, after the v0.1.19 tag)

Not released yet: each change is a file in [`changelog.d/`](changelog.d/) until the next release folds them in (`npm run changelog:preview` lists them).

<!-- changelog.d: fragments only; a bullet here fails npm run changelog:check -->

## v0.1.19 ([release](https://github.com/three-feathers/agent-durbar/releases/tag/v0.1.19), 2026-11-14)

- **Adding a folder tells you which floor it went to** (spec 033, issue #1709, owner report): a folder added while its floor was off or not on screen looked as if nothing happened, so it was added again and again. Now the Add a folder dialog names the floor and lets you pick another (**Put it on floor**), a floor that was off is switched on, a toast names the floor with **Show** (it never moves you by itself) and says it aloud, and adding a folder that is already in the office starts no second session and says on which floor it is. Checked on the simulator only.
- **The red confirm button of a destructive dialog reads in the arcade and under the Retro Pixel theme** (spec 052, issue #1565): End all sessions, Reset usage totals, Delete session, profile or note and Remove were drawn in the dialog's own colour with text in that colour too (contrast 1:1), so the button looked like an empty box. They are red again with text at 6:1 or better at rest and hovered. The hovered button also keeps its contrast in dark themes (it was 4.14:1). The Photo office was not affected.
- **Every destructive dialog's red confirm button is now checked** (spec 052 FR-053, FR-076, issue #1647): a new simulator check opens all 19 `Delete`, `Remove`, `Reset`, `Revoke`, `End` and `Discard` confirmations and reads their red button at rest, focused and hovered (4.5:1 or better) in the arcade, the pixel theme, the Cave world and three retro-style user themes, at two window sizes. Nothing was wrong, so no colour changed; the check stops a new dialog from shipping a blank red button. Checked in the simulator on Linux only.
- **Activity tab uses the window width** (spec 052, issue #1466): Tools > Activity no longer sits in the 880px column, so Decision is no longer cut off and Action wraps less; Time, App, Mode and Decision stay on one line. Other Tools tabs and screens are unchanged. Checked in the simulator at 1440x900 and 1280x800 (`check-activity-table`).
- **A refused MCP token no longer asks you to sign in** (spec 058 FR-232, issue #1621): when a remote MCP server whose entry sends its own token (a credential header such as `Authorization` or `X-API-Key`, or a `${NAME}` variable) and has no OAuth answers 403, its row now reads *Refused: token not accepted* with "Check the header or its variable; Sign in does not change it." instead of *Refused: needs sign-in*, and no longer offers *Sign in again…*, which could not change a header. When the header uses a variable that Agent Durbar's own environment does not have, the row names that variable, never its value (and only a name that looks like a variable name, so a key pasted inside `${...}` is never shown). A 401 and servers with OAuth keep the sign-in. Unit tests and the simulator; not checked against a real server.
- **MCP errors hide the Copilot CLI list's keys too** (spec 069 FR-070, issue #1639): a server from `~/.copilot/mcp-config.json` that echoed one of its header or env values in an error showed it under *Details* and in *Copy diagnostics* when the value had no known token shape. Those values now join the list of values that mask a Copilot session's MCP errors, tool cards, edits and history, like the folder extras and Claude Code's list already did; a value under a secret-looking name (`SMTP_PASSWORD`, `X-Api-Key`, `PIN_CODE`, `SLACK_WEBHOOK`, `SENTRY_DSN`) is always masked, and so is a URL whose path or host carries an id, while under other names plain settings such as `true`, `production`, `1234`, `https://api.github.com`, `/usr/local/bin` or `us-east-1` are left out so they are not hidden everywhere; a value stays masked for the rest of the run after the file changes, and notes and conversation search use these values also while the key vault is locked. They stay in main and are never shown or logged.
- **Expand the browser in a chat** (spec 080 FR-134..FR-139, issue #1691): the browser bar's new last button, **Expand browser**, makes the Browser tab fill the whole window, so a page is as big as in your usual browser; **Back to small browser** (the same button) or **Esc** puts it back next to the chat, with your draft still there. Esc pressed inside the web page still goes to the page. Checked on the simulator in Flat, the Photo office and the Retro arcade at 1440x900 and 1280x800.
- **Fixed: the Open source check no longer fails when the last reply is still finishing** (spec 083 T068, issue #1697): `check-notes-source` waits for the reply bubble and an idle chat, and records what the screen reader region announced instead of reading its last text; a late "Reply finished" no longer replaces "Message shown." in the check. Test only, the app is unchanged; the mutant that removes the announcement still fails it.
- **Search conversations** (spec 095, issue #877): a box in the top bar (or its magnifier on a narrow window; **Ctrl+Shift+F**, ⇧⌘F on a Mac, changeable in Settings > Keyboard, also in Ctrl+K) finds a word or phrase in the title, your messages and the agent's replies of every open session and every earlier session of the folders you have open (earlier Copilot and Codex sessions by title only; tool output and hidden sessions are not searched). Results show in the List view, grouped *In the office* and *Earlier sessions*, with up to three masked excerpts and the words marked; Enter or a click opens the chat (or resumes the session, or opens the VS Code chat) at that message. Local only: nothing is stored, the search runs in Durbar on your computer, excerpts are masked before you see them, and the end line says what was not searched. Simulator-checked at 1440x900 and 1280x800 (`shots-095`); not tried on a real store.
- **A slow start no longer makes the Retro arcade or the Prehistoric cave request the Photo office's floor picture** (spec 097 FR-363, issue #1238): the Photo floor is now set only under the Photo world's own rule, so a world stylesheet that arrives late cannot pull in another world's files. The Photo office looks the same.
- **The simulator's office now starts with one layout search instead of sometimes two** (spec 097 FR-363, issue #1238): with 15 or 40 simulated agents the start is steadier and does less work (about 47 probes instead of 85 at 40 agents when the race hit), and the start budgets in `check-office-ops` are tightened to 1 search and 20 and 50 probes. Simulator only: the real app's start-up is unchanged and not measured.
- **A lounge seat's Draft tag no longer covers the person** (spec 097 FR-316, issue #1511): in the Real office (the Retro arcade and the Photo office) the Draft tag of a person seated in the lounge lay over the person's stool and foot; it now stands on top of the seat's small screen. The office overlap check fails a Draft tag that overlaps a lounge person's body box. The placement is the team's choice; the owner has not confirmed it.
- **Hover card no longer lost when a neighbour's desk slides under a resting pointer** (spec 097, issue #1536): in the Retro arcade, when an agent fell asleep and the layout moved another desk under a still pointer, the held card could close. A held card is now let go only by a real pointer move. Checked in the simulator on Linux (`check-097-hover-card`).
- **The schedule letter flies in the Real office** (spec 097 FR-377, issue #1587): when a schedule fires, the small letter now flies from the Postbox to that agent's name plate in the Photo office, the Prehistoric cave and the Retro arcade, as it already did in the flat office (it never flew in a Real office before). It is skipped, as before, under reduced motion, and now also when the Postbox or the plate is outside the window (the flat office too).
- **Helper kids art, and no mug or lamp on the desks** (spec 097 FR-371, FR-374, issue #1641): the Photo office desk lost the white mug at its right end and the Prehistoric cave desk lost its small oil lamp (the same desks otherwise, same size). The six clay-toy helper kids (seven poses each, 42 small pictures and their sizes in the Photo office's `world.json`) are the pictures of the helper kids that play on the Photo office desks (issue #1642, the next fragment). Checked by `test:realoffice-assets` (size caps, metadata); the owner looked at the kids as shipped and said they are ok (2026-11-13).
- **Helpers are clay-toy kids playing on the desk in the Photo office** (spec 097, issue #1642): with 1 to 4 agents on screen, each helper is a little clay kid standing on its agent's desk beside the person, in a pose for what it is doing (building blocks, thinking, waiting by a toy alarm clock, a toppled tower when it failed, cheering when done), up to 6 kids and then a **+K** block that opens the agent's chat. Hover a kid for its task, click it (or Enter) for its activity. With more agents the small round heads under each plate stay, and now show the kids' faces.
- **Worlds must be distinct in their pictures, not only on their cards** (spec 102 FR-016, T093, issue #1402): `npm test` now fails when a world reuses another world's fixture file (`test:world-distinct`), and `npm run check:world-distinct` (Electron, dev-side) also finds recoloured or re-encoded copies and floors, walls or desks of the same average colour, with limits measured on the three real worlds. A pair listed in `worlds/known-clashes.json` that no longer clashes now fails, so the Cave PR must delete its entry. Nothing visible in the app.
- **A contact sheet of every world's pictures, and a required `distinct-check` on each world's card** (spec 102 FR-017, T094, issue #1402): `npm run world:contact-sheet` draws one JPEG (git-ignored, never committed) with each world's wall, floor, desk, monitor and fixtures side by side for the owner's look. Each `worlds/<id>/card.json` now needs `distinct-check`: `pending` until a person has looked, then passed with a name, a date and how many worlds were compared; `test:world-catalogue` fails a card without it. The three existing cards are pending. Nothing visible in the app.
- Nothing changes yet for Retro Pixel users (spec 102, T227, issue #1400): the one-time move of a stored Retro Pixel theme to the Retro pixel people is built but switched off until the Retro arcade ships, so there is no visible change until the flip. Checked by `test:characters`.
- **Retro arcade: the narrowest plate keeps its status word and marks a failed helper** (spec 102, issues #1574 and #1600): on the 122 px plate of the narrow cabinet (the fallback plan and rooms of 20 or more agents) "Limit reached" is no longer cut, in the system font or in a wider one (regular weight there, a slightly wider box): the plate's own dot is left out and the helper count reads `+3!` (the mark in the warning colour) when a helper failed or waits, with or without a pencil; the helper marks are 4 px from the word. Only that plate's name and tooltip say it in words; the default and cave offices are unchanged.
- **Arcade no longer scrolls with a status filter set** (spec 102, issue #1588): at 1280x800 with 11 to 15 agents and a status pill on, the arcade's bottom row was cut off; the hall now also takes the filter note's spare top padding (keeping the note clear). Checked in the simulator by `check-102-arcade-filter-note`, run on macOS only: the bug was measured at 1280x800 on macOS, and Windows and Linux font metrics were not measured, so only the check, not the bug, is known to be macOS-specific.
- Retro arcade: with a notice strip showing at 1280 wide, a folder of 4 or fewer agents is no longer split across two rows when 10 agents are in 3 folders; the hall takes the floor's padding to keep it whole (spec 102, T342, issue #1598).
- **Retro arcade: the focused Office/List chip shows its keyboard focus** (spec 102, issue #1666): the selected top-bar chip had a dark ring inside its yellow fill that read 1.03:1 against the dark strip, so it looked unfocused. It now has a light ring outside the chip. Measured in the simulator at 1440x900 and 1280x800 (`check-052-focus-ring`, 16.4:1); the Flat, Photo and dark looks are unchanged.
- **Settings > World: pick where you work from picture cards** (spec 102 FR-300, FR-301, FR-302; T266 in part): the Display section's *Office layout* and *World* rows are replaced by a **World** section with a card for **Plain** (today's flat office) and one for the **Photo office**, each with a picture of the app in that world, its name, one line and *Built in*. The chosen card has a 2 px edge and an *In use* label; the arrow keys move between cards and Space or Enter choose it, and it applies at once; the picture zooms toward the people on hover and focus (not with Reduced motion or Motion off). Nothing is migrated and your choice is kept where it was. Worlds that are not open yet are not listed. Checked by `check-settings-worlds` (with mutants).
- **Office people in Settings > Characters, with Retro pixel shown as Not open yet** (spec 102 T290..T293, spec 077 FR-078): Office people can be picked in any Real office world. Retro pixel is a disabled "Not open yet" card in this release (the `retro` flag is off); a saved choice is kept, with a line saying what is drawn meanwhile, and Retro pixel people draw in the Photo office and the cave once the flag opens. The Photo office keeps its own look when it draws the Retro pixel people. In Flat both draw the theme's own figures for now (the Flat heads are the next step). Checked by `test:characters`, `test:realoffice-layout`, `check-characters` and `check-097-crews`.
- Spec note only: Retro pixel and Office people as Characters choices in every world (spec 102 T225, split into T290..T296, with new running frames for Retro pixel people as an art task) are specified; the owner answered every question. No code change.
- **World previews, taken by the app itself** (spec 102, FR-302): a new simulator scene, `simscene=preview`, shows three people idle at their own desks with nothing on top, and a driver takes one 640 px picture per world (Plain, Photo office, Prehistoric cave, Retro arcade), each under 48 KB, with the spot the card will zoom to. They ship with the app for the World section of Settings (not shown there yet); `npm run shots:docs` retakes them. Checked in the simulator on Linux at 1440x900 and 1280x800; no AI credits.
- **Team templates: the window's channels** (spec 111 T007, FR-010, FR-023, FR-100): `teams:list`, `teams:put`, `teams:delete`, `teams:duplicate` and `teams:markMine` (window only, through `ipcHandle`), with the preload calls, the `api.ts` types and an in-memory simulator fake. Main checks every argument (types, sizes, ids through a `Map`, own keys only) and refuses with a sentence that names the field, never the value. Main gives every member a store-only `memberId` (128 random bits) on create and duplicate; the window cannot send one on a creation, and on an update a member keeps its id and its imported-brief mark by `memberId` (a reorder or a removal cannot move a mark; an unknown or repeated id is refused). `teams:markMine` is the only way to clear a brief's file mark for a member that keeps its `memberId` (a member sent as new loses it, a known gap of FR-023) and refuses anything else in the call. A window `put` is always `own`. A save that comes back `changed` or `failed` says so (with the reloaded list for `changed`) so the editor can keep what was typed, and `blocked` tells the window to show a notice. Nothing visible yet (the editor is T008/T009). The store gives every stored member its own `memberId` at the load and in `putImported` (a hand-edited file cannot make a mark vanish), `put` and `markMine` refuse a stored template that still lacks them, and the ops copy the window's argument from its own data properties only (no getter runs). `teams:list` also says once when a bad file was set aside (`setAside`), `putImported` gives every member a fresh id, a handler that throws answers one fixed `failed`, and the window's value is bounded before any copy (32 keys in a template, 64 in a member, 5,000 values in all; fixed sentences, no key named). Checked by `test:teamsipc` (380 checks, 42 mutants seen to fail, one equivalent named) and `test:teamstore`.

## v0.1.18 ([release](https://github.com/three-feathers/agent-durbar/releases/tag/v0.1.18), 2026-11-13)

- **A faster, stricter pass in the long-chat check** (spec 005 FR-210, issue 1610): `check-chat-visibility` reached the 600 s ui-suite limit on a loaded Linux run. Its one pass over the whole chat now steps 1.5 screens and asserts that every one of the 4,000 messages was laid out in some frame (a step of 6 screens fails with `CVPASS=6`); the two size entries also get a 900 s limit as a stated margin (it hides slowness, it does not remove it).
- **Fixed: a DPR 2 UI check on Linux no longer runs on a screen too small for its window** (spec 008 FR-213, issue #1591): `scripts/ui-suite.js` started each driver under `xvfb-run` with Xvfb's default 1280x1024 screen, so at `UI_SUITE_DPR=2` most of a 1440x900 window was off screen and painted-pixel checks could not see the lower rooms. The screen is now 1920x1100 times the density at colour depth 24 (`UI_SUITE_XVFB_SCREEN=WxHxD` overrides it). macOS and Windows are unchanged. Checked by `test:uisuite`; the Xvfb run itself was not done (no Linux here).
- **Fixed: `check-ui-text` no longer reads a dialog's buttons in their hover colours** (spec 008 FR-214): the real pointer rests where the window sits on the screen, so the larger Xvfb screen left it over End-all (4.14:1); the check now moves the pointer off the page first and fails with `is hovered at the read` if a control still is.
- **Fixed: two more colour-reading UI checks no longer depend on where the pointer rests** (spec 008 FR-214): `check-097-chat-contrast` and `shots-102-arcade` move the pointer off the page and assert no control is hovered before they read colours.
- Fixed: the UI checks' pointer reset no longer reports a slow first run as a hovered control (spec 008 FR-215, issue #1648): `pointerOff` re-sends the move to 0,0 every 250 ms for up to 3 s and, if a control is still hovered then, fails with `the pointer did not leave` and the list.
- **The UI shards are balanced again** (spec 008 FR-189): `scripts/ui-suite-timings.json`, the seconds per UI check that only decide which shard runs which check, is refreshed from the median of five green main runs (mean of Linux and Windows). 23 checks that were missing (the slowest are `check-needs-panel`, `check-ui-text` and `check-chat-visibility`) now have a time. With the new times the predicted slowest and fastest of 4 shards are 1.01 apart (they were 1.65 with the old file), and 1.01 apart of 3 shards (was 1.44). Checked by `test:uisuite`; no app code changes.
- **Stacked PRs no longer clash in `npm run specs:numbers`** (spec 021 FR-094): a PR stacked on another inherits its spec numbers (only what it adds beyond the shared history counts; a fork PR never joins a stack); a separate PR taking the same number is still a clash. Tests: `npm run test:specnumbers`; to check afterwards, re-run the `Spec numbers and picture budget` job on #1232 once this is on main (#1240 still needs its own fixes).
- **The primary button reads at 4.5:1 in a user theme with the retro look, at rest, hovered and pressed** (spec 052, issue #1614): a user theme that picked the retro look and left the button colours alone got Clean Office's pale pair, 3.8:1 at rest and 2.8:1 hovered (the hover mixed in black, which only lowered it). The hover now mixes toward the button's own text where that keeps 4.5:1, and away from it (black under light text, white under dark text) where moving toward it would lower the contrast, as on a blue, brown or grey fill; the theme repair holds the text to 4.5:1 on the rest and the hovered fill, for every fill and text colour tried. The built-in themes and the Clean look are unchanged in contrast terms (Retro Pixel 15.3 rest, 11.4 hovered).
- **The keyboard focus ring on the Tools, Settings, Office and List buttons and on the ⋯ menu items reads on the retro look** (spec 052, issue #1615): the ring was yellow on the cream strip and the cream menu (1.0 to 1.2:1; the pressed Office button was yellow on yellow). The menu items now use the page's ring colour and the four buttons an inset ring in the text colour of their own fill (15.3:1 in Retro Pixel; 3.8 to 12.1:1 in a user theme with the retro look). The buttons on the dark bar keep the yellow ring. The arcade bar is unchanged and stays a known gap.
- **The focus-ring check no longer fails on a busy runner** (spec 052, issue #1651): `check-052-focus-ring` now waits until its first Tab press has moved focus before it focuses the first control, instead of racing it. Test driver only, nothing changes in the app.
- A failed server's row in the MCP list keeps a readable text column: the name and the help text no longer get squeezed by the buttons, which wrap below the text when they do not fit beside it.
- **Settings keeps its place when you switch an MCP server off or on** (spec 069 FR-069, issue 1624): the switches of the Copilot CLI list and the Claude Code list rebuilt the whole Settings page, which jumped back to the top and dropped the focus. A repaint of Settings now keeps the scroll position and the focused control, also while agent app accounts, quota and policy rows arrive after it; only your own scrolling, a key or a click moves the page. New check `check-069-toggle-scroll` reads twelve switches at both window sizes. Not verified on macOS or Windows.
- **Rename pencil no longer drawn over a plate's name or status while the office rearranges** (spec 097 FR-365, issue #1513): in the Real office, when desks ease to a new layout (an agent moving to the lounge), each moving plate's rename pencil now hides for the ease and fades back in at its spot, instead of being drawn displaced over the name and the status icon for a few hundred milliseconds (seen in the release clip and wherever every pencil shows: touch screens, Linux without a pointer). A pencil with the keyboard focus stays shown, and one that gets the focus while hidden shows at once and does not fade in a second time when the ease ends. Reduced motion is unchanged. Checked in the simulator from real screen captures, Photo office and arcade, 1440x900 and 1280x800, on macOS.
- **Retro arcade: a plate's status word and helper dots** (spec 102, issue #1558): at 1280x800 a plate with 3 helpers read "+3 dots Compacti..."; now the +N and the status word stay whole and the helpers' dots drop out whole, a waiting or failed helper's dot last (the plate's dots follow the order of the heads). On the narrowest plate the longest word can still be shortened.
- **The arcade's "Showing only … · Show all" notice can be read** (spec 102, issue #1559): in the Retro arcade the line that says a status pill is filtering the office was dark purple on a dark page (1.09:1), and the same line in the List and the List's "No agents match these filters." were dark on dark (1.00:1); they now read in the arcade's light inks (9:1 and better) and Show all stays underlined. Clean Office, Retro Pixel and the other worlds are unchanged.
- **No office scroll under a strip in the Retro arcade** (spec 102, issue #1566): at 1280x800 with 15 agents and the MCP problems strip shown, the arcade scrolled and its bottom row was cut off; the hall now takes the floor's own padding while it would otherwise scroll, and gives it back when the strip goes. Checked in the simulator by `shots-058-mcp-launch` (Photo and arcade, both sizes); macOS only.
- **Arcade keeps small folders whole at 1280x800** (spec 102, issue #1590): 10 agents in 3 or 4 folders no longer have a folder cut across two rows at 1280x800; the hall takes a lower lobby (106 px) to keep them together, and does not scroll. The arcade layout test now runs at the real room heights. With the MCP strip shown the split remains.
- **Letter skeleton for team-template names** (spec 111 T032, FR-158): `src/shared/skeleton.ts` maps letters that look Latin (spec 003's closed Cyrillic, Greek and Latin lookalike lists) to their Latin letter (NFKD, table, lower case, table again, lower case, NFC), with spec 109's digit folds on request, and `nameKey` builds on it (compatibility forms fold through the NFKD) and is idempotent; best effort, not a full confusables set. Pure code, nothing visible yet. Checked by `test:skeleton` (178 checks: table equals the lists, a golden check of every row, an idempotence grid of 36,000 names, a sweep of every code point up to U+2FFFF, thirteen mutants seen to fail).
- **Team templates have their store** (spec 111 T006, issue #1306): main keeps the templates in one file (`team-templates.json`, format `agent-durbar-team-templates`, at most 50), written by temp file, flush and rename (mode 0600 on POSIX; a folder the store creates gets 0700, an existing one keeps its mode), and a broken, newer, foreign-format or not-UTF-8 file is moved aside once as `team-templates.bad-<time>.json` with one notice (the newest 5 are kept). A read that fails once (at the load and before a save) is tried again before anything is moved or refused, and a file that still cannot be read at a save makes that save fail without touching it; a Windows rename held for a moment is retried. If another running instance saved meanwhile (the app takes no single-instance lock), a save (it re-reads the file and compares a SHA-256 of its bytes) is refused with `changed` and the store reloads, so a template saved by the other instance is not lost (except two saves in the very same instant: there is no lock). `isBlocked()` is true while a file it could not read at the load, or a bad file it could not move, is still in the way, and stays so until a save finds the file readable (nothing re-checks between saves). Such a save reads the file again first: a file that is readable and valid by then is reloaded and the save answers `changed`, never moved aside. A dangling symlink as the store file is moved aside like any bad file on Linux and macOS; on Windows (no `O_NOFOLLOW`) it reads as no file and the next save replaces it. Known gap: the folder is not flushed after the rename (same as schedules; see #1620). Leftover temp files of dead processes are removed. Nothing shows in the app yet. Tested with an in-memory file system (42 mutants killed) and with the real one in a temporary folder; not run on Windows or macOS (the 0600/0700 modes are POSIX only).
- **Team template validator and frame guard** (spec 111 T004, FR-002..FR-005, FR-010..FR-012): `src/shared/teamtemplate.ts` holds `checkTeamTemplate(value, level)`, `checkMember` and `frameCheck`: limits (own brief 4,000 characters and 80 lines, imported 2,000 and 40), unknown member keys refused, own-key lookups only, hidden characters refused in role and brief and in every imported string (ZWJ and ZWNJ allowed between letters or marks of Arabic and Indic scripts), briefs masked with `maskOutgoing`, names compared on `nameKey`, and a best-effort guard against text that forges the brief's frame (also on the joined `Role: <role>. <brief>`, marks and look-alike brackets folded). Pure code, nothing visible yet. Checked by `test:teamtemplate` with 63 mutants.

## v0.1.17 ([release](https://github.com/three-feathers/agent-durbar/releases/tag/v0.1.17), 2026-11-12)

- **A helper's button that has the keyboard focus is not retired** (spec 005 FR-242, issue #1483): a finished helper whose button held the focus (its head in the Real office with `+K` open, its tile in the Open office, its row in the list) could be retired by the next end and the focus fell to the page. It is now kept while its own button has the focus (as the block in the chat already was) and retired by the next end once the focus leaves. Checked by `check-helper-retention` (tile in the flat office, row in every world, head with `CHR_WORLD=real`; mutants without each part of the rule fail).
- **Fixed: UI checks that wait on the window focus no longer fail when another check's window is open** (spec 008 FR-210, issue #1498): with the suite's default of two drivers at once, `shots-097-screens` failed 5 of 5 runs on a Mac (`timed out waiting for the window active again`) and passed 5 of 5 one at a time. `scripts/ui-suite.js` now runs entries marked `focus: true` (`shots-097-screens`, both worlds, and `check-097-hover-card`) alone, after the others, when the windows share a desktop (macOS, Windows, Linux with a display); Linux under xvfb is unchanged. Checked by `test:uisuite`; Linux and Windows runs not done.
- **Windows: a `.cmd` program in a folder with spaces now starts** (spec 009 FR-029, issue #1525): VS Code's `code.cmd`, npm's `npm.cmd` and other `.cmd` programs went through `cmd.exe /d /s /c` without the outer pair of quotes that `/s` removes, so a path such as `C:\Program Files\nodejs\npm.cmd` broke at the first space. One shared helper now builds that line for all three routes and still refuses characters cmd.exe could interpret; an update check that is refused this way now says *This command cannot be run safely on this computer.* instead of `exit EUNSAFE`. Not run on Windows by the author; Windows CI runs the real case.
- **A Markdown-escaped token is hidden on the permission card** (spec 012 FR-180, issue #1490): a token written with an escape (`ghp\_…`) was shown in a permission request's summary, while the desk already hid it. The label mask now hides such a token in a command, a tool request, the activity log and the live readout, and shows the rest as written; when it cannot be sure, the card shows its neutral words.
- **An escaped token is hidden everywhere the shared masks run** (spec 012 FR-181, issue #1541): a token written with a backslash inside (`ghp\_…`, `g\hp_…`, `sk\-proj\-…`) was shown, whole or in part, in the "Approve for this session" label, the activity log and its export, the brain view's step targets, the MCP config view and copy preview, and the read tools' answers to the agent. These now hide it and keep the rest of the text as written. Text glued after a hidden environment value (`[redacted:NAME]_<rest>`) is now hidden like text glued after any other mask, also when that text is written with a backslash. The approval card hides a token cut by an escaped line break (`\n`) the same way. The reply line on a desk and the live readout hide the rest glued after an escaped token (`ghp\_…@…`) as the other surfaces do. A tool name longer than 256 characters is shown as "tool". Known, not fixed here: the desk's mask slows down on text with very many hidden-value markers (issue #1593).
- **The macOS title-bar check expects the Settings title where Settings puts it** (spec 052 FR-097, issue #1458): the check wanted the title on the centred column, but since the Settings section list the title lines up with the list-and-page block (x=194 at 1440 wide), on purpose. The rule and the check now say so, and the check also fails a title under the traffic lights. Test-only, no change to the app.
- **check-crew waits for the crew scenes before it reads them** (spec 077, issue #1460): the `dark fixture` entry of the Cheeky Crew driver failed on the macOS and Windows CI runners at "grown desks draw portraits (0)" and "crew scenes on screen (0)", and passes on a Mac and on Linux. The driver now waits at most 5 s after picking the crew for the crew scenes to be drawn, then runs the same checks unchanged. The cause on the runners is not proven and the fix is not yet seen on them; it needs a nightly. Test-only, no change to the app.
- **Same-file checks compare 64-bit file IDs exactly** (spec 083, issue #1491): the notes store, the world store, the machine `policy.json` read and the agent-file read on Windows now read inode and device numbers as bigints, so two different files whose NTFS file IDs differ only in the last bit (above 2^53) are no longer taken for one. Nothing stored changes. Not run on Windows or macOS; checked with stubbed IDs on Linux.
- **In the Retro arcade, the first draw no longer puts a request pill over a name plate** (spec 097 FR-358, issue #1434): for about half a second after the office appeared at 1280x800 the limit and inbox pills lay over plates. Checked with `check-097-slip-plates` on the simulator; not run on a Mac.
- **check-097-crews waits for the lobby's visits to settle before it reads the log** (spec 097, issue #1459): the Characters driver failed on CI at `sets/rebels/owl-runA.webp 0 net::ERR_FAILED` (Linux) and at an unloaded picture (macOS), while its own load skipped the wait for the lobby's visits. It now waits at most 5 s for them, checks the scene did not change before it reads, and prints request times when a file fails; `ERR_FAILED` is still a failure (a mutant that injects it into the log must fail; a real one is `read:` only). The cause on the runners is not proven and the fix is not yet seen on them. Test-only, no change to the app.
- **Pointer log for two more hover-card steps** (spec 097, FR-328): the simulator driver `check-097-hover-card` now records the pointer events of its asleep-card step and of its third-agent held-card step and prints them when one fails, like its first pointer step. Diagnostics only: no wait, limit or verdict changed. Checked by `test:pointer-log` (the wrapping in the driver's source) and a forced failure.
- **Desk-feature checks also run in the Retro arcade** (spec 102, issue #1399): test drivers only; five desk-feature checks (hover card, desk marks, one face, chat look, chat contrast) now also run in the Retro arcade in the UI suite. Nothing changes for users.
- Spec note only: the arcade layout check with the chat open (`check-office-overlap` with `OFFICE_WORLD=retro`, already in the `arcade` job of `check-parallel`) is recorded as done in spec 102 (T261); T225 is pointed to PR #1528 and the acceptance wording of #1399 is written as an owner question. No code change.
- **Pictures of the Retro arcade at 12 and 15 agents, in rooms and with one room each, at both window sizes** (spec 102 T281, issue #1428): looked at on macOS in the simulator; the layout check's picture option no longer leaves a tooltip over the cabinets. At 12 and 15 agents at 1280x800 the plate keeps its helper count and dots, as the spec says.
- **shots-102-arcade reads a person only once it has a box** (spec 102, issue #1517): the arcade driver failed once on Linux CI (run 42966366604) with `IndexSizeError ... getImageData ... source width is 0` in the "every person whole beside the cabinet" check, a person whose box rounded to 0 px. The check now reads such a person again for the same bounded 5 s an unloaded picture gets, and if it stays so fails naming the person, its size and its style. The cause of that one red is not established and the fix is not yet seen on Linux CI. Test-only, no change to the app.

## v0.1.16 ([release](https://github.com/three-feathers/agent-durbar/releases/tag/v0.1.16), 2026-11-11)

**Known issues**

- **Project notes file** (issue #1464): a local process that swaps a folder link at the right instant can still make Durbar move another folder's notes.md into your project under a random name (the message names it), a delete with no inode record (file written by an older version, or on a drive without inode numbers) can still remove it, and a write can still overwrite it. This is recorded in docs/safety.md.
- **Announcements** (issue #1320): toasts, the chat's label and the notice strips still show an agent or folder name as written.

**Memory and performance**

- **A long chat no longer grows without limit in memory** (spec 005 FR-238, FR-239, issue #1380): every chat kept every message and tool call while the app stayed open, so the window grew by about 35 MB an hour in the simulator. A chat now keeps its newest 300 messages, the newest 300 steps of a turn or helper still running, and at most 2,000 steps in all. Your last question and the last reply are always kept, as are waiting questions and permission cards and the live parts of a turn. One line at the top says how many earlier messages and steps are not kept in this view (the agent app's session keeps its own record). While you are scrolled up in an open chat nothing is removed, and what you focus or select, or a note saved from a message, stays. Checked with `test:chattrim` and the `check-chat-memory` simulator driver (Open office, Real office and Retro arcade); Linux only.
- **Finished helpers no longer grow without limit in memory** (spec 005 FR-240, FR-241, issue #1418): an agent now keeps its newest 50 finished and 20 failed helpers in full. Older ones leave the office and the helper lists, and their block in the chat becomes one line with the helper's name, result and status. A helper that is still running, the one whose activity you have open, and one you are focused on or selecting text in are never removed. Checked with `test:subretire` and the `check-helper-retention` simulator driver (three worlds, 1440x900 and 1280x800); Linux only.

**Agent follow and chat**

- **Auto-follow no longer brings back an old "finished"** (issue #1444, the known issue of v0.1.15): if an agent finished while its chat was on screen and you then sent it another message, the window could go to the office and then reopen the chat with `<name> finished`, although the agent was already working on your message. A reply you saw in its chat now counts as seen, and a new message replaces a completion still waiting. Checked in the simulator on Linux.
- **A chat keeps its place across the Browser tab** (spec 005 FR-235, issue #1168): a chat that was following the end is at the end again, and still following, when you come back from Browser to Chat, also when the agent wrote meanwhile. A chat you had scrolled up keeps its position. The chat now keeps its width while collapsed, also where scrollbars take room (Windows, Linux). Checked on macOS only, with a simulated classic scrollbar, at 1440x900 and 1280x800; not on Windows or Linux.
- **On a Mac, the top of the rail's resize handle resizes the rail** (spec 005 FR-236, issue #1457): its top 40 pixels used to move the window instead. Checked in the simulator; not on a real Mac.

**Safety and privacy**

- **A Claude session's instructions and settings no longer show in the process list** (spec 012 FR-179, issue #1379): the folder's CLAUDE.md text, a chosen agent profile and the folder's accepted hooks and rules used to go to Claude Code on its command line, which other accounts on the same computer can read. They now go through private files that only your account can read and that are removed when the session's Claude process ends. A Claude Code too old to read them (before 2.1.69 for CLAUDE.md, before 2.1.281 for agent profiles) gets a message asking you to update it. Checked with unit tests on a fake Claude process (macOS); not with a real Claude session or on Windows.
- **Screen-reader announcements no longer read out a token or a home folder** (spec 012 FR-178, issue #1419): lines such as `<name> finished. New reply.` used the name as written. They now go through the same mask the desk already uses. A command or question in a request is said exactly as the card shows it, and "needs your OK" is said even when the request is hidden. When masking is not possible, a fixed line says a line was not read out. Toasts, the chat's label and the notice strips still show the name as written (#1320). Checked with `test:announcemask` and one simulator run; no screen reader run.
- **Deleting the project notes file deletes only the file Durbar wrote** (spec 083 FR-055, issue #1463): a folder link swapped within microseconds by a local process (for example a sandboxed agent) could make *Delete the file* remove a notes.md in another folder, or make the way back replace one. Durbar now records the inode of the file it writes and deletes only that one (a notes.md with the same text but another inode, after a `git checkout`, is left as changed). The way back is a hard link, or on drives without hard links a copy into a new notes.md that never replaces a file. Part of the problem is still open (see Known issues, #1464). Checked by unit tests with injected file calls and a real swapper process; Linux only.

**Copilot on a Mac**

- **Playwright MCP can start again under Copilot on macOS** (spec 087 FR-024, issue #1173): Copilot's private temp folder is now a short one under /tmp when the system temp folder is too long for a Unix socket (macOS /var/folders). The folder is still private to your account and removed afterwards; Windows is unchanged. Checked on Linux; not on a real Mac.

**Behind the scenes** (nothing changes in the app): the team merge gate reads CodeQL results and a PR's changed files more strictly (FR-090, FR-091, #1422, #1479), the UI check runner now ends a timed-out check's display and window whole (FR-199, #1417), the live-activity UI check re-reads the lobby until it settles (FR-191, #1481), and the README build-cost sentence says how many machines started on each day (#1387). Checked by script tests only; not against live GitHub, not on macOS or Windows.

## v0.1.15 ([release](https://github.com/three-feathers/agent-durbar/releases/tag/v0.1.15), 2026-11-10)

**Agent follow and chat**

- **Auto-follow tells you before it moves you** (spec 052, PR #1414): with Auto-follow on, a full-screen notice now says where it is taking you and why ("Priya is working on this", "Priya finished. See if it is OK.") and counts down 3 seconds before the window goes to the office or opens a chat. **Stay here** / **Stay in the office** or Esc cancels the move. Screen readers hear it once; with reduced motion nothing in it moves. Checked in the simulator in Open office, the Photo office, the cave and the arcade at 1440x900 and 1280x800. On macOS only its check script was run after it landed; that run found the script needed a Mac fix (PR #1443, merged). Known issue (#1444): a "finished" that arrived while its chat was open can reopen the chat after the notice; the fix, PR #1445, is for v0.1.16.
- **Accessibility** (spec 005 FR-237, issue #1324): a screen reader no longer hears "Working…" instead of "<name> needs your OK" or a question when both are announced in the same instant: a request outranks a status.

**Safety**

- **Lookalike letters on approval cards** (spec 003 FR-113..FR-119, issue #1300): a host, program or MCP tool name that mixes scripts (a Cyrillic letter in a Latin word), uses a styled or fullwidth form or mixes digit systems keeps every letter and gets a visible marker after the odd one, and the card adds one plain line saying so, with an address's host as the network reads it (`xn--…`). *Allow for this session* now names exactly what it grants: the whole command, the punycode host, the server and tool. Grant chips and the *Why was this allowed?* sentence say *Has lookalike letters*. A warning only: every button stays. Checked in the simulator at 1440x900 and 1280x800; not on a real session.
- **`CLAUDE.md` imports checked by their text first** (spec 012 FR-177): the Claude adapter checks each `@path` import of the folder's `CLAUDE.md` against the folder by its text before any file is read, checks links inside the folder before they are followed, then checks again once resolved; a path that cannot be resolved is skipped. Checked by tests with Windows and POSIX path cases; not run on a real Windows machine.
- **A tool name that looks like a secret is not kept in the usage file** (spec 020 FR-033, issue #1291): it is counted under `[hidden tool]` (a server name under `[hidden server]`), and an existing file is cleaned when the app starts, with its counts kept. Checked with unit tests, no real session.
- **Profile confirmation matches what you saw** (spec 043 FR-044): a profile from a folder's repository is trusted only for the exact file text shown in its confirmation; if the file changes before you confirm, the dialog is shown again with a note, and Claude Code sessions start with exactly the text that was confirmed. Linked agent files are not used. Checked by tests; not in the simulator window or on a real session.

**Real office**

- **Every crew in Real office** (spec 097 FR-335, FR-336, issue #1154): the Characters choice Cheeky, Pirate, Monster, Royal and Rebels now draws that crew's own people in every Real office world, and Settings says "Real office draws the <Crew> people.". Minimalist and My characters still show the world's own people.
- **Rearrange desks and rooms** (spec 097 FR-345..FR-348, issue #1152): drag a desk by its name plate or a room by its folder sign (Esc cancels, a tiny press is still a click); Alt+arrows on a name plate (Alt+Shift+arrows for its room) and the folder menu's Move earlier / Move later work too, on the same saved order as the Open office.
- **Helper heads stay on one row** (spec 097 FR-338, bug #1384): when the heads do not fit, the last place is **+K**; no head wraps onto a second line any more (the known issue of v0.1.14).
- **A narrow room's sign keeps its + and menu** (spec 097 FR-356, issue #1392): in the cave at 1280x800, a one-desk room cut off its sign's + and ⋯ buttons; it now shows the short sign, with the path and cost on the name's tooltip.
- **The rename pencil on an arcade cabinet** (spec 097 FR-357, bug #1427): it showed at the floor's top-left corner and did nothing; it now sits at the plate's right end and the name field opens over the cabinet's marquee.
- **Retro arcade follows the other worlds' rules** (spec 102 FR-307..FR-310, issue #1428): big screens with 4 or fewer agents, and its blinking and typing motion starts 2 seconds after the room is drawn. The arcade is not offered yet, so nothing changes for anyone today.
- Minimalist crew art (spec 097, T245): six new drawn people are in the repository, owner-approved; not selectable yet.

**Looks and contrast**

- **Theme contrast checks every colour form** (spec 070 FR-080, issue #1259): colours written as `rgb()`, `hsl()`, a name or with transparency are now measured and repaired like hex ones; one that cannot be measured is reported as unmeasured. Checked by unit tests and the dark-fixture UI check.
- **Readable chips and badges in the Retro arcade** (spec 070 FR-081, issue #1278): attachment chips, ask tags, selected rows and tone-coloured badges were 1.2 to 2.7:1 on dark fills; they are now at least 5.9:1. Badges coloured by the status pair are not part of this fix.

**Build, CI and team tools** (nothing changes in the app)

- A GitHub outage of the Electron download no longer fails every CI job: a workflow keeps the download cache filled for all three systems and builds check the Electron zip offline against the lockfile (spec 008 FR-195).
- Pull requests that change no UI code no longer start the three-system UI checks; `main`, the nightly and the merge queue still run them (spec 008 FR-191).
- Parity checklists (#1147, #1404) tick themselves when a listed issue closes as completed (spec 021 FR-088).
- The merge gate accepts a CodeQL check that GitHub skips on a PR with no code (spec 021 FR-089, issue #1420).
- Test fixes: the colour parser growth check on Windows (spec 070, T049) and the Real office hover-card check's window focus (spec 097, issue #1408).
- Docs: the feature inventory matches the code (spec 025 FR-021, #1398); spec 080 records a macOS dialog behaviour with numbers for later agent browser control (issue #1415).

- **Known issue: auto-follow notice** (issue #1444): if the agent whose chat you are in finishes while you are typing, then after the notice moves you to the office a "finished" notice can reopen that chat after 3 seconds (it already happened as a quick flicker in v0.1.14); Escape cancels it; fix is in review for v0.1.16.

## v0.1.14 ([release](https://github.com/three-feathers/agent-durbar/releases/tag/v0.1.14), 2026-11-09)

- **Context on the monitor frame** (issue #1148): in Real office a thin line round each desk's monitor frame fills clockwise from the top-left as the agent's context fills. It is soft white up to 90 % and red above 90 % (the same steps as the chat header's ring); it draws nothing while the context is unknown, never tints the screen, and does not move. A screen reader hears "context N %" with the desk's name. In the Photo office, the cave and the arcade; 15 agents still fit at 1440x900 and 1280x800.
- **Helpers at the desk** (issue #1139): each desk's sub-agents show as small round heads with a status dot under the name plate in the Photo office, the cave and the Retro arcade (at 1440x900); click one to open its activity view, **+K** shows the rest, and the name is in the tooltip. Where there is no room the plate keeps its "+N". 15 agents still fit both window sizes. Known issue (issue #1384): a desk with six or more helpers can wrap the sixth head onto a second line instead of showing **+K**.
- **The arcade's drag-a-file overlay is readable** (issue #1153): it had light text on a light fill; it now uses the page surface, and a new check keeps each world's own colours whatever theme is picked.
- **The arcade's name plates show their whole line** (issue #1303): a lounge plate's text was cut at 12 and 15 agents; the plates now have room for the full name.
- **More secrets masked** (issue #1227): short passwords (4 to 7 characters with a letter and a digit) after a secret name, several-word passwords, `Bearer` values after a short first word, `mysql -pPASSWORD` (also across continued lines), AWS secret key names written with spaces, `Cookie` values without `=`, and unfinished key starts are hidden; ids, versions and algorithm names (`HS256`, `tls13`, `v1.2.3`) stay readable. Checked by tests only; no real service was called.
- **Network home folders hidden in helper names**: a home folder on a network share (`\\server\share\name`, also with extra backslashes, or `//server/homes/name`) no longer shows its user name in a helper's name, task or tooltip, in Real office and the flat office.
- Coming next, not visible yet: the code for a fourth agent mode, Autopilot, is in place behind a switch that stays off, so the mode menu has no new item and nothing changes for anyone. One stricter check applies today: remote control checks its mode again after its own dialog. Tested on fakes only, not in a real session.
- For contributors: a pull request builds the unpacked Linux app once, inside the required *Office layout check* job, and two slow UI checks wait for the page to be steady instead of guessing (no check was dropped or loosened).

## v0.1.13 ([release](https://github.com/three-feathers/agent-durbar/releases/tag/v0.1.13), 2026-11-08)

- **Model, tokens, cost, context and the last tool in the zoom** (issue #1148): hovering a monitor (or tabbing to its desk) shows, under the agent's name, the same facts the Open office desk shows: Model, Now or Last tool, Usage, Cost, Context and time on the task. Unknown facts are left out, and every line is masked like a screen line.
- **The hover card opens over the whole desk** (issue #1151): hover a person, desk or name plate, not only the monitor. An asleep agent has its card too, with its last reply; a resting agent's card shows the session's changes ("6 files changed, +120 −35, 1 commit, 40 min · counted by Agent Durbar"), as in Open office.
- **A desk's unsent draft** (issue #1149): a small Draft tag sits on the name plate while a chat has text you have not sent, and the plate's name says "unsent draft", as in Open office.
- **The Characters crews, said honestly** (issue #1154): Real office draws the Office people for every Characters crew that has no Real office pictures yet, and the line under Office layout says why. Prehistoric now draws its own people in the Photo office.
- **A section list in Settings**: a list on the left jumps within the page, marks the section you're in as you scroll, works with the keyboard and in every world, and becomes a row in a narrow window.
- **Masking fixes** (issues #1237, #1298, #1295, #1296): two tokens of different kinds written back to back are hidden whole; a secret name split by quotes (`'pass'"word"=...`) is hidden; the activity log hides a command, tool name or token split by an escape the way the chat does; the chip of an "approve this exact command" scope is masked before it is shortened.
- **A permission request never shows a secret-looking tool name or command** (issue #1290): the *Needs your OK* card, its buttons and tooltips, the grant chips and list, and the "why was this allowed" text show a name like a token as `tool`, and a command keeps its shape with the secret hidden. A card offering "Allow for this session" says when a part is hidden and that the grant covers it. Escape codes are shown as visible markers, and a request Durbar cannot check has no Allow button. What you approve is unchanged.
- **A tool named like a secret is hidden** (issue #1284) on the chat, desk, helper card and Settings screens; the chip keeps its icon and label.
- **A helper's task no longer shows a token or a home folder** in the Sub-agent activity panel (issue #1330), its title or the office helper tile (also network `\\server\share\user` paths).
- Conversation search no longer drops a long message because it holds an emoji.
- The Retro arcade's plan chip is readable (contrast 2.99:1 to 13.14:1) and follows the world's colours.
- **README for first-time visitors** (issue #1256): the Real office leads the page, with a one-line problem statement, a download line, "Works with / Not yet" and a shorter safety lead. The release clip films the Real office.
- **Build cost**: the README shows an estimate of what building Durbar with AI agents has cost so far (API list prices, not billing), with a daily chart. Method and privacy: `docs/build-cost.md`.
- Coming next, not visible yet: the Needs you panel ("Your door") is built behind a switch that is off; five new Real office crews (Pirate, Royal, Rebels, Monster, Cheeky) are in, approved, and wait to be wired up; the first piece of the AI character maker is in the code.

## v0.1.12 ([release](https://github.com/three-feathers/agent-durbar/releases/tag/v0.1.12), 2026-11-07)

- **Drag the agent list's width** (spec 005 FR-236, issue #1223): the right edge of the rail beside a chat is a splitter. Drag it, or focus it and use the arrow keys (Shift or PageUp/PageDown for bigger steps, Home and End for the smallest and largest); double-click goes back to 220 px. It stays between 180 px and 40% of the window, is remembered on this computer and is never part of a session. Checked in the simulator at 1440x900 and 1280x800.
- The release clip's step captions are numbered 1, 2, 3... in the order they are shown (spec 008; the v0.1.11 clip read "3." then "2."). Checked by `test:releasevideo` with a mutant; the clip was not re-filmed.
- **Agent rules split** (spec 021 FR-081, FR-082): `AGENTS.md` is now a shorter core every agent loads, and task-only rules (incident triage, merge mechanics, review rounds and pace, chat and decisions, keeping docs in sync) moved word for word to `docs/rules/`, each linked from `AGENTS.md`; a new priority order (bugs, existing-world parity, the Retro world, new features, other worlds) is in the core. Checked with `npm run rules:check` (per-file budgets, every task file linked) and a line-by-line verbatim check.
- MCP: the chat's *Sign in...* button now starts the sign-in (it used to only open the MCP list and showed nothing when the row had no button), also in an idle chat, and the MCP row shows Sign in for a server whose automatic renewal gave up even while Copilot still says it is connected (spec 058 FR-229, issue #1229). Checked with fake-adapter tests; not on a real server yet.
- **Notes: Open source** (spec 083 FR-046, [issue 699](https://github.com/three-feathers/agent-durbar/issues/699)): a note's `⋯` has **Open source**. It opens the chat the note came from (a past session through Previous sessions, sending nothing), scrolls to the message inside the chat and outlines it for a moment; if the session is gone or the message is not found, a toast says so and the note stays.
- **Real office: finished agents sit in a lounge row** (spec 097 FR-246..FR-249, issue #1220): an agent that is done and whose reply you have read leaves its full desk for a compact row under the desks, seated on their chair beside a small desk and monitor with their name plate; asleep agents too, lamp and screen off. An unread reply keeps the full desk until read. The freed room goes to the working and waiting desks; in the Retro arcade the seats are stools beside a small console. Moving back to work eases (none with reduced motion). Who moves is the flat office's lounge rule. Simulator-checked at 1440x900 and 1280x800.

## v0.1.11 ([release](https://github.com/three-feathers/agent-durbar/releases/tag/v0.1.11), 2026-11-06)

- **Open office: the monitor screen shows who needs you, as in Real office** (spec 001 FR-230, FR-231, owner decision): a desk's screen turns amber when its agent needs you, orange at the spending limit or on a coffee break, red on an error, and green when a finished agent's reply is not read yet. The glow around the desk card and the waiting halo behind the screen are gone; the status word and the New badge stay. All three desk styles (office, pixel, crew) do it.
- **The nightly macOS UI checks press the Mac's own keys** (spec 008, issue #839): the answer-key and paste checks use Command on a Mac, as the app expects, and the Real office glass check no longer takes the CI machine's Reduce transparency setting for a bug. One check (characters) now explains itself when it times out. No change to the app.
- **Simulator About version from package.json** (spec 008 FR-179, #1162): the simulator's About label is the app version plus ` (simulator)`, generated at build time, so a release bump edits only `package.json` and `package-lock.json`. Checked by `test:simversion` (with a hard-coded-version mutant).
- **A `Cookie:` or `Set-Cookie:` header with one opaque token is now hidden** (spec 012 FR-168, issue #1159): `redact()` hid `Cookie: name=value` pairs but showed `Cookie: <long token>` whole. A single token of 8 or more characters is hidden, also after pairs, with `Set-Cookie` attributes kept; prose like `cookie: my session bug` stays. Checked on Linux by `npm run test:runtime-redact`; not run on Windows or macOS.
- **The merge gate no longer reads "condition holds" as a hold** (spec 021 FR-083, issue #1190): `scripts/pr-gate.sh` matches `on hold`, `owner hold`, `do not merge`, `don't merge`, `hold lifted` and `off hold` only as whole words, so text like "the condition holds" or "threshold lifted" no longer holds or lifts a PR; real phrases still do. Checked by `npm run test:trustedcontent` (fake `gh`) on Linux; not run on Windows or macOS.
- **Windows UI checks steadier** (spec 052, issue #1179): `check-auto-follow` and `check-edit-popups` no longer fail on the first try when a neighbour driver's window takes the focus on the shared Windows desktop. Test driver only, no app change; checked on repeated Windows CI runs.
- **Copilot MCP sign-ins renew themselves at once after an expiry** (spec 058, issue #1201): when a call to a remote MCP server fails with a sign-in error, or Copilot reports the server needs a sign-in after it was connected, Agent Durbar reconnects that server immediately in the background (it waits only for another call to the same server in that chat), with *Renewed 12:40* on the server's row. If it worked, the agent gets one short automatic message to retry the step that failed, naming the server; that is one extra message and uses AI credits, and it is never sent at a spending limit, after Stop, or with *Refresh MCP sign-ins automatically* off. Your own message is never resent, and there is no timer. Probed on a local test server only; not checked on a real Entra server, a real model, macOS or Windows.
- **Copilot MCP sign-ins are checked in the background before every message** (spec 058, issue #1205): beside each message you send, and when a chat starts, Agent Durbar asks every remote sign-in server that reads *connected* for its tool list (a real round-trip, no AI, no credits; your message is never held; a server with a successful call or check in the last 5 minutes is skipped). A server that answers *unauthorized* is reconnected at once; only a clear sign-in signal counts (an HTTP 401, Copilot's own "OAuth challenge required", `invalid_grant`, an Entra sign-in code), so other errors restart nothing and a server that answers its tool list is not restarted by the check (a reconnect before a message after a quiet hour is a separate rule); a server is checked at most once every 5 minutes; one that does not answer in 30 seconds is marked *Did not answer a check* on its row (that request is not cancelled in the runtime, and no second one is sent while it is outstanding). Nothing shows in the chat while all is well, only *Reconnecting <name>…* during a reconnect, and the agent gets no extra message from it. New *Settings > Diagnostics* (off by default): the activity-log row *renewed (automatic)* is now written only with it on. Probed on a local test server only; not checked on a real Entra server, macOS or Windows.
- **The MCP sign-in renewal check no longer fails after the day it was written** (spec 058, PR #1211's CI): `shots-058-mcp-signin-refresh` reads the *Renewed 12:40* row against the run's own day, with the page clock held still, instead of a fixed date. Test driver only, no app change.
- **Real office chat text is now checked against what is really behind it** (spec 097, issue #1146): a new check measures every line of chat text, link, code chip and header text against the screen pixels under it, in the main and a helper chat at both window sizes, and fails under 4.5:1 or when header text runs past the window edge. The unreadable helper chat itself was fixed by #1164.
- **Real office: the monitor screen shows who needs you** (spec 097 FR-236, FR-238, owner decision): a desk's screen turns amber when its agent needs you (also on its empty desk while it waits at the Boss), red on an error, orange at the spending limit, and green when a finished agent's reply is not read yet, with a **New reply** tag above the name plate. The text on the screen stays readable on every colour. The corner call lights and the red and amber glows are gone. The arcade's cabinets do the same.
- **One face per agent in Real office** (spec 097, issue #1193): the left rail row showed a flat figure while the chat header showed the agent's Real office portrait; the rail, the header and the Needs you faces now use one picture. Checked in the simulator at 1440x900 and 1280x800 (`check-097-one-face`: Real office and Flat with two crews).
- **Real office: rename a session from its name plate** (spec 059, issue #1155): the same pencil as on an Open office desk appears beside the name when you point at or tab to a plate, and **F2** on a focused plate now edits the name in place too (it did nothing in Real office before). Enter saves, Esc cancels.

## v0.1.10 ([release](https://github.com/three-feathers/agent-durbar/releases/tag/v0.1.10), 2026-11-05)

- **No empty More menu in a helper's chat** (spec 005 FR-234, issue #1145): a sub-agent's chat header no longer shows a More button that opens an empty popover; a main agent's chat keeps More. Checked by the simulator driver `check-helper-more` at both sizes on Linux (not run on macOS or Windows).
- **The release clip zooms on what changed** (spec 008, owner ask): each scene of `npm run release:video` eases in to the part of the app that changed, holds, and eases back, with a caption for every step, so a viewer can follow it; `-zoom` stills let you check the frames. The privacy check now refuses any written-out home folder (`/home/<name>`, `/Users/<name>`, `C:\Users\<name>`), and the simulator's sample paths are neutral (`~/projects/demo/...`). Team tooling and simulator data, 0 AI credits. Checked by `test:releasevideo`; not run on macOS or Windows.
- **Account limits per agent app** (spec 011 FR-080..FR-094, issue #690): Settings > Agent apps shows what each provider itself reports about the account, separate from per-session usage: figures with their units, the source, when Durbar last asked and the reset time. Absent data says "not reported", never zero; a stale reading or a failed read is never shown as current. Read-only and free: Copilot `account.getQuota` (an account GitHub bills by usage reads "billed by usage, see GitHub", not a request count), Codex `account/rateLimits/read`; Claude Code has no free call and says "not reported". No polling. Near a reported limit (10 % left, awaiting owner review) it warns and names other ready apps; only your own click opens **Continue in another app…**. Both reads were checked on real Copilot and Codex accounts on Linux (2026-11-02): free, no session or turn started; Windows and macOS not checked.
- **A very large file written by an agent no longer stalls Durbar** (spec 012 FR-167, issue #1052): while a big `Write` streams in, the chat masks only the part it can show, instead of the whole file at every update. An 800,000-space write took about 9 s of CPU on Linux; on an Apple M4 it went from 2.2 s to 0.4 s. Checked with `test:edits`.
- **Helper chat keeps its "Back to parent chat" bar in view** (spec 015 FR-020, issue #1142): the "Sub-agent activity" bar stays at the top of a helper's chat while you scroll, on an opaque background, so the parent chat is always one click away. Checked by the simulator driver `check-helper-bar` at both sizes on Linux (not run on macOS or Windows).
- **Agent rules shortened** (spec 021 FR-063): two sentences in `AGENTS.md` say the same thing in fewer words (3,000 to 2,997); no rule changed. Checked with `npm run rules:check` and a before/after token diff.
- **The merge gate refuses held PRs** (spec 021 FR-079): `scripts/pr-gate.sh` now says "not ready" for a PR labelled `status:blocked` or `needs-owner-approval`, or one an owner has put on hold in a comment until an owner lifts it (only the date and link are printed). Checked with a fake `gh` and five planted mutants.
- **The merge gate ignores quoted hold words** (spec 021 FR-080, issue #1178): `scripts/pr-gate.sh` no longer treats `on hold` or `do not merge` inside code, a fenced block or a `>` quote in an owner comment as a hold (or a quoted `hold lifted` as a lift); a hold written as prose still refuses the PR. Checked by `npm run test:trustedcontent` (fake `gh`) on Linux; not run on Windows or macOS.
- **Every world gets the features of Open office** (spec 021 FR-078, owner decision 2026-11-04): a rule and a check, no change in the app. `docs/feature-inventory.md` now names an issue for each Real office gap (#1147 lists them: helpers, desk figures, marks, hover card, drag to rearrange, themes, characters, rename pencil, chat look), corrects cells that were stale, and the inventory check fails a world offered with a missing Open office feature. An owner decision waives only the cell it is written in and must read `(owner decision, YYYY-MM-DD)` or `(owner decision, #NNN)`; a Real office `works` note with a limiting word (only, no, not, ...) counts as a gap, which gave the trouble signs #1171 and four chat-level rows #1172. Checked by `npm run test:featureinventory`, simulator-free.
- Test driver only: the helper-bar and helper-more UI checks look for the renamed read-only chat class (`helper-ro`), so `main`'s UI checks pass again. No change for users.
- **Needs you inbox: no giant avatars** (spec 052, issue #1174): with Real office chosen (for example in the List view), the inbox could open with the waiting agents' faces several hundred px wide, hiding the requests and their Allow and Deny buttons. The faces are 30 px again. Checked by the simulator driver `check-inbox-avatar` at both sizes on Linux (not run on macOS or Windows, nor in the packaged app).
- **Serious mode only hides the mood drawings; Motion is its own switch** (spec 052 FR-069, issue #1182): Serious mode no longer stops walking, typing and lights, so the office does not look broken with it on. A new Settings switch, *Motion* (on by default), and the system's reduced motion setting stop the movement. Settings of an older version keep Serious mode as it was and have motion on. Checked in the simulator at 1440x900 and 1280x800 (`check-serious-motion`); not run on Windows or macOS.
- **The rename pencil follows its name when a card's live line changes** (spec 059, issue #1135): a new line of live activity could move the names in a room while their hover pencils stayed put, over the task line. Checked with `shots-001-live-activity`, now part of `check:ui`. The check runs once per animation frame and reads where the names are only when a live line changed height (`check-office-ops`: one layout read per live paint).
- **A click on the middle of a short name opens the chat, not rename** (spec 059 FR-030): the rename pencil is invisible until hover, and on a short name with no room beside it, its box could start left of the name's middle and take the click. The whole box now stays in the name's right half. Checked in the simulator at both window sizes.
- **End-of-session summary on the lounge, in the briefing, with the agent app's own numbers** (spec 085, issue #604): a finished agent resting in the lounge shows its files and lines in its bubble (click it: the changed-files list) and in the peek card's text; by keyboard the desk label names the numbers and the folder chip lists the files; **Since you were away** adds one line for the sessions you ended yesterday and today (numbers only, 7 days kept on this computer); and the card adds a second sentence with the totals GitHub Copilot reports (files, lines, AI credits, requests), named as reported and never merged with Durbar's own count. Claude Code's status line and Codex's `notify` are not used yet. Simulator, unit tests and the driver at 1440x900 and 1280x800; `getMetrics` was checked on a real GitHub Copilot session (it is free of model cost; a non-zero `codeChanges` was not seen), and the feature itself was not run end to end on a real session.
- **A helper's chat in Real office no longer shows the floor picture behind its text** (spec 097, #1140): every chat, main or helper, new or resumed, has the same world look and follows the Office layout setting, also when it changes with a chat open. Checked with the simulator driver `check-097-chat-look` at both sizes.
- **No zoom under a still pointer** (spec 097, #1040): pressing Esc back to the office with the pointer resting on a screen no longer opens that screen's zoom; the next real move does, and keyboard focus zoom is unchanged. Checked in the simulator at both sizes, with a mutant; not seen or verified on macOS.
- **Status counts in the top bar keep their width** (spec 097, Changes 2026-11-04): on a system font whose digits differ in width (seen on Windows), a count change made its pill a fraction of a pixel wider or narrower, which shifted the top bar and made it measure itself again. Each count now has a fixed width per digit. The simulator driver `check-office-ops-real` also sets motion on itself, so a Windows runner's "reduce motion" setting no longer makes its moving-people row find nothing, and it fails when any top-bar part changes size on a status update.
- **Real office is lighter** (spec 097 FR-180..FR-184, T149..T153, simulator only): a status change no longer re-measures the lobby's name chips or rebuilds name plates and folder signs (simulator op counts: layout reads per update 65 to 3 at 15 agents and 67 to 3 at 40, measured before the slips joined the room; with the slips 15, as they are drawn again on every update, T184).
- **Real office's lamps no longer blend** (spec 097 FR-180, T150): each lamp is a gradient tinted in advance, and an off lamp is a dark gradient. The look moves very slightly: a lit monitor is a little less amber. Light is the owner's call: the before and after pictures are in the pull request.
- **Real office's lights move in steps** (spec 097 FR-180, FR-181, T151): pulse and breath change about 3 times a second, and glows and call-light halos are static gradients instead of box-shadows. A screen's glow now starts right at its bezel.
- **Real office's dialogs have no blur** (spec 097 FR-182, T152): a dialog is the opaque world surface, its text checked at 4.5:1 or more on the computed colours; small menus and toasts keep the glass, and `test:theme-tokens` holds the allow list for the whole app.
- **Real office decodes smaller pictures** (spec 097 FR-183, T153): people and the desk come in a half-size set chosen by `srcset`, made by downscaling the same art (no new drawing), a moving person's second frame included: pictures decoded at 15 agents 17.4 to 5.7 MB at device pixel ratio 1; the pack is 8.23 of its 10 MB. At ratio 2 the full set is chosen, as before.
- **Real office buttons**: the chat header's buttons no longer show Retro Pixel's hard offset shadow in the Photo office or the cave (spec 097 FR-245, issue #1144); a world sets its own plain chrome style and leaving it gives the theme back. Checked by `test:theme-tokens` and `shots-097-chat` (simulator, Linux; not run on macOS or Windows).
- **Autopilot in the adapters** (spec 099 phase 2): Copilot, Claude Code and Codex can run a goal, count its continuations, answer questions with one fixed sentence, stop in each app's own order, clear the goal after every end and fail closed when that is not confirmed. Tested on fakes only, marked not verified. Not reachable yet: nothing in the window or in main can turn it on, and nothing changes for a session that is not on Autopilot.
- **A new logo family: the human is the sovereign** (spec 101): the app icon on Windows, macOS and Linux, the README and the website favicon are now a command prompt wearing a crown with three bots below. The top bar shows the same icon in your theme's colours, and Settings > Logo lets you choose one of four icons (Prompt sovereign, Terminal chair, Figure with badge, Underscore floor); the choice is kept on this computer. In a Real office world the top-bar icon takes that world's colours (photo, cave, loft). The Dock, taskbar and Finder icon is fixed at build time and does not follow the choice. Checked with `test:logo-assets` and the simulator driver `check-logo-family` at 1440x900 and 1280x800; not looked at on a real macOS or Windows desktop. Not a trademark clearance: only a quick web search was done.

## v0.1.9 ([release](https://github.com/three-feathers/agent-durbar/releases/tag/v0.1.9), 2026-11-04)

- **Still highlights (no motion)** (spec 001 FR-192, issue #780, WCAG 2.2.2): Settings > Highlights has a switch that keeps the coloured glow and the New badge on desks that need you or have a new reply, but stops the movement that goes with them: the person's jumping and pulsing, the glow's breathing, the badge's pulse and the waiting badge's pings, the waiting person's waving hand and the tap on the desk after a minute. In the Real office the whole room stands still. It does not stop every animation (the system's reduced motion setting does that; some small ones, such as a theme's own figures, still follow it). Off by default and saved on this computer. A failed save says so and the switch shows what is really saved. `durbar_settings` (MCP) now reports `stillHighlights` next to `seriousMode`. Checked in `check-unread` (simulator, both sizes), `test:stillhighlights` (main's handler on a stand-in `electron` with a real store) and `test:ipcchannels`; not run on a packaged app, Windows or macOS.
- **Long chats open faster, and keep following the end** (spec 005 FR-210..FR-215): when you leave a chat, its older messages are marked with their measured height, so reopening a chat of 4,000 simulated messages lays out only the ones near the view (the timing of each run is printed by the driver, never asserted; a long history opened for the first time is not faster, nor is a chat closed from its Browser tab). The marks are dropped when the width or a layout setting (density, theme, font) changed (FR-213), and for a message that changed while the chat was closed (FR-215). A chat scrolled up, even by a little, is no longer pulled back to the end by a streaming turn, and a late scroll event no longer stops the chat from following a turn (FR-214). Scrolling, copying across messages, find and keyboard focus are unchanged. Simulator only; not run on Windows or macOS.
- Test driver only (issue #1102): the long-chat visibility check no longer fails on a loaded machine or on a 2x screen. No change for users.
- Test driver only (issue #1127): the long-chat visibility check runs one window size per CI slot, so it no longer hits the suite time limit. No change for users.
- **The agent rail** (spec 005 FR-190, FR-194, FR-216; spec 052 FR-215, FR-216): with a chat open, a rail on the left replaces the strip at the top. It lists every agent by folder, with the folder's count; whoever needs you comes first, in a taller row that says how long they have waited and what they ask, and their folder moves up. **Your door** at its top shows the Needs you count and opens the queue. A folder collapses and expands with a click, Enter, Space or a double click, still showing its count and how many in it need you; opening a chat in it expands it, and the collapsed folders are remembered per window. On a Mac the rail's top row and the chat header move the window. Checked in the simulator by `check-rail` at 1440x900 and 1280x800 (all 15 agents, no page overflow, two row heights, contrast) and `test-railorder`; not tried on a real Mac or Windows.
- **A short clip of a release's changes, as a trial** (spec 008 FR-171..FR-178, owner request): `npm run release:video -- --version <tag> --out <dir>` records 30-60 s of the simulator (title, one captioned scene per chosen change, end card) with the captions in a band under the app, and writes an MP4 and a GIF (ffmpeg needed), refusing to finish when a scene's text shows a local path, the user or host name or a token shape. A fragment may now end with optional `Highlight:` and `Scene:` lines (`changelog.d/README.md`), which the fold drops. The release notes now start with a Highlights block built from those lines, and the release steps in `changelog.d/README.md` make the clip part of the flow, starting with v0.1.9 (the first clip is shown to the owner before it is published; older releases get none). Team tooling, no app change, 0 AI credits. Checked by `test:releasevideo` and a v0.1.7 trial on Linux; not run on macOS or Windows.
- **Open with menu in the chat header (spec 009 FR-019..FR-027, #685).** The header's *Open in VS Code* button is now **Open with**: it lists the installed VS Code, VS Code Insiders and VSCodium, and **Show in folder** for the system file manager. It opens the agent's folder only and says the conversation stays in Durbar; **Continue in another app…** (More menu, when a second agent app is installed) is still the way to hand it over. A folder that is gone or a file is refused with a message; a failed launch says so. Something the file manager would run is selected, not opened. A project folder that is a symbolic link is opened at its real path, with a note saying so; a link to a file or to nowhere is refused.
