The folder status dialog's **Open in editor** and **Continue in VS Code** go through the same folder checks and use the first installed edition that can open the folder (on Windows, the next one when the first is only a `.cmd` and the folder's name has a character `cmd.exe` could misread); the profile editor's button is renamed **Open in editor** but opens the profile file as before. Simulator, unit tests and a driver at 1440x900 and 1280x800; no editor or file manager was launched by a test, and the one real check (a folder opened in Finder) ran before the later changes.
- **Agent events are checked in the app** (spec 010 FR-035, issue #1039): every event from Copilot, Claude Code or Codex now passes the same shape check the tests use before the window or any other part of Durbar sees it. A malformed one is dropped, with a log line that names only the event type and the fields at fault, never their values. Checked with `test:runtime-core` and one short real session per app (149 events, none dropped).
- **Settings: "Saved" only when it was saved** (spec 011, issues #1094, #1047): asking again for a value already in force while the settings file cannot be written no longer says Saved, and a failed save no longer leaves a `.tmp` file next to the settings. Checked by `test:credits` (injected write and rename failures, mutation-checked).
- Test driver only (issue #1136): the Codex stop-escalation test waits for the signals instead of a fixed sleep, so it no longer fails on a loaded machine. No change for users.
- **No MCP config left in the temp folder** (spec 012, issue #1093): the private folder holding `mcp-config.json` (MCP env and header values) for a Claude session is now removed also when the app quits while Claude still runs; every other ending (spawn failure, error, kill, close) was already covered and now has a test. `npm test` no longer leaves `durbar-*` folders behind and fails a suite that does.
- **A helper started in the background no longer reads Done while it works** (spec 015 FR-018, issue #1141): when an agent launches a helper in the background (Claude Code's `run_in_background`, Copilot's background task), the agent's own call returns at once; the helper's chat block, card and transcript now stay Running until the helper itself finishes or fails, and a helper still running when its session stops reads Failed ("Stopped with its session"). Foreground helpers are unchanged. Checked with mapper tests and a simulator driver; not seen on a real session.
- **Auto-follow work, opt in** (spec 052 FR-182..FR-190, issue 686): a new switch in Settings (off by default, kept on this computer; main answers a real `ok`) makes a sent message take you back to the office with that agent's desk marked and focused, and open its chat (focus on the chat title, never on Allow or Deny) when it finishes, asks, errors or reaches its limit. It never answers or approves anything, waits while you type, have a dialog or menu open, or the window is in the background, stops following when you open another agent yourself, and respects reduced motion (no animation). A strip under the top bar shows Previous and Next over the attention queue (the Needs you inbox order, then unread replies) with the position and count (keys `[` and `]`), a Pause button, and, when nothing needs you, Next returns to the office with the last desk focused. Checked in the simulator (`check:ui` check-auto-follow, both sizes, 15 agents) and `test:autofollow`; not run against a real agent session.
- **Needs you text no longer runs words together** (spec 052 FR-214): the inbox, the answer bar, the announcements and the Real office slips read "search_companies) Allow for this session ...", not "search_companies)Allow ...", and a card with several questions shows all of them. Checked by the simulator driver `check-inbox-text`.
- **MCP sign-ins kept fresh** (spec 058 FR-204..FR-217, first part): before a chat's first message after an hour without a reply (or a wake from sleep), Agent Durbar asks Copilot or Claude Code to reconnect the chat's remote MCP servers, so the app renews an expired sign-in with its own refresh token. The message waits at most 5 s under one quiet line; Stop sends nothing; a server that still needs a sign-in gets a line with Sign in…. On by default (*Settings > MCP servers*); in Copilot's sandboxed modes (Read-only, Ask first) only the sign-in line, since a reconnect there did not renew the sign-in (Linux probe); Durbar never sees a token; no local server, no Codex chat, on macOS only servers that already need a sign-in. Simulator, fakes and unit tests; the apps' behaviour probed on Linux.
- **Organizations can require the OS sandbox on macOS and Linux** (spec 076 FR-209, issue #734): Durbar reads the machine `policy.json` (`/Library/Application Support/AgentDurbar/managed/` on macOS, `/etc/agent-durbar/managed/` on Linux) for `permissions.requireOsSandbox`. Only a root-owned file in root-owned, not group- or world-writable folders counts, checked along the path as written and through any link on it. A policy folder whose file is missing or broken also requires the sandbox, and the log says why. Windows is not read yet. Checked with `test:sandbox-floor`; no real session.
- **Agent-app updates: a vendor "updates are disabled" answer is no longer shown as done** (spec 081, #662): on Linux, `claude update` with updates disabled exits 0, and Durbar now reports it as *Managed by your organization*. The two Claude fixtures are recorded shapes; checked on Linux with an isolated copy, macOS and Windows still open.
- **Copilot gets a private temp folder** (spec 087 FR-023, issue #755): Copilot's sandbox always keeps a temp folder writable. Durbar now starts the Copilot runtime with a private one (a new `durbar-cprt-` folder, mode 0700, removed when that runtime stops or the app quits) instead of the shared temp folder, with `TMPDIR` (and `TEMP`/`TMP` on Windows) pointing at it. The chip tooltip and the approval card say that approved commands can write there too. Checked for real on Linux and macOS (2026-11-04, Ask first): a write in the private folder was allowed, and a folder beside it in the shared temp folder (`/tmp` on Linux, the per-user temp folder on macOS), `/tmp` and the home folder were refused; no folder was left behind. Not run on Windows; Copilot sessions of the same run share the folder.
- **Fewer layout reads when an agent's status changes** (spec 097 FR-184, T156, T159): fitting headers and usage lines and placing the rename pencils is done only for what a redraw changed, and in full whenever something that decides a fit changes (a status filter, Simple or Expert, the doors' signs). Layout reads for one status change in the simulator: 650 to 98 at 15 agents, 998 to 86 at 40; a redraw asks for the floor's style once. The compact desks' tapping hands now move (they showed one position because of a `steps(1)` that holds the first keyframe). New simulator check rows count renders, layout searches and reads, animations, stale fits and misplaced marks. The start (T157) and the halo's cost (T158) are measured and not changed.
- Internal (Autopilot is not reachable in the app yet): a goal with a line starting with `/` is no longer refused on Claude Code, and the goal limits (4000 UTF-16 units, six reserved words) were checked against the real CLI (spec 099).
- **Autopilot core, types and event guard** (spec 099 phase 1): the shared run counter (stop at limit + 1, end once, backstop, fail-closed guard), the goal text and end-reason helpers, the `autopilot` event and `autoAnswered` checks. Not reachable yet: no adapter reports it and there is no setting or button.
- **Previous sessions and chat history, Claude Code** (spec 099, user-visible, live): a `Stop hook feedback:` message that Claude Code writes itself (a synthetic message, for example from your own Stop hook) is no longer shown as a prompt you typed and no longer becomes the session title. A message you typed with the same words still shows. Checked with `npm run test:autopilot` and mutation checks.
- **The next built-in world, the owner's third and fourth looks** (spec 102, still behind its closed world flag): the floor is used at 3, 8 and 15 agents, in staggered rows that never mix two folders (a zigzag was tried and removed, see the spec).
  A folder of 4 or fewer stays one group under one sign; with 12 to 15 agents in 4 folders some still continue on the next row.
  A small room stands close and centred, drawn up to five times bigger with 4 agents or fewer, over a lower lobby when that lets it grow, its text growing with it. Cabinets come in four bodies (upright, cocktail table, racer, pinball) and four colours. Everything is dark at night, and nobody is cut by a cabinet.
  An asleep agent sits on a stool, while an idle agent stands awake. Waiting and limit plates and away notes are dark. The "needs you" pills keep to a lane under the lobby.
  Checked by `test-realoffice-aisles`, `test-realoffice-assets` and the `shots-102-arcade` driver at 1440x900 and 1280x800.
- **No "undefined" status** (spec 102, every world, not only behind the flag): an agent at its limit or asleep with no label of its own showed "undefined"; it now says *Limit reached* or *Asleep*, the words spec 052 FR-076 and 097 FR-120 already use. A folder sign's path now gives way before the folder's name when the font is wider than expected (seen on Linux).
- **Asleep people in the next built-in world are drawn, not built** (spec 102 FR-259, still behind its closed world flag): the seated `sit` frame of each of the 15 pixel people is now a Codex-drawn picture shrunk to the pack grid (`scripts/build-arcade-sleep.py`), not a standing frame with a stool drawn under it. Faces were kept readable (the standing frame's skin tone, closed-eye lines kept); looked at on contact sheets and in the simulator at both sizes, not yet by the owner.
- **World packs, phase 1** (spec 102): a strict zip reader and a world-pack validator for packs from outside the app (zip layout, names, sizes, picture headers, `world.json` and its colours), with a hostile fixture set in `npm test`. Nothing in the app calls them yet, so no user sees a change.
- **Website guards before the redesign** (spec 104 phase 1, for contributors): the site build now fails if a world or feature that is not in a release is named on a page, in a caption or transcript, or in the README (`docs/site/unreleased.json`), leaves out the notes of draft specs from its specs index, takes its download only from a stable app release (never an asset-only release such as `media-library`, a pre-release or a test build), allows same-origin video (`media-src 'self'`) and checks a video manifest (`docs/site/media.json`: budgets, hashes, captions, poster, licence and who made and approved each file). `npm run blog:check` (in `npm test`) guards the coming agent blog: tokens, private paths, addresses, chat markers, unknown hosts, owners' first names, evidence on `main`, a security hold, and a human read and an agent review where the spec asks. Nothing is published to a blog yet; no page shows a video yet. Checked by unit tests with stand-ins and mutants; the capture measurement ran on Linux under Xvfb only.
- Compaction: the events of Claude Code, Codex and Copilot were checked on real sessions (manual compaction) and the shapes are recorded. After a Codex compaction the context ring waits for the next message instead of showing the old size, and a Copilot manual compaction ends its "Compacting" mark from the answer when no end event comes.

## v0.1.8 ([release](https://github.com/three-feathers/agent-durbar/releases/tag/v0.1.8), 2026-11-03)

- **Settings that cannot be saved are said so** (spec 011, issue #1023): when the settings file cannot be written, a switch (serious mode, notifications, break resume, update check, chat links, default cap, characters, places) no longer reports success and then forgets the choice at restart: it stays as it was and the row shows "Could not save this setting. It stays on." Things that must keep working stay in force for the current run: the session browser's first-use note, an update announced once, the last new-folder location, the theme, turning the OS-sandbox always-continue choice off and a lower default cap; where it matters the window says it may not last after a restart. Checked in `test:credits`, `test:sandbox-ops`, `test:browseripc` and the simulator driver `check-settings-save` (both sizes); no Windows or macOS run.
- **Codex no longer starts a second copy of each local MCP server on every status read** (spec 012 FR-164, issue #1077): the MCP status at session start, with the first message, after a sign-in challenge and in the context figures was read with one request that made Codex 0.163.0 run every local server's command again (in Read-only too). Each server is now read by name on the session's own connection; the names of your own `config.toml` servers come from Codex's `config/read`, which starts nothing. Checked by unit tests and by driving the built adapter against the real `codex app-server` with no prompt (a start marker per stdio server stayed at 1 over a start, three status reads and two context reads; 7 before). Not run on macOS or Windows or with a signed-in account.
- **Activity export is written safely** (spec 052, issue #973): *Tools > Activity > Export* now writes through a temp file and a rename, mode 0600, and refuses a link, hard link, folder, FIFO or device at the chosen name instead of writing through it, like the notes export. Content and dialog are unchanged. Checked with real temp folders in `test:activity` (link target untouched); not run on Windows or macOS.
- **Text popups stay open while you select their text** (spec 052 FR-237): a drag that starts in a dialog and ends outside it (selecting a field's text and overshooting) no longer closes it. It did for 11 popups: the Rename, Rename floor, Place, palette, Schedules, spending limit, New session and New profile dialogs, the floors popover's new floor name, the Needs you inbox and the keyboard sheet. A click into a folder's rename field no longer closes the Folders panel. One shared helper decides; checked in the simulator with real mouse and keyboard input at both window sizes, and by unit tests.
- **MCP servers: the chat's actions in Settings, and a health check at launch** (spec 058 FR-195..FR-203): Settings > MCP servers now has **Sign in** and **Sign in again…** on a chat's row (they open that chat's MCP list and start the same sign-in there) and **Restart where it failed (N)** for a server that failed in several chats; a server of your agent apps' lists whose command cannot be found says so. When the app starts, one calm strip lists the MCP servers that need attention, with the reason and a suggested fix for each; nothing is fixed, started or sent for you. Simulator and unit tests only; not checked on a real agent app, macOS or Windows.
- **Fixed: Copilot MCP Sign in for Microsoft Entra servers** (issue #1084): since v0.1.7 it failed with "Microsoft Entra authentication requires the host to configure an Entra client ID", because a dependency update brought a Copilot runtime that needs an app registration Agent Durbar does not have. The Copilot runtime is back to the one from v0.1.6 (SDK 1.0.24). Not checked with a real Entra sign-in yet.
- **MCP dialog: a visible Tools button, tool rows that fit, and tokens for the ticked tools** (spec 069 FR-067, FR-068): each server row has a *Tools* button (wrench, "Tools (24)" or "Tools: 20 of 24 on") instead of only the tiny arrow; long tool descriptions stay inside the card (2 lines, full text on hover); each tool shows "about N tokens" and the server's token figure counts only the ticked tools until *Restart session*. Simulator checks (`shots-069-mcp-tools`, both sizes, two looks, three mutations); not verified on macOS or Windows.
- Settings > Agent apps: a Homebrew app with an update waiting no longer shows "Could not check: exit 1" (`brew outdated` exits 1 when it finds one). A failed check now also shows a short, masked detail line. (#1081)
- **Copilot's sandbox switch checked on macOS** (spec 087 K16, issue 834): a real probe shows the OS sandbox clears in place on a switch to Allow all and comes back on the way out, as on Linux. The probe's own cleanup now handles what macOS leaves behind. No change in the app.

## v0.1.7 ([release](https://github.com/three-feathers/agent-durbar/releases/tag/v0.1.7), 2026-11-03)

- **Waiting desks no longer pulse** (spec 001 FR-210): the glow behind a Flat desk's screen is still, which gives back most of the renderer's CPU with many agents in view (1.6 % of a core against 6.8 % at 15 agents on the simulator under Linux). Checked by `check-001-halo`; not measured on macOS or Windows.
- When Codex's auto-review or Copilot's assisted approval approves a tool call, its card now says so ("Allowed: agent app classifier"). Claude Code reports no reason, so its calls still show no tag.
- **A merge can no longer leave a renderer script unparsable unnoticed** (spec 008): `npm test` now includes a fast check that every `src/renderer/*.js` parses, with a stray-quote self-check. Syntax only; run on Linux.
- **No more doors** (spec 017, owner decision 2026-10-31, spec 021 FR-067): the **Office with cabins** layout and its Settings row, the door button on a desk, meeting-room signs, the waving and wallet poses, the flames, room door plates and **Close all** / **Open all** are gone. **Open office** (and the new Real office) are the layouts. **Hide folder** and floors stay, and so does the plain status word. Closed rooms open on the first start (`closed` in `durbar-folders.json` is dropped; `durbar.doorsigns` is no longer read). Scheduled prompts have no door check any more: a schedule for a session that had a closed door runs as normal. **Nothing held was sent:** a prompt that was queued behind a door when you updated stays queued; one notice asks once to **Send** (one by one, through the schedules), **Discard** or **Decide later**, and what is left stays in Settings > Schedules as **Held from before the update**. Remote control that a door had turned off stays off; the update never turns it on. Lost with the doors: one click to hold a session's schedules (use **Pause** or **Pause all schedules**, which skip a run instead of queuing it) and one click that also cut remote control (use **Stop remote control**). For MCP clients: `durbar_list_folders` no longer returns a `door` field (spec 041 FR-066). A saved theme that names `door-art` or `--door-*` still loads; they are ignored. Simulator-checked on Linux; not run on Windows or macOS or with a real session.
- **Old app-level MCP servers are gone from the folder extras dialog** (spec 020 FR-032): the "Old app-level servers (not applied)" section with *Move here* and *Remove*, from the first versions, is removed. Those servers were never used. An old settings file that still lists them is ignored and its lists stay as inert text, but the secret values stored for those old servers are dropped the next time you change a secret; your folder extras and their secrets work as before. Checked with unit tests and the simulator; not run on Windows or macOS.
- **Feature inventory** (spec 025 delta 2026-11-01, owner request): `docs/feature-inventory.md` lists each user-visible feature with its state in Flat, Real office and the arrangement worlds, its spec and how each is proven; `npm test` checks that specs and proofs exist and that every built spec is listed. Docs and scripts only; 0 AI credits; a row's state is not proven by the check.
- **Floor switch always repaints the office** (spec 033 FR-088, issue #787): if a side panel (filter, Floors panel, Settings floors list) fails while a floor is switched, the office is still drawn with a full layout. Hardens one possible cause of the unreproduced report; #787 is not proven fixed. Checked in the simulator with `scripts/check-floor-repaint.js`, which fails without the change.
- **Corporate and the three Durbar court themes are removed** (spec 035 FR-017..FR-020, owner decision): Clean Office and Retro Pixel remain. If Corporate or a court was your theme, Durbar switches you once to Clean Office and keeps your Characters choice. A theme of your own that used one of their styles or people-and-furniture kits keeps working: that one value is drawn like Clean Office, with a note in Settings. Specs 037 and 039 are marked deprecated and superseded (new `deprecated` spec status, spec 021 FR-068). Checked in the simulator and by unit tests; not checked on a real session.
- **The AI theme maker and Import are retired** (spec 040 FR-031, owner decision, 2026-11-02): the AI theme maker is gone and there is no way to make a new theme with AI until the world maker (spec 102) exists. Settings > Theme no longer offers *Make a theme with AI* or *Import a theme*. Themes you already saved keep working, stay in the list tagged "yours", and can still be exported; the new **Remove the active theme…** deletes one after a question (the theme in use goes to Clean Office; the update deletes nothing). The theme maker's IPC channels and its screenshots are gone, and organisation switches for the maker (`allowThemeMaker`, `features.themeMaker`) have nothing to turn off. Checked in the simulator at both sizes and by unit tests; not checked on a real session.
- **Scheduled prompts: two fixes** (spec 044): a one-time schedule you edit while its run is going out no longer runs a second time, and a schedule you delete while another run is going out no longer fires. Checked with two scheduler tests that fail without the fixes; not run against a real agent app.
- **A message after a stuck Stop is no longer lost, and a session reopened after one keeps working** (spec 052 FR-228..FR-230, issue #1028; checked on a real Copilot runtime on Linux, not on macOS or Windows): when Copilot has not answered Stop after 5 seconds the row says "Stop sent: Copilot has not answered yet" instead of only "Aborted". A message sent then waits for the Stop to land (at most 30 seconds, then the chat says it was not sent); before, the late Stop cleared it with no error. Reopening a session whose End has not landed waits the same way; before, it opened, and the next call failed with "Session not found".
- **The spending limit after a stuck Stop, and a raise that was too small** (spec 052 FR-231..FR-235, issue #1028; measured on a real Copilot runtime on Linux, not on macOS or Windows): a message sent after a stuck Stop at the limit waits on the limit card; before, the card could read "stopped here" while the message stayed behind. Stop at the limit no longer shows "No response was returned". A raise that stays under what was used keeps the card and the message; before, the message was lost, or the card said "credits added" and the turn ended. A session over its limit opens again and asks at the next message; before, Copilot refused to reopen it.
- **Needs you cannot answer by accident** (spec 052, FR-206..FR-208): a row's button answers only the request it was shown for (a request already answered in the chat is not answered again; the row says "Already answered"); a spending-limit row shows the amount it will add, has no letter keys and says what you pressed, never "Allowed"; holding A, S, D or Enter (or Alt+A, Alt+S, Alt+D in a chat) no longer answers down the queue. Checked in the simulator with `check-inbox-safety` (all three failed before) and five mutants; not run on Windows or macOS.
- **Answer bar, message box, limits and plans cannot answer by accident** (spec 052 FR-209..FR-212, spec 066 FR-064): a repainted answer bar no longer puts focus on Allow once (a native Enter answered a different request); for a spending limit the bar only says "Show in chat"; a held Enter in the message box no longer sends the draft; a limit answer in flight cannot be answered again, a late one no longer hides the next limit, and 0.004 is not an amount; a plan card does not take focus while you type. Checked in the simulator with `check-answer-safety` and native held keys; not run on Windows or macOS.
- **A click or key inside Needs you stays there** (spec 052 FR-213): clicking a row's amount or button no longer opens that agent's chat behind the dialog and moves focus to its message box (typing 50 there sent "50" as a message); Enter in the amount raises the limit; after an answer focus stays in the dialog. The plan card takes focus only when no dialog is open, the box is empty and focus is on the page (spec 066 FR-064); an agent's own option labels no longer catch the answer bar's focus; a question answer in flight is locked.
- **Hung spending-limit answers and typing on plans** (spec 052 FR-209..FR-213, spec 066 FR-064): a limit card says "Sending…" and unlocks with a note after 30 seconds; Stop always goes on (the Copilot adapter gives up on a stuck runtime call after 5 seconds); a Needs you row in flight says "Still waiting"; the answer bar does nothing on a locked card; typing when a plan arrives goes to the message box; a repaint keeps focus in a limit row's amount.
- **Stop goes on even when the runtime hangs** (spec 052 FR-209): Copilot `stop`, `interrupt` and End session give up on a hung limit cancel, abort or disconnect after 5 seconds; a settled limit card keeps its status when a late answer fails; pasting when a plan arrives goes to the message box.
- **No more Simple / Expert switch** (spec 052 FR-236, owner decision 2026-11-02, spec 021 FR-300): Settings > *Detail level*, the palette items and the welcome card's "Do you write code?" question are gone; everyone gets what Expert showed (tokens, model, context ring, paths, tool names and commands). People who had Simple move to it with no notice, and the old stored choice is dropped once. The one plain wording kept: the spending-limit chip says "No spending limit". The Settings section that held the switch is now **Display** (Spacing, Live activity and the office options). Simulator-checked at 1440x900 and 1280x800; not run on Windows or macOS.
- **E2E real accounts green again** (spec 062, #856): the daily real-account check read an agent app's "OS sandbox could not start" as an empty answer. It now fails on Linux and macOS when a sandbox cannot start, continues without it only on Windows for Claude Code and Copilot (recorded in the row), and installs `bubblewrap` and `socat` on Linux. The daily schedule is back and a failed scheduled run is reported by the nightly watch. Checked by three hand runs on all three OSes and `test:platforms`.
- **Text on macOS is a little stronger**: the app no longer thins its strokes, so grey text in chats and on cards is easier to read (spec 070).
- **Settings > Organization** (spec 076 FR-208, issue 602): a read-only section lists, for each agent app, whether your organization's policy file applies and what it blocks (permission modes in the same words as the greyed-out menus, counts of model, server and tool rules, the files it came from, and what could not be read). A policy that could not be read in full, or a failed read, is shown as unknown, never as "no policy". Nothing is written. macOS profiles, the Windows registry and server-managed settings are still not read. Checked with unit tests (`test:orgwords`) and the simulator driver `shots-076-orgpolicy`; not checked on a managed machine.
- **Monster Crew** (spec 077 FR-075, issue #815): a fourth crew in Settings, Characters, next to Cheeky, Prehistoric and Pirate: six friendly monsters (Frankenstein, a ghost, a mummy, a sea creature, a vampire and a werewolf), each drawn working, waiting and resting like the other crews, on any theme. Each session gets one at random among those used least, kept per session; your saved choices and the other crews are unchanged. Checked on the simulator at 1440x900 and 1280x800 (`check-crew`, `check-characters`); not run on Windows or macOS. The 18 pictures add about 0.6 MB to the app (the crews' pictures together are now about 3.0 MB, under a cap raised from 2.5 to 3 MiB).
- **Pirate Crew** (spec 077 FR-074, issue #815): a third crew in Settings, Characters, next to Cheeky and Prehistoric: six pirates (a captain with a tricorn, a sailor with an eyepatch and a red bandana, a bearded hook pirate, a round cook, a privateer with a feathered hat and a cabin boy), each drawn working, waiting and resting like the other crews, on any theme. Each session gets one at random among those used least, kept per session; your saved choices and the other crews are unchanged. Checked on the simulator at 1440x900 and 1280x800 (`check-crew`, `check-characters`); not run on Windows or macOS.
- **Animal Rebels Crew** (spec 077 FR-077, issue #815): a sixth crew in Settings, Characters, next to Cheeky, Prehistoric, Pirate, Monster and Royal: six animal characters (a bulldog, a fox, an owl, a panda, a punk cat and a raccoon), each drawn working, waiting and resting like the other crews, on any theme. Each session gets one at random among those used least, kept per session; your saved choices and the other crews are unchanged. This is the last of the five crews of issue #815. Checked on the simulator at 1440x900 and 1280x800 (`check-crew`, `check-characters`); not run on Windows or macOS. The 18 pictures add about 0.69 MB to the app (the crews' pictures together are now about 4.5 MB, under a cap raised from 4 to 4.5 MiB; 4.25 MiB did not fit).
- **Royal Crew** (spec 077 FR-076, issue #815): a fifth crew in Settings, Characters, next to Cheeky, Prehistoric, Pirate and Monster: six royal characters (an alchemist, a princess, a knight, a jester, a queen and a king), each drawn working, waiting and resting like the other crews, on any theme. Each session gets one at random among those used least, kept per session; your saved choices and the other crews are unchanged. Checked on the simulator at 1440x900 and 1280x800 (`check-crew`, `check-characters`); not run on Windows or macOS. The 18 pictures add about 0.66 MB to the app (the crews' pictures together are now about 3.8 MB, under a cap raised from 3 to 4 MiB; 3.5 MiB did not fit).
- **Notes in the project: `.durbar/notes/notes.md`, ignored by git** (spec 083 Changes 2026-11-01, replaces #957): the optional project file now lives in `.durbar/notes/` next to a `.gitignore` holding `*`, so `git add -A` never stages it, in any folder name (a real-git test covers NFC and NFD names, spaces and a leading `#` or `!`); Durbar no longer writes `.git/info/exclude`. The `.gitignore` is checked before every write: if it was changed, is a link or is too big, nothing is written; if it is gone, it is made again. The Notes screen's box is now *Keep these notes in git*, unticked, and asks in a system dialog before Durbar's `.gitignore` is left out. An old `.durbar/notes.md` is left alone (and an old `info/exclude` line from v0.1.5 stays, which makes *Keep in git* do nothing in that clone) and no longer followed: every folder's switch starts off after this update, so turn it on again to write the new file. Checked with unit tests and real `git` (not on macOS or Windows by hand).
- **Floors count up from the bottom** (spec 092 Q-D): the lift and All floors now show floor 1 at the bottom, and PageUp goes to the next number up, as in a real building. Floor names, numbers, badges and what a screen reader hears are unchanged. Checked with unit tests and the simulator; not tried on a real keyboard's PageUp key or with a screen reader.
- **The chat in Real office follows the world** (spec 097 FR-106, T120; still behind Real office): a 64 px portrait cropped on the face with a lamp-coloured ring and status dot in the chat header, the lamp in words next to the status, and the world's tint on the panel (opaque; every text pair checked for contrast). The chat keeps the whole window's width, and its words, buttons and tab order are the flat ones. Checked on the simulator at 1440x900 and 1280x800, not on a real GPU, Windows or macOS by hand.
- **The Real office uses far less CPU when nothing changes** (spec 097 FR-228, FR-229): its motion starts on the graphics side from the first moment, and redraws 12 times a second, all on one clock.
- **Photo office keyboard:** a folder sign that redraws while its + or room menu button has the focus keeps the focus on that button (spec 097 FR-230).
- **Nine world moments for the Photo office and the Prehistoric cave** (spec 097 FR-118..FR-129, still behind Real office and the cave's closed flag): the first run, the empty floor (three empty desks under the flat sentence), an agent asleep at its desk (it rests in the dark at its desk, "Asleep · finished 34 min ago"), the inbox as slips at the Boss door (one-line pill when crowded) and a dialog header with the Boss door, the spending-limit slip at the Bank, the MCP request bubble, the coffee-break post-it ("at Coffee corner until 6:13"; every place has its own post-it colour) and a lift ride between floors. Every word, button and order is the flat one (the cards' own buttons are clicked). Nothing animates but transform and opacity; reduced motion and Serious mode stop it. Checked on the simulator at 1440x900 and 1280x800 (`scripts/shots-097-moments.js`), not on a real GPU, Windows or macOS by hand.
- **MCP context cost and warning** (spec 100): the MCP chip, the chat's MCP dialog and the token summary show what each server's tool definitions take in the session's window (reported by Copilot and Claude Code, counted as "at most" for Codex, which loads tools on demand, so a Codex figure never warns). Above 15 % or 30 % of the window (configurable, or off, in Settings > MCP servers) the chip changes colour with the reason in words and one dismissible line names the heaviest servers; a burst of compactions adds a hint to the compaction line. Nothing is ever switched off by itself. Checked with unit tests, fakes and the simulator at 1440x900 and 1280x800; checked on real Copilot, Claude Code and Codex sessions (Linux): Copilot's figures come from its tool definitions, and Claude Code's tools count when its tool search is off.
- **A damaged MCP server file is no longer overwritten** (spec 019): it is moved aside as `durbar-mcp.json.unreadable-<time>` before the next save, so your folder servers and saved secrets are still there to recover.
- **Pre-review bot, built without a key** (spec 103, team tooling under `scripts/`, no app change): a read-only first read of one of our PRs for the coordinating session, never an approval. Refuses outside authors, forks and Dependabot first; reads the diff and the filtered PR text in a detached worktree with only Read, Grep and Glob, confined by a hook; per-run and monthly spend ceilings with a ledger; an off-by-default `post` path as a GitHub App that stays shut until all three owners confirm. Checked with a fake SDK (`npm run test:prereview`, 0 credits); no real call made. Review fixes: a signed gate payload, safe results writes, glob allow-list, path whitespace refused (a Grep path with a leading space escaped the hook), the real pinned SDK binary tested against a loopback mock, `prereview ack` needs a terminal and a typed word. The optional Codex runner was removed. Third round: split tokens are stripped before they are masked, PR-shipped ignore files cannot hide files from Grep, the API key goes to the claude process on a file descriptor instead of its environment, CI fails instead of skipping the real-SDK test. Fourth round: a glued key and every private key block spelling are masked, and the report names changed binary files and `.svn`/`.hg`-style paths as not searched. Fifth round: the operator never runs or trusts code, the trust list or the gate key that the bot's user could have swapped (root-owned top folder, ancestor-ownership check), one repository constant, title renames counted. `install` empties its folder inside the root-owned layout instead of removing it.

## v0.1.6 ([release](https://github.com/three-feathers/agent-durbar/releases/tag/v0.1.6), 2026-11-02)

- **The secret mask, compared with public lists** (spec 012 FR-080, issue 1009): debug logs, desk lines, notes, MCP output and tool labels now hide about 70 more token shapes (npm, Google, Stripe, AWS, Azure, GitLab, Hugging Face and others), private key blocks, `password=...` style values, cookies and signed URLs; there is now one mask instead of two levels. Every entry of gitleaks, GitHub's list and detect-secrets is in a [coverage table](specs/012-claude-codex-adapters/secret-coverage.md) with why it is or is not hidden. No list is complete: secrets with no fixed shape, and encoded or split ones, still pass. A long crafted line can no longer stall the app for 4.5 s while it is masked.
- **`specs:check` refuses conflict markers** (spec 025 FR-018): a merge left in a spec now fails the check, naming the file and line. Development only; `test:specsindex`.
- The Floors panel is easier to use (issue #1010): the on/off is a toggle switch like the one for MCP servers, **Show only** is now a **Focus mode** icon button with a tooltip, Up and Down are arrow buttons, Rename and Delete are quiet icon buttons, and you can drag a floor by its handle to reorder it.
- Switches (floors, MCP servers, Settings) are darker green when on and follow the office world, so on and off are easier to tell apart.
- **Rearrange the office by drag and drop** (spec 033 delta 2026-11-01, issue #677): drag a room's header, a desk or a lobby place to put it before or after another; the same with Alt+arrows (Alt+Shift+arrows for the room) or *Move earlier / Move later* in the folder menu, with a spoken note and the focus kept. The order is saved with the floors, is the same in every theme, and only sequences things: the layout stays automatic and 15 agents still fit. *Reset the office order* is in the command palette. Simulator and unit tests (`test:roomorder`, `shots-033-order`), 0 AI credits; a failed save of the order is reported, and a removed folder loses its saved place; not looked at on macOS or Windows.
- **The account check test no longer times hung cleanups with the clock** (spec 052): `test:account` proves hung Copilot cleanup calls do not hold the account answer by the answer arriving at all (a 10 s race guards a hang), instead of a 1 s wall-clock budget that failed under CI load. Test-only.
- **A refused sign-in by your organization is explained under the failed MCP tool call** (spec 058 FR-193, FR-194, issue #841): when a tool fails with an Entra Conditional Access code (for example `AADSTS530003`, a managed-device check), the chat shows one fixed line saying this usually means your organization's sign-in rules refused it, and that signing in again from a browser your organization allows may help, with a **Sign in again…** button that opens the MCP servers dialog. The server's own error text is never repeated in that line. Not yet run against a real tenant.
- **Mac keychain prompts: the signature check re-run** (spec 058 FR-160): a local unsigned mac package build on a Mac (no account, app not launched) kept GitHub's signature on the Copilot runtime (`codesign -dv`: `copilot`, team VEKTX9H2N7) and the app's strict deep verify passed. The signature check and the one-runtime retry were mutation-checked. The real prompt count with two sessions is still not counted on a Mac (#623).
- **Prehistoric cave world, not offered yet** (spec 097 phase 4, spec 102): the pack for a second Real office world is in the app but switched off: a cave of rock, torchlight and slate monitors with six cave people (Paco's art), 1.8 MB, loaded only when shown. Nothing changes on screen until the owner opens it. When on, Settings gets a World row (Photo office, Prehistoric cave) and a Characters pick of Prehistoric draws the cave people in the Photo office too. Not finished: nine moments (lounge, walk, empty office, first run, floor jumps, inbox, limit card, elicitation, coffee bar) have no cave design yet, so the cave cannot ship.
- **Lobby people face their fixture** (spec 097): in Real office, visitors now stand with their back to you, turned toward the Library shelf, the Terminal (running), the Boss door, the counter or the Postbox, with their feet on the floor in front of it (new pictures for all 15 people, mirrored to face the fixture; the pack is 5.9 MB, 0.2 MB smaller). Checked on the simulator at 1440x900 and 1280x800 with 15 agents; p03 and p15 lift the same leg in both running pictures.
- **Calmer redraws of the office** (spec 097 FR-185, T148): an idle crew portrait is dimmed by a class the code sets on the drawing instead of a `:has()` rule, so one text change in the office no longer makes the browser recalculate the style of most of the page (1401 elements at 15 agents to 16, 2417 at 40 to 41, in the simulator). The rule's cost did not depend on the Characters choice. The look is unchanged. What one status change costs is still more and is measured in a later change; the new simulator check `check-office-ops` counts the elements recalculated by one text change.
- **Real office, phase 2** (spec 097): the monitors show each agent's newest output, masked like the desk's live readout and repainted at most every 2 seconds; hover a monitor or tab to its desk to zoom it, Esc closes; with 4 agents or fewer the monitors are big enough to read. People move in small steps (typing, waving, thinking, resting; visitors sip, knock and jog), and nothing moves with reduced motion, in Serious mode, in a hidden window or out of view.
- **Masking** (spec 097): a token with an invisible character inside (zero-width, soft hyphen, a joiner) is now masked on desks, titles and Real office monitors.
- **Real office: people move smoothly** (spec 097): each person breathes and sways on their own timing, a typing person's head and hands move on their own, and someone going to a place zooms out at the desk and in at the place, leaving a post-it on the desk that says where. The room stays still when someone leaves; desks are a little smaller at 15 agents as a result. Reduced motion and Serious mode stop it as before; hidden or off-screen desks pause.
- **Real office mode, phase 1** (spec 097): Settings > Office layout has a third choice, **Real office**: the same agents in a realistic open-plan office, a person at each desk in a pose for its status, a monitor and a name plate with the status word, one zone per folder, and the places along the back wall with their visitors standing on the floor in front of them at a real size against the door (name tags above their heads) (one fixture per place, wider when crowded); whoever is at the Terminal jogs in place beside it (new running pictures for all 15 people, no treadmill). Status lights: warm lamp while working, amber call light when it needs you, small steady red on an error, orange at the limit, off when asleep, and a slightly darker room when few agents are active; every light repeats a word or icon, none flashes, they stand still with reduced motion or Serious mode and are not drawn with forced colours. Flat stays the default and loads none of it. Adds 6.1 MB of pictures (15 invented people, MIT). Checked on the simulator (layout check at 1440x900 and 1280x800, unit tests for the layout and the lights, an asset test); live screen text, motion, crews and manual lighting come later. While Real office is shown, the whole app takes its look: a dark wood-and-glass top bar and toasts, frosted-glass menus, popovers and dialogs, warm neutrals and oak-and-amber buttons and focus rings, every text pair checked at WCAG AA; status colours and fonts stay, forced colours use the system colours, and leaving Real office brings your theme back. With the Cheeky or Prehistoric characters, Settings says Real office shows the Office people until that crew's world is ready.
- **Office layout** (spec 097): choosing an Office layout in Settings keeps keyboard focus on the button you pressed.
- **Real office: worlds and people as separate packs** (spec 097 phase 4, spec 102): the Real office room and its people now live in separate folders and load only when shown, `html[data-world]` names the shown world, and Settings > Characters says **World's default (Office people)** in Real office. A **World** row appears in Settings when a second world is offered; none is yet, so nothing changes on screen. Characters with no Real office poses (Minimalist, Cheeky, Prehistoric) now say so in one line under Office layout. No new pictures; the pictures only moved.
- **A weekly check of the office worlds** (spec 098, for contributors): `scripts/world-matrix.js` and a weekly workflow take one picture and run the page checks per world moment and world, on the simulator. Nothing in the app changes. A failed weekly run opens the nightly issue.
- **Downloadable worlds move to their own repository** (spec 102 delta 2026-11-01, owner decision): packs will be served from `three-feathers/agent-durbar-worlds`; worlds that ship in the app stay here, the app holds each pack's hash and loads nothing without one. Spec only; nothing is built.

## v0.1.5 ([release](https://github.com/three-feathers/agent-durbar/releases/tag/v0.1.5), 2026-11-01)

- **The README is short** (spec 021 FR-065): about 90 lines instead of 400. The pitch, status, a feature list, install, the three permission modes, a quick start, development and credits stay; every removed detail moved unchanged to `docs/features.md`, `harnesses.md`, `install.md`, `safety.md`, `themes.md`, `development.md` and `limits.md`. The website is unchanged. Docs only, no app change.
- **A real Copilot session could not switch model, effort or window at all** (spec 030 FR-067): the switch asked the runtime to refuse any model it counted as unavailable, and on a paid plan it refused every model but Auto. Durbar no longer asks that, and checks the request against the account's own model list first. Not checked on a free plan or on a model the plan lacks: such a model may now fail at the next message, with the runtime's error, and the chip keeps the chosen one until you switch back.
- **Model, effort and context chosen one after another stay together** (spec 030 FR-060..FR-066, owner report): during a reply, on a session that has not started and after a slow answer, a second choice replaced the first, depending on the order. Each choice now builds on what the session runs next, a queued switch holds all of them and Cancel leaves nothing; a new model keeps the effort and the long window when it offers them and says so when it cannot. Claude Code and Codex sessions are checked against their own model list (their own models were refused), and Claude Code's default now offers its efforts. Checked with `test-model-choice`, the adapter tests and the driver `check-model-choice` at both sizes.
- **The top bar has no Demo / Live badge** (spec 052 FR-181): the pill under the app name is gone. It was a label only. A demo is still marked by the checked "Turn off demo" item in the ⋯ menu and by the composer hint ("Demo, no credits used" or "Demo: sample data").
- **Stricter reads of two files** (spec 083, issue #917): the connection key file and slash command files are now refused when they have a second hard link, and a slash command file is opened without following a link; all three capped file reads share one tested helper (`src/shared/cappedread.ts`). Checked by `test:cappedread` and the key file and slash command suites.
- **Export, delete all and removed folders' notes** (spec 083 T034b, FR-048, issue 699): the Notes screen has **Export…** (main shows the system save dialog and writes every note, done ones included, in the `notes.md` format, masked with the values known now, no session ids, mode 0600 where the file system has modes, atomically, refusing links, hard links, folders and devices) and **Delete all…** (asks with the count, Cancel focused, then a system dialog; only the notes it counted, tombstones kept). **Remove folder** asks in Durbar's own dialog, with **Also delete its notes (N)** (unticked, only when the folder has notes; ticked, a system dialog asks and only that folder's notes file is deleted; `.durbar/notes.md` is never touched). The simulator writes nothing. Checked with `test:notesexport` (real temp folders, a fake save dialog, the IPC handlers on a stand-in Electron) and the driver `check-notes-export` at 1440x900 and 1280x800; not checked by a person on Windows or macOS.
- **Notes: keep a folder's notes in the project, if you want** (spec 083 T042, issue 699): the folder's Notes screen has the switch *Also keep these notes as `.durbar/notes.md` in the project*, off by default. Turning it on asks first (*Cancel* focused): the file may be committed and pushed, text outside notes in an existing `notes.md` is removed, and an existing file's notes are counted and added as *From the project file*; inside a git repository a box (on) adds `.durbar/` to the clone's local `.git/info/exclude`. Turning it on and *Delete the file* also ask in a system dialog the page cannot answer; if the exclude line cannot be added the switch stays off until you choose to turn it on without it; the dialog counts lines of your own text an existing file would lose. Turning it off asks *Leave the file* or *Delete the file* (deleted only when it holds exactly what Durbar would write, never a link, and by a random name checked just before, which closes the swapped-folder race for a process that cannot read the other folder and narrows it otherwise; an empty `.durbar` goes too). A folder deleted and cloned again at the same path is recognised (by its creation time, where the file system records one) and not written into, and only turning the switch on creates the file. The file is masked with the token shapes and every known value of the folder and its open chats, waits while a secret is still locked, and is not written again by itself once it has disappeared. A file with merge conflicts or a failed write is shown on the screen and in the drawer. Not offered for a `.code-workspace`, greyed when the folder is missing; the simulator answers *Demo: no file written*. Checked with unit tests on temp folders (`test:notesswitch`) and a simulator driver (`check-notes-switch`); not run on Windows or macOS by a person.

## v0.1.4 ([release](https://github.com/three-feathers/agent-durbar/releases/tag/v0.1.4), 2026-10-31)

- **The list-columns check retries a lost hover on Windows** (spec 001): `check-list-columns` moves the pointer to the room-header pencil again (up to 4 times) until the page sees `:hover`, instead of relying on one mouse move that a Windows runner can drop while it activates the window. Test-only, no app change.
- **`check:office` waits instead of guessing** (spec 001 FR-191): the Browser tab check waits until the native view is attached and on the window (and gone after Back), and a figure off its lobby slot fails only once the figures and slots have stopped moving, so loaded machines no longer fail it by chance; failures say what was last seen. Checked with planted failures under Xvfb on Linux.
- **Better AI themes** (spec 040 FR-024..FR-030, issue #903): the theme maker's reply is no longer cut at 8,000 characters (Claude); it now draws the room wall, window and floor and a desk backdrop; knows what each colour paints (so lobby icons and primary buttons stay visible) and that place names fit a lobby tile (14 characters); gets an art style guide (bold, filled, one palette) with a warning for sparse or thin art; and checks text on every room tint and the brand colour on the lobby tiles, so dim paths and washed-out buttons are fixed by the fix pass. The lobby art no longer covers the "+" add-place button and keeps its proportions. Checked with unit tests, the simulator and the office drivers; no real-model before/after run yet.
- **Page tabs in the session browser** (spec 080 US6, FR-120..FR-133, issue 910): each session's browser holds up to 10 tabs in a strip above the address bar, with + (Ctrl+T, ⌘T), close (x, Ctrl+W, ⌘W, Delete in the strip) and Ctrl+Tab / Ctrl+Shift+Tab. The bar's ⋯ menu has *Open chat links in a new tab* (off by default, app-wide): with it on a chat link opens a new tab, and a link a tab already shows focuses that tab without reloading it; the link menu has *Open in a new tab*. A page's same-site `target=_blank` link opens a tab (http(s) only, 5 per page load); the tabs share the session's private profile, which is wiped once when the session ends; background tabs are dropped first when more than four pages are loaded. A page that opens a tab never takes the keyboard focus from the composer or the chat: the new page gets it only when the page that asked had it. Main decides every tab and checks every tab id. Checked on the simulator (`check-browser-tabs` at 1440x900 and 1280x800, `check:office` with 10 tabs) and in `test:browsertabs`, `test:browseripc`, `test:browserviews`, `test:browserpane`, `test:navigator`; not on Windows or macOS, and ⌘W in the bar on a Mac is unchecked.
- **Notes: the `.durbar/notes.md` mirror in main** (spec 083 T041, issue 699): main can now write a folder's notes to `.durbar/notes.md` and read edits back (masked, marked *From the project file*, conflicts never overwritten), and add one escaped line to `.git/info/exclude` without running git. Nothing turns it on yet: every folder's switch reads as off until the Notes screen gets it (T042), so no project file is read or written. Checked with unit tests (`npm run test:notesfile`): links, junctions (Windows only), FIFOs, hard links, size caps, swaps between checks and the Windows rename retry.
- **Notes file format** (spec 083 T040, issue 699): the writer and reader for `.durbar/notes.md` that the project-file mirror and *Export…* will use. No visible change yet. Notes are kept in creation order, so a new note only adds lines at the end. A file with git conflict markers outside code blocks is refused. A note added by hand with a bare marker line or a `## ` heading is read as a new note. Text outside notes is reported. Checked by `npm run test:notesmd`: 1,000 seeded documents, hostile input and every size cap.
- **A Notes screen per folder** (spec 083 T034a, issue 699): *Notes (N)…* in the folder's `⋯` menu, *All notes of this folder…* in the Notes drawer and Ctrl+K *Notes of <folder>* open every note of the folder, newest first, with search, *+ Note*, *Edit…*, *Rename…*, *Copy*, *Delete…* and a folded *Done (N)* group; **Mark done** / **Mark not done** (also in the drawer) take a note out of the drawer, the `/` list and the header count and back. Nothing changes on the office floor. Checked on the simulator (`check-notes-screen`, `test-notesscreen`); *Export…*, *Delete all…* and *Also delete its notes* come next (T034b).

## v0.1.3 ([release](https://github.com/three-feathers/agent-durbar/releases/tag/v0.1.3), 2026-10-30)

- **Fix: lobby seats stuck out of their place tile in narrow windows** (spec 001 FR-189, issue #737): at 900 px wide and under, with several agents in one room and helpers, a place's count, icon or "+N" circle could be drawn outside its tile, and at 640 px the Postbox and the Add button were pushed out of the lobby. Below about 1000 px the Boss, corner, Bank and Postbox now shrink further so the five places keep 28 px each; a place under 56 px shows its icon and its "+N" circle only (name and count stay in its tooltip and label), and the Boss drops its wait from the tile under 150 px. Wider windows are unchanged. With 7 or 8 custom places at 640x480 a circle can still stick out. Checked on the simulator only (`npm run test:places`, `check:ui -- --only check-office-fill`, `check:office`).
- **Desks in one row keep one height when a task wraps to three lines** (spec 001 FR-190, #897): the task line now reserves its three lines (48.6 px), so a three-line task no longer makes its desk 0.6 px taller than the desks next to it.
- **`check:office` waits on conditions, not fixed pauses** (spec 001 FR-186, issue #854): the layout check no longer sleeps a fixed time after a resize, a click or a panel; it waits for the window size, the page state and a settled layout, with a clear message on a timeout. Test-only, no app change.
- A session resumed from Previous sessions no longer shows its title twice on the desk: the grey line says `Resumed. New messages continue this session.` and the title stays in the card's name (#451).
- **Instant tooltip on the top bar's status counts** (spec 001 FR-187, issue #875): hover a count (Working, Waiting, Idle, Error, Done) or Tab to it and the app's own tooltip appears at once, the same on Windows, macOS and Linux, naming the state and the count ("Idle · 7 agents idle or paused. Click to show only these.", "Click to show all." while that filter is on). Escape hides it; it stays below the count, inside the window, in every theme and in Serious mode, and is the count's accessible description. New shared helper `src/renderer/tooltip.js`; the other chips keep the system tooltip for now. Checked on the simulator only (`npm run test:tooltip`, `npm run check:ui -- --only check-status-tooltip`).
- **The status tooltip check copes with Windows runners losing focus** (spec 001 FR-187): `check-status-tooltip` now retries a hover, keyboard or redraw step only when the window really lost focus, was resized or scrolled (which rightly hides the tooltip), and fails at once with what it saw otherwise. Test-only, no app change.
- **Real macOS tests on pull requests that touch platform-sensitive files** (spec 008 FR-135, retro #854): `src/main/`, `src/adapters/`, test and shell scripts and dependencies now also run `npm test` on macOS before the merge; docs and UI changes do not. Not a required check. Checked by `test:uisuite` (the workflow file); the filter itself is proved on the first real pull requests.
- **Merge gate lists approvals apart** (spec 021, #883): `scripts/pr-gate.sh` prints a trusted approving review as `approved by: <login> at <sha>` instead of an unanswered comment; later comments, change requests and open threads still block, and outside text stays hidden. Checked with a fake `gh` test and a planted mutation.
- **Claim by assignee** (spec 021 FR-063, retro #854): `scripts/claim.sh <N>` claims an issue or the review of a PR with GitHub's assignee (refuses a taken item, the later of two racing claims backs off, `--release` gives up), so `taking #N` and `FINAL` comments are no longer needed; `checks run at <sha>` stays. Checked with a fake `gh` (`npm run test:claim`), not against real GitHub.
- **Unreferenced screenshots removed** (spec 021 FR-063, T044, #854): 79 pictures in `docs/screenshots/` that no document, page or driver refers to were deleted (about 15 MB less in a working tree; the git history is unchanged). Checked by `npm test` (`test:site`, `test:uisuite`).
- Process: the 18 one-release spec folders are one release table in spec 008, and a spec's index status is one fixed word (draft, specified, building, built, released, superseded) with a short note, checked by `npm run specs:check` (spec 021 FR-063, spec 008, spec 025).
- **Fewer `check:*` scripts** (spec 021 FR-063, T046, retro #854 B6): 30 standalone `npm run check:<name>` aliases for drivers that `scripts/ui-suite.js` already runs are removed. Run one driver with `npm run check:ui -- --only <driver>` (`-- --list` names them); `check:office`, `check:themes`, `check:geometry`, `check:perf`, `check:rename`, `check:mac-titlebar` and `check:fuses-launch` stay. README, design rules and driver headers updated. Checked with `npm run test:uisuite`, `test:site`, `specs:check` and `changelog:check`.
  New `npm run test:docsscripts` (in `npm test`) fails when an `npm run <name>` in README, CONTRIBUTING, AGENTS, `docs/` or a quickstart names a script missing from `package.json`.
- **Retro decisions recorded** (spec 021, FR-063): the owner-approved plan from the 2026-10-29 retro (#854) and the new operating rules are in the spec; no behaviour change.
- **Shorter rule files** (spec 021 FR-063, retro #854): `AGENTS.md` is the one rule text for agents, at about 2,990 words instead of 6,600; `CLAUDE.md` and the Copilot instructions only point to it; the agent notes are cut to the pitfalls still worth reading, and the checked facts about Copilot, Claude Code, Codex and git move to `docs/agent-apps.md`. `npm run rules:check` (part of `npm test`) keeps each rule file within a word budget. No change to the app.
- **Faster `npm test`** (spec 021, T041): `test:runtime` and `test:notes` are split into six suites that run in parallel (the old names stay as aliases); no test file was dropped. Development only, no behaviour change.
- **Weekly retro** (spec 021 FR-063, task T049): a scheduled workflow opens one "Weekly retro" issue every Monday with the week's facts (PRs, CI failures, red scheduled workflows, idle claims, rule-file sizes) for the agents to answer. No change to the app.
- **Codex mobile access guidance** (spec 054): ChatGPT mobile uses a connected desktop/SSH host and managed app-server; one read-only Durbar test session did not appear in the already-paired mobile app. The desktop UI was closed and remote-control state was not verified.
- **E2E real accounts paused** (spec 062 FR-009, #856): the daily scheduled run of `e2e-real.yml` was red from 2026-10-25 and spent credits, so the `schedule` trigger is off until the cause is fixed; it can still be run by hand. `test:platforms` checks it.
- **A sleeping Cheeky character in a nap bed reads like the couch seat** (spec 077 FR-071, issue #871): with Characters = Cheeky, the character in a bed is now the same head and shoulders as on the couch, upright on the pillow with the blanket over its lower edge, its name under it and its chat number on the top-left corner. A bunk with one bed has no posts and uses the whole column, so the bed is drawn at a sensible size. Other characters' beds only lose the posts of a one-bed column. Checked on the simulator at 1440x900 and 1280x800 (`check-crew`); two sleepers stacked at 1280x800 stay small (known gap).
- **Prehistoric Crew** (spec 077 FR-072, issue #815): a second value of Settings, Characters: six friendly cave people (a young bearded man, a woman with braids, a big bearded man, a curly ponytail, an inventor with round glasses and a shaman with feathers), each drawn working, waiting and resting like the Cheeky crew, on any theme. Each session gets one at random among those used least, kept per session; Cheeky and your saved choices are unchanged. Checked on the simulator at 1440x900 and 1280x800 (`check-crew`, `check-characters`); not run on Windows or macOS.
- **Simulator: crew characters fit the demo names** (spec 077 FR-073, issue #879): in the simulator, each demo agent wears a Cheeky or Prehistoric character that suits its name (Ada no longer turns up as a bearded caveman), the same every run, so the demo and its screenshots are stable. Real sessions keep the random pick and saved choices are unchanged. Checked with `test:crew` and the `check-crew` driver at 1440x900 and 1280x800.
- **Chat links open in the session browser** (spec 080 T001, T013, T017, T018, issue #556): a click on a link in a chat opens it in that session's Browser tab (asking the one-time note first; *Not now* sends it to your usual browser that time). Ctrl+click (⌘+click on a Mac) and the link's right-click menu (*Open in system browser*) use your usual browser, and *Settings > Open chat links in* switches the plain click. A mail link only offers *Open* and waits for your click (only a plain address with a subject, cc or bcc is accepted); any other kind of link is refused. Up to four pages stay loaded: the least recently shown others are dropped and reload when shown (*Reloaded to save memory*); a page you typed into or submitted a form from is never dropped. Checked on the simulator (its own test pages) on Linux; memory use was not measured.
- **Notes: save just a selection** (spec 083 T033, issue 699): select text inside one message and a small *Save to notes* button appears beside it (never over the message box); the message's `⋯` has *Save selection to notes* while a selection lies inside it. Only the selected text is saved, masked like every other note (known values and token shapes only: a fragment of a secret selected by hand is stored as selected), with a link to its message; a selection across messages, outside the chat log or in the read-only VS Code view shows no button; the button also hides when the selection is scrolled out of the log or the Notes drawer opens. Checked on the simulator (`npm run check:ui -- --only check-notes`); no real session, no screen reader.

## v0.1.2 ([release](https://github.com/three-feathers/agent-durbar/releases/tag/v0.1.2), 2026-10-29)

- **Desk pencil no longer covers the details column** (spec 001 FR-185, issue #850): on a wide desk card the rename pencil sat over the first letter of "Last tool" or "Last activity"; it now stays in the name's column. `check:office` tests the pencil against the card's details text, role and task line.
- Process: commits and PRs carry only the owner's identity (no AI co-author trailer or "generated with" line) and every commit is SSH-signed so GitHub shows Verified; setup in `AGENTS.md`, `docs/collaboration.md` and `docs/agent-setup/` (spec 021 FR-061).
- **A scheduled prompt can be 10,000 characters, and is never cut silently** (spec 044 FR-120..FR-125, issue #843, owner decision): the limit was 2,000 and a longer paste or a longer stored prompt lost its tail without a word. Now the prompt box keeps everything you paste; from 8,000 characters it shows a counter ("8,412 / 10,000"), and over the limit it says "12,345 of 10,000 characters: shorten it to save" (read out to screen readers) and **Save** stays off until it fits. The app refuses a longer prompt too. A stored prompt that is over the limit (an edited file) is kept whole, is **not sent**, and its row says "Needs review" with **Edit…**; the run log shows *not sent: the prompt is over 10,000 characters*. A once-only schedule over the limit is paused for review instead of being marked done (fixing it needs a future time), and a closed door refuses a held run ("not kept") once its file would pass about 3.5 MB. Checked with unit tests (`test:schedules`, `test:schedhist`: parser, save, runs, counter words, 50 schedules at the maximum) and the simulator driver `check:sched-edit` at 1440x900 and 1280x800; not tried with a real agent app (no 10,000-character prompt was sent to Copilot, Claude Code or Codex).
- **Floors: a lift panel and an All floors view** (spec 092 phase 2, FR-006..FR-008, FR-013..FR-017, FR-028, FR-031..FR-037; owner decision on issue #782): with two or more floors on, a lift panel at the right of the office has one numbered button per floor, with a badge counting the agents on other floors that need you (waiting, error, limit reached, New reply); its **All** button shows every floor on one screen as before phase 1, and the choice is remembered. Checked on the simulator (`shots-092-lift`, `check:office` with the panel and the All floors view at 1440x900 and 1280x800: no density lost) and with `test:floornav`; not tried with a screen reader.

## v0.1.1 ([release](https://github.com/three-feathers/agent-durbar/releases/tag/v0.1.1), 2026-10-28)

The first update after the first stable release, unsigned and not notarized like every build so far. Highlights: **List columns that follow their content and calmer room headers**, a readable folder name in every room header, a status pill that says what it hides, **Characters** as a Settings choice separate from themes (with the experimental Cheeky characters), a schedule's run history, **+ Folder** can create a new repository or an empty folder and clone a repository, one floor at a time when several floors are on, *Restart without the sandbox* for Copilot, a Running MCP server whose calls fail now says so, and release hardening (build provenance attestations, pinned Electron). Each entry names the spec and how it was checked; what was not run on a real Mac or Windows PC is written in the entry.

- **List columns follow their content; calmer room headers** (spec 001 FR-182..FR-184, issues #812, #813): in List, Agent app, Active, Usage and Status fit their text (with a cap), Agent and Folder keep more room so names show whole, Current task takes what is left and is the one that is cut, and Role no longer repeats the agent app ("no prompts · gpt-5.2" instead of "Copilot (no prompts) · gpt-5.2"); every cut cell shows its full text on hover and the row's screen-reader name keeps all of it, and a narrow window drops Role, then Folder, instead of scrolling sideways. In the office the rooms' bottom wall has no doorway notch any more, and the pencil in a room header is only an icon (a tile shows on hover or keyboard focus). Checked on the simulator at 1440x900, 1280x800, 1024x700 and 640x480 (`npm run check:listcols`).
- **The folder name in a room header is always readable** (specs 001 FR-179 and 059 FR-169, issue #788): the name keeps at least 8 characters (it was squeezed to two letters next to a floor tag, a schedule chip and MCP); when the header is tight the pencil, the schedule chip, MCP, the floor tag and the cost give way, one at a time, into the folder's ⋯ menu (which gains MCP extras and Cost); the folder pencil sits after the name, never over it; the name's tooltip shows the name and the path. Checked on the simulator at 1440x900 and 1280x800 (`npm run check:office`). Measured on Linux fonts only.
- **A status pill now says what it hides** (spec 001 FR-180, FR-181, issue #790): while the Working, Waiting, Idle, Error or Done pill filters, the office and List show "Showing only idle · 3 hidden · Show all" (Show all is the pill's own toggle; a screen reader is told once; hidden sessions that need you are named: "3 hidden (1 needs you)"), so a finished session the filter hides no longer looks lost, and the layout reserves no lounge width for the finished agents the filter hides. Checked on the simulator at 1440x900 and 1280x800 (`npm run check:statusfilter`); the cause in the reported session is not confirmed.
- **The chat title uses the free room in the header** (spec 052 FR-180, issue #789): the session title and the line under it (agent app and model) no longer stop at 40% of the row with a gap before the buttons; they are cut with an ellipsis only when the row is really full, and the full title stays in the tooltip. Checked on the simulator at 1440x900 and 1280x800 (`npm run check:chat-title`).
- CI: the nightly macOS UI job has 80 minutes instead of 45, so the whole driver suite can finish (it was cancelled two nights running).
- **Release notes name every agent app** (spec 008 FR-130): the intro of the release notes no longer says "Requires GitHub Copilot (copilot login)". It says at least one of GitHub Copilot, Claude Code or OpenAI Codex must be signed in, and that the project is not affiliated with GitHub, Microsoft, Anthropic or OpenAI.
- **Release hardening** (spec 008 FR-132..FR-134): release files will carry build provenance attestations you can check with `gh attestation verify` (README, *Verify a download*; first effective on the next release), the Copilot runtime downloaded at build time is checked against `package-lock.json`, and Electron and electron-builder are pinned to exact versions.
- **Test the Claude adapter against old and new Claude Code CLIs** (spec 012 FR-163, T136): `DURBAR_CLAUDE_BIN=<absolute path>` (developer/test hook, ignored in a packaged build) makes detection use that `claude` file first, and the opt-in `npm run test:claudecli` checks every flag the adapter can send against each installed CLI's `--help` (`--version` and `--help` only; no session, no credits). Checked on Claude Code 2.1.302, 2.1.303, 2.1.304 and 2.1.309; unit tests in `npm test` with a 2.1.309 help fixture, mutation-checked. Runtime behaviour is not covered.
- **Claude helpers show their text and thinking** (spec 015 FR-016, FR-017): Claude Code only sends a sub-agent's text and thinking when started with `--forward-subagent-text`, which Durbar never passed, so a helper card had tool chips and a result but no text. Durbar now passes it (SDK: `forwardSubagentText`) when the installed Claude Code is 2.1.211 or later, and never for an older or unreadable version. Tests only; not seen on a real session.
- **Dependabot waits 7 days** (spec 021 FR-062): version updates for npm and GitHub Actions are proposed only once a release is 7 days old; security updates are not delayed. `test:supplychain` requires the cooldown; mutation-checked.
- **Dependabot limited to minor and patch updates** (spec 021 FR-062): semver-major updates and Electron are ignored (a major is planned work with its own spec, an Electron bump updates the pinned hashes by hand); `test:supplychain` checks the config.
- **Supply-chain guards** (spec 021 FR-062): weekly grouped Dependabot updates for the npm packages and the GitHub Actions; `npm test` now fails on a lockfile entry outside the npm registry or without an integrity hash, on a committed executable or archive, and on a workflow action not pinned to a commit; the jobs that use CI accounts run in a `copilot` environment that the owner can protect with reviewers; a hardening checklist for the always-on agent host. Checked by the new `test:supplychain` suite, with planted bad inputs.
- **Agents read only the team's text** (spec 021 FR-060, contributors): comments, issues and PRs by people outside the team are not read by the team's coding agents but sent to a human as a link (prompt-injection guard). `scripts/pr-gate.sh` hides outside text and refuses outside PRs, and the new `scripts/gh-trusted.sh <N>` reads an issue or PR with outside text hidden. Checked by `test:trustedcontent` with planted payloads.
- **A schedule's run history, and "Schedules here (N)"** (spec 044 FR-036..FR-045, issue #624, owner request): every Durbar schedule's row, in the folder's dialog and in Settings > Schedules, has a **History** button that lists its last 50 runs under it, newest first, each in words with an icon (sent, sent with Run now, skipped because the agent was busy or paused, not sent at its limit or after its session ended, missed, could not send with the reason, held at a closed door, loop ended). A busy, paused or held skip that repeats is one line with a count ("4 times, until 08:45"). **Open chat** goes to the chat a run went to; a chat that is gone says *chat closed*. The schedules file is now saved safer (a private temp file, fsynced and renamed over it, a failed write kept and retried, a file that cannot be read is moved to `.bad-<time>` instead of being overwritten). The log is kept by the app next to the schedules (no prompt text, error text, path or token in it; old files load as they were; deleted with the schedule), and the window asks for one schedule's log only while its list is open. The folder's menu item and the Folders panel button now read **Schedules here (N)** and open the same folder dialog as before. Checked with unit tests (`test:schedules`, `test:schedhist`, `test:ipcchannels`) and the simulator driver `check:sched-history` at 1440x900 and 1280x800; not seen on a Mac or Windows, and a real session's id surviving a restart (for Open chat on older runs) is not verified.
- **Unlisted talk pages on the project site** (spec 050 FR-014): the site can carry hand-written talk pages that nothing on the site links to, marked noindex, with a strict same-origin CSP. The first is the LEVEL UP, ARCHITECT talk (Barcelona, 27 October 2026) with its word-cloud host page, an architects' notes page and its video. `test:site` checks they stay unlisted and self-contained.
- **Remote control stays user-only with the newer Copilot SDK** (spec 054, Dependabot #828): the SDK's `remoteSession` setting is gone in 1.0.26, so Durbar's `remoteSession: 'off'` is only a hint there; the real control is that remote control starts only from your confirmed *Control from GitHub* action. A test now pins that no start, resume or rename turns it on. Read from the published packages and fake-session tests only; not verified on a real session.
- **A Running MCP server whose calls fail now says so, and the MCP dialog reads the state again** (spec 058 FR-187..FR-192, issue #779 A and B): when a server reads *Running* but its last tool call was refused for a missing sign-in or by your organization's access policy (Entra Conditional Access, for example "the device must be managed"), the row reads "Running · last call needs sign-in" or "· last call blocked by your organization's access policy" with one hint, in the chat's MCP dialog and in Settings > MCP servers; a later successful call clears it. Only the class is shown, never the error text, claims, IDs or e-mail addresses. The dialog also reads the agent app's server list again when it opens (at most once a minute), and has a **Refresh** button (keyboard friendly); an agent app that cannot report its list again (Codex) says so. Checked on the simulator at 1440x900 and 1280x800 and with unit tests; not seen on a real agent app. Per-server logs in the app (part C) wait for an owner decision.
- **Characters, separate from themes** (spec 077 FR-064..FR-070, issue #814): Settings has a **Characters** choice next to **Theme**: *The theme's own* (the default), *Minimalist* or *Cheeky*, each with a preview, applied on top of any theme and kept with the other settings. Cheeky Crew is no longer a theme: a saved Cheeky Crew theme becomes Clean Office with Characters = Cheeky, once. Checked with unit tests and the simulator (`check:characters` on Retro Pixel and Corporate, `check:crew` and `check:office` with Cheeky on both, 1440x900 and 1280x800); not run on Windows or macOS by a person.
- **Cheeky characters** (spec 077, experimental): a **Characters** choice in Settings, on top of any theme (see the Characters entry), with 13 young, cheeky, office-safe characters (punk, gamer, hacker, rocker, buzzcut, bookworm, scientist, metalhead, bob-glasses, skater, surfer, disco, sporty), each with a working, waiting and resting picture shown by status. Each session gets one at random among the least used, kept per session. The status cues, moods and the Serious mode stay as in every theme; the art waves and holds a mug itself, so the code draws no hands over it; a missing picture falls back to the plain figure. The pictures are AI-generated (ChatGPT), reviewed and finished by a person, with their provenance in `src/renderer/crew/ASSETS.md`. Checked with `test:crew`, `test:crew-assets`, `test:scenekits`, `check:crew` and `check:office` (simulator, 1440x900 and 1280x800); Windows not run by a person (Paco looked at it on his Mac).
- **Cheeky Crew: big, clear portraits** (spec 077, owner feedback): the desk portrait fills the space left of the monitor (88 units; the monitor's status colour, the badge corner and the card text stay uncovered); the round avatars (lobby, room chips, chat header) crop the face, with a per-picture face point; a couch seat shows head and shoulders with the hair or hood uncut. At 1280x800 a couch seat is smaller than its chat-number badge, which covers it. Checked with `check:crew` (face visible for desk, helper, walker and seat at 1440x900 and 1280x800).
- **Cheeky Crew: idle portraits in full colour** (spec 077, owner decision): an idle desk, card or sub-agent keeps its character at full colour instead of washed out; the furniture and text stay dimmed, and idle still reads from the resting pose, the cup and the word "Idle". Other themes are unchanged. Checked with `check:crew`.
