- **Cheeky Crew: new art for 10 characters** (spec 077, owner decision): sporty, disco, skater, hacker, buzzcut, bookworm, metalhead, scientist, gamer and the surfer (now with a bigger head) have new pictures; punk, rocker and bob-glasses are unchanged. Face crops were re-read for the new pictures.
- **Cheeky Crew: the New and needs-you glow** (spec 077 FR-062, spec 001 FR-178): a crew desk with a new reply or one that needs you glows like every other, and its person zooms a little less (up to 15 percent) so the face never reaches the chat number.
- **Cheeky Crew: safer fallbacks** (spec 077 FR-063, review on PR 621): with the character module missing, the office draws the plain figure instead of failing; a closed session frees its character; and the chat header and inbox avatars switch to the plain figure when a picture fails to load.
- **Fix: nightly "E2E real accounts" install step** (spec 081, 2026-10-27): the E2E and install-check scripts now pass the installer download to the harness setup, so the official-installer installs no longer stop with "No network layer is set up". CI scripts only: the app already passed it. Checked with a unit test on a local server.
- **Claude Code older than 2.1.309 runs without the OS sandbox floor** (spec 087 FR-003a): that release closed two sandbox escapes (shell startup files outside the sandbox; the `--mcp-config` file writable from sandboxed commands). Below it the session still starts, the chat chip says the Claude Code is too old and to update it, and Durbar's own checks still apply.
- **Restart without the sandbox** (spec 087 FR-022, FR-022d, FR-022e, issue #806): a Copilot session switched from Read-only or Ask first to Allow all keeps its OS sandbox until a new session; the Allow all confirmation now says so, and *Allow all · Sandboxed (until a new session)* on the chat chip is a button, also *Restart without the sandbox* in the mode menu, that asks first (grants cleared, remote control off, waiting requests denied, MCP servers start with the next message, no AI credits) and continues the same conversation in a new Allow all session through the existing Restart session (nothing re-sent). A sign-in refresh, or End session, no longer keeps showing a floor the session no longer has. Claude Code and Codex never show it. Simulator and fakes only; not run on a real session.
- **Copilot sandbox switch probe** (spec 087 T066, K16, #806): `scripts/sandbox-floor-real.ts copilot <out> switch` checks whether Copilot's OS sandbox can be cleared in a running session (a control in Ask first, the switch to Allow all and `options.update`, then a narrow-back or a resume), with the session's tools limited to the shell and read back before any prompt, a spend gate before every prompt (at most 3, a 1 AIU stop, unknown usage stops), only the exact probe command allowed to run, and a curl/write baseline before spending. Linux and macOS only. Dev tool only; dry run checked. Not run on a real session yet: the VPS it was tried on has no bubblewrap, so 0 prompts were spent and K16 stays open.
- **Allow all now turns Copilot's OS sandbox off in the running session** (spec 087 FR-022c, T066, T072, issue #806): switching a Copilot session from Read-only or Ask first to Allow all lowers its sandbox in place, and the Allow all confirmation says "Copilot’s OS sandbox is also turned off for this session."; switching back re-sends it. A real probe on Linux (Copilot CLI 1.0.98, 3 prompts) showed the sandbox clears in place and comes back; Durbar's own switch is tested on the simulator and with fakes, and macOS and Windows are not verified. If Copilot refuses or does not answer, a short message says so, the sandbox is treated as still on and *Restart without the sandbox* stays offered; an answer that comes later is followed, and a switch back to a narrower mode sends nothing until it comes. The T066 probe's cleanup now also removes the empty `.git/hooks` and `.git/config` placeholders the sandbox leaves in its session folder.
- **Floors as places, step 1: one floor at a time** (spec 092 phase 1, issue 782): with two or more floors on, the office shows one floor's rooms and the shared lobby, each at the best density for its own agents (the 15-agent rule holds on each floor at 1440x900 and 1280x800). **PageDown** goes to the next floor that is on and **PageUp** to the previous one (Fn+Down / Fn+Up on a Mac laptop), instantly, once per press, announced as "Floor Client B, 2 of 3"; a floor that is taller than the window scrolls first and changes floor only from its edge; the keys keep their own meaning in text boxes, selects, menus and dialogs. The current floor is remembered; if it is switched off or deleted the view moves to the nearest floor that is on. A floor with nothing on it says so and keeps the lobby; a lobby visitor from another floor names its floor and does not walk. The status pills, the inbox, the title and the usage total still count the whole building. With one floor on nothing changes. Not yet: the lift panel with attention badges, the slide and "open an agent, land on its floor" (later steps of the spec). Checked on the simulator and with unit tests (`test:floornav`, `shots-092`, `check:office` per floor screen); not tried on a real Mac or Windows keyboard.
- **Create a folder from + Folder** (spec 093 phase 1, issue #805): **+ Folder** (and the welcome screen's add button) now asks *Add existing folder…* (first, so Enter keeps today's behaviour), *New repository…* (`git init`, on this computer only: no remote, no account) or *Empty folder…*. The place is picked in the system picker, which opens where the last folder was created; the name is checked by the app (one folder name that works on every OS: no `/` or `\`, `..`, Windows device names (also `CON .txt`, `CONIN$`, `COM0`), trailing dot or space, leading `-`, or `.git` in any spelling a Mac or Windows reads as `.git`) and an existing name is refused with the typed text kept. The new folder joins the office like an added one, in the top bar's mode (Read-only by default; *Allow all* asks first). `git init` runs through Durbar's one git module (a separate runner from the read-only status runner) with an argument list (no shell), a private empty hooks folder, fsmonitor off and every transport off; a failure removes the folder without following links. Ctrl+O, **File > Add Folder…** and the palette's *Add folder* still open the system picker directly; **File > New Folder…** (Mac) and the palette's *New repository* / *New empty folder* go straight to creating. Checked with the simulator at 1440x900 and 1280x800, fakes, and a real `git init` in tests (Linux; not run on macOS before merge).
- **Clone a repository from + Folder** (spec 093 phase 2, issue #805): **+ Folder**, **File > New Folder…** and the palette's *Clone a repository* take an `https://` or ssh address (`git@host:owner/repo.git`, `ssh://…`); anything else (`file://`, `ext::`, `git://`, `http://`, local paths, a leading `-`, a password or a token in the address, also percent-encoded) is refused with the reason and the text kept. The name starts as the repository's; a progress dialog shows git's phase, percent and last lines (masked), the time so far, a "still waiting" line after 30 s without output, and **Cancel** (Escape too), which ends only the git Durbar started and then removes the half-made folder ("Cancelled. Nothing was kept."). Quitting during a clone does the same. In *Allow all* the confirmation names the host ("This code comes from github.com; agents can run it without asking."). The clone uses your own git credential helper and ssh agent and never prompts (ssh in BatchMode, also added to a plain-ssh `core.sshCommand`); only https and ssh can be used, through two independent layers that hold even against a git config that allows other transports or rewrites the address; submodules are not cloned; no hook runs during the clone; your environment's `GIT_*` variables are dropped except absolute `GIT_SSL_CAINFO`/`GIT_SSL_CAPATH`. Checked with the simulator at 1440x900 and 1280x800, fakes, and real git in tests against a local bare repository, a silent local listener and an ssh stub (Linux; CI also on Windows). **No clone over the network was run**, credential-helper sign-in windows are not checked yet, and it was not run on macOS before merge.

## v0.1.0 ([release](https://github.com/three-feathers/agent-durbar/releases/tag/v0.1.0), 2026-10-26)

The first stable release, unsigned and not notarized like every build so far. Feature freeze since the owners' decision of 2026-10-26 (spec 021 FR-059, spec 091): every known user-impacting bug is fixed or written as a known limit (spec 090). Highlights: **New reply marker and desk glow**, **Notes** (save a message, a drawer, header count), a clearer approval card for "for this session", honest spending-limit and Safety-model wording, a stable-release path in the tooling, and fewer flaky tests.

- **Helper-card repaint check no longer flakes under load** (spec 001, FR-169, #763): `test:helpercard` compares the best of 3 CPU times against a same-run baseline as a ratio (limit 20, timer-tick floor) instead of a fixed millisecond limit. Test only; a repaint that cuts the 1 MB result again still fails it (ratio about 4800).
- **A finished agent's reply is marked "New" until you have seen it** (specs 001 and 005, issue #691): when an agent finishes while its chat is not shown, its desk, list row and chat-strip item get a **New** badge (dot and word, a green glow on the desk, "new reply not read yet" in its accessible name, one announcement, a single pulse only when motion is allowed). It is a marker, not a state: the counts, sort, filters and permissions do not change, and an unread agent stays on its desk instead of resting on the couch. It clears when its chat is shown or with **Mark as read** in the card menu (also Shift+F10 / the Menu key on a focused desk or list row, new); closing the chat or going back to the office does not. Not kept across a restart. Simulator-checked (`npm run check:unread`, 67 unit checks, 1440x900 and 1280x800); not run on real sessions.
- **Desks now glow, and the person asks for attention** (spec 001 FR-178, owner request): a desk with a New reply glows green, and a desk that needs you (permission, question, limit) glows amber-orange and wins over New. No border; the card never moves. The person at the desk zooms in and out (three bigger pulses when it starts, then a slower one) while it needs you or has a New reply; with reduced motion only the static glow shows. The New badge and the status words stay, so colour is never the only cue.
- **A Copilot custom agent's subtask no longer shows a raw host error** (spec 002 FR-075, #703): the runtime's "Standalone server does not support session effect 'custom_agent_prompt'" now reads *Custom agents aren’t supported in Durbar yet. Run this agent in Copilot directly.* The session carries on. Custom agents still do not run inside Durbar. Unit-tested with the exact error text; not reproduced on a real session.
- The Settings list *What Durbar does not do* and the README now say spending limits are soft by one call (the call already running can go a little over), instead of "checked between replies". The README *Safety model* also says that an MCP server's read-only mark is trusted, that masking is best effort, which prompts Durbar stores, and that on Linux without a keyring the agent apps keep their sign-ins in plain files.
- **The approval card says how far "for this session" reaches** (spec 003 FR-013, FR-108): a write grant covers every file in the folder, and *Allow for this session: any arguments* covers every simple command of that name (`git` also `git push`); a plain line above the buttons says so. Tested with a unit test and a simulator check; the grant itself is unchanged.
- **Release tooling handles a stable tag** (spec 008 FR-129): a tag such as `v1.2.3` makes a draft that is not a pre-release and, published with `gh release edit v1.2.3 --draft=false --latest` (printed in the job summary), is the latest release; alpha, beta, rc and test tags stay draft pre-releases, never latest. The decision is one tested function (`releaseKind`) that `release.yml` reads. A stable release's notes say "unsigned build" instead of "alpha", and `changelog:build` accepts a stable heading. Checked by `npm run test:changelog` with planted mutants; the workflow first ran on v0.1.0's own release.
- **Stopping a Codex or Claude session ends its helper processes on Windows** (spec 012 FR-079): the close and kill steps use `taskkill /t /f` instead of ending one process. POSIX is unchanged. Checked with a fake child only; not verified on Windows.
- **`test-redact` no longer flakes under load** (spec 012 FR-080 delta, issue #684): the adversarial 1 MB cases compare their CPU time with a 1/16-size input of the same shape, best of 3 (growth up to about n log n passes), instead of a fixed 1000 ms limit, so a busy machine cannot fail a linear `redact()`; a quadratic rule still fails (checked with mutants). Test only.
- **The weekly test audit script no longer needs `flock` or GNU `stat`** (spec 021 FR-058): on macOS it uses a `mkdir` lock and BSD `stat`, which fixes `npm test` on the macOS release build. Linux behaviour is unchanged.
- **Weekly test audit** (spec 021 FR-058, harness only): every Friday a system timer on the owner's VPS has Claude Code audit the tests on a fresh copy of `main` (read-only, mutation-checked) as its own unix user with no token and no view of the owner's home, then a separate filing unit validates the answer (strict JSON, no links) and files at most 5 new, de-duplicated issues. Unit templates and install steps (sudo, by the owner) in `docs/agent-setup/`. Checked with `test:weeklyaudit` and fakes only (no real session); user-unit sandboxing was probed and does not apply on that host, hence the separate user.
- **Floor switch-off timing check no longer flakes under load** (spec 033 SC-003, issue #454): `shots-033` compares the switch's own time with a same-run yardstick (ratio limit 16, sanity cap 2000 ms) instead of a fixed 500 ms, so a busy machine cannot fail it; a per-agent delay or a repeated layout search still fails (checked with mutants). Test only.
- **Smaller screenshots** (spec 050 FR-012, issue #759): the pictures in the README and on the website are now 256-colour palette PNGs, 63% smaller (the 76 linked pictures went from 15.6 MB to 5.7 MB, the site from about 15.0 to 5.6 MB). `npm run shots:docs` compresses every picture it writes, and `npm run shots:compress` does it by hand; a quality guard keeps the original when a picture would look worse. Plain Node, no new dependency. Checked with `npm run test:compressshots`, `test:site` and one simulator retake.
- The website's wording follows the latest release: a stable release says "Download" and "Install Agent Durbar" instead of "alpha" (a pre-release keeps the alpha wording).
- **Rename pencil on wide cards** (spec 059, issue #781): when the mode icon wraps below the session name, the pencil sits after the name instead of over its first letters. `check-office-overlap` also fails a pencil over the first half of a name.
- **Notes: header count, saved-message marker and drawer search** (spec 083 T030..T032, issue 699): the chat header shows **Notes N** when the folder has notes (tooltip: how many are from this session) and opens the drawer; a message you saved shows a small note icon (*Saved to notes: <title>*) that opens the drawer on its note and goes away when the note is deleted; the drawer has a search field (title first, then text, case-insensitive; Esc clears it). Everything shown from a note is masked first. Checked on the simulator (`npm run check:notes-count`, `check:office` with the count shown and the drawer open, 300 notes open and search in under 100 ms); no real session and no screen reader.
- **Notes: no more lost note when several Durbar processes take over a stale lock** (spec 083 FR-008, issue #723): only one process at a time may break a stale lock, a lock is released only if it is still the releasing process's, and a writer that lost its lock stops instead of writing; leftovers of a crash are swept. Tested with an injected file system and with four real processes on Linux. Not run on Windows or macOS: delete-pending errors on creating the lock, antivirus read errors, NTFS inode and time values, hard links on FAT/exFAT, and rename-aside behaviour.
- **Notes: save a message, the Notes drawer and notes in the `/` list** (spec 083 T020..T024, issue 699): every finished message has a **⋯** with **Save to notes**; a drawer over the chat log (More > Notes…, Ctrl+Shift+M / ⇧⌘M, Ctrl+K *Notes*) lists this session's and the folder's notes with **Insert** (into the message box, never sent), **Edit…**, **Rename…**, **Delete…** and **+ Note**; the `/` list offers notes after the commands, also in a chat whose agent app lists no commands. Notes are stored on this computer (0600) and masked first, and the window masks again. Checked on the simulator (`npm run check:notes`, `check:office` with the drawer open) and with unit tests; no real session and no screen reader. The notes count, the saved-message marker, search, selection save, the per-folder screen and the project file are the next slice.
- **Copilot's chip no longer says plain "Sandboxed" in Ask first** (spec 087 FR-021b, K15, #755): it reads *Ask first · Sandboxed (partial)*, because a command you approve can still write outside the session folder (seen on Linux, 2026-10-25). Claude Code, Codex and Read-only are unchanged. Tested with the unit test and the simulator; no real session was run.
- **Known limits in plain words for the first stable release** (README; by @rubenlupi, replaces #768): the Known limits section is rewritten in plain words; adds the very-narrow-window seat (#737), the Mac exit-127 fix and real-updater checks not yet run, the OS sandbox items not checked, the broad "for this session" approval scope (spec 003 FR-013) and that the desk person keeps moving until an in-app reduce-motion switch exists (#780). Docs only; checked with specs:check and the site test.

## v0.1.0-alpha.18 ([pre-release](https://github.com/three-feathers/agent-durbar/releases/tag/v0.1.0-alpha.18), 2026-10-25)

Cut on Vikram's standing OK for a pre-release about every 8 hours (version PR #757, spec 089). Highlights: **cabin doors with meeting-room signs** and a door you can close yourself (with held prompts), an **Office with cabins or Open office** layout switch, **helper cards** that show what a sub-agent does, **"Why was this allowed?"** on tool cards, an **OS sandbox** as a second lock under Read-only and Ask first (checked on Linux only), and fixes for long custom answers to questions. Unsigned and not notarized; not marked latest.

- **Helper cards show what a helper does** (spec 001, issue 697): with few agents (parent desks at least 1.4x grown), each sub-agent is a card with labelled **Task**, **Now**, **Result** or **Failed** and a facts line instead of a name-and-status tile; two levels by room, the small tile when there is none, 15 agents unchanged. A field the agent app did not send reads *not reported*, never another field; the text is masked, then cut to 240 characters, in the card, its tooltip and its accessible name. **Task, Result and Failed text is always on the floor when there is room; only Now follows Live activity on desks (no hide option, owner decision).** Also fixes a Claude Code helper (`Agent` / `Task`): its result reaches the card and no stray tool chip stays in the parent chat; an `update` no longer ends a helper. Simulator and unit tests only (no real session, 0 credits).
- **"Why was this allowed?" on tool cards** (spec 003 FR-099..FR-107, issue #605): a tool card that ran without asking shows a tag (Allowed: session rule or session rules, Allowed: Allow all, Allowed: folder read, Allowed: attached file); the open card says why in a sentence and, for a session rule, offers **Undo this rule** (the grant chip's own revoke, validated in main). Only what Durbar decided is named: the agent apps' own reasons (Claude Code settings or classifier, Codex, Copilot approvals) are not reported yet and show no tag. A write across several folders names, and Undo removes (by call id, in main), every rule that allowed it (up to 500 folders; beyond that the card says so); a read says "folder read" only when the broker checked the path against the session's folders. Unit tests on the real broker and planted mutants; simulator driver `check-why-allowed`; not run on a real session.
- The `check-ui-text` UI check now runs as two drivers at once (light and dark parts), so it no longer hits the suite's 600 s limit; the same surfaces, themes and sizes are checked (#738).
- **Cabin doors with meeting-room signs** (spec 017 FR-003..FR-014, owner request, issue #616): each session is an office cabin. While the agent works, must not be disturbed or is away, a closed door with its photo and a sign (Busy, In a meeting, Do not disturb, Away) is drawn in place of the desk; an agent waiting for you stands in front of its door and waves under the Waiting for you sign; when it is done the door stands open with a Done tag (Available when free, Alert on an error). The desk's status pill keeps its plain word. Derived from the normalized state (no new status system; the plain word stays in the tooltip, the accessible name and the list); a click on a closed door opens the chat; Settings > Detail level turns it off. Simulator-checked (`shots-017-signs`: door, photo, word, icon and colour per state, contrast of at least 3:1 in all six themes, 15 agents at 1440x900 and 1280x800).
- **Close a cabin door yourself** (spec 017 FR-015..FR-022, owner answers 2026-10-23; spec 044 FR-110..FR-112; spec 054 FR-040): a door button on each desk puts the agent in Do not disturb; its scheduled prompts are held at the door (not sent, not lost, kept across a restart) and remote control goes off; opening offers Send all (in order) or Discard, Keep closed by default. The spending limit has its own pose (an empty wallet, Limit reached, also in the lobby), a wait at the Boss waves, an error shows small flames; still with reduced motion. `test:doors`, `test:remote`, `shots-017-signs`. Remote messages cannot be queued by Durbar (they go into the agent app directly).
- **Held prompts, safely** (spec 017 FR-024, 044 FR-113, 054 FR-042, review on PR 631): Send all goes through the schedules (Pause all, a paused schedule and a schedule's own limit stop it; runs are credited), the dialog lists the held prompts (masked), an open door with held prompts shows the count, a full door says so, remote control turned off by a closed door is shown as off, and the doors file is written atomically and size-checked.
- **Office layout: Office with cabins or Open office** (spec 017 FR-027, owner 2026-10-24): Settings > Detail level > Office layout switches between the cabins (default) and the open office from before them. Switching to Open office with a door you closed asks first (Keep cabins, Open the doors, or Discard / Send all for held prompts), so held prompts are never hidden; remote control for an agent behind a closed door offers to open it. A door you closed says "DND" on every desk and in the desk's accessible name (FR-028), and an agent app compacting its conversation has its own **Compacting** sign (FR-026).
- **DORA evidence and review-ready preflight** (spec 021): clarify evidence-based release-incident and corrective-release records, and bring low-cost readiness checks earlier without weakening review or CI coverage.
- **Release-cut and label steps for DORA records** (spec 021): classify the release and record its tag on the incident issues; the `incident` label is tested; test builds are not deployments.
- **Simulator checks that nothing ran now run** (spec 021, issue #748): 13 drivers that asserted but were never started (checkpoints, Plan first, Codex refusal, session continue and remove, MCP elicitation, usage totals, hand-over, default agent, folder setup, VS Code import, credit cap, Tools) are in the UI suite, three stale ones were repaired, two one-off picture scripts removed, and `npm test` now fails on an asserting driver that is in neither the suite, `shots:docs` nor an explained skip list. The suite takes about 2.5 minutes longer.
- `scripts/pr-gate.sh` reads every comment and review of a long PR (it stopped at 30), so a ready PR is no longer reported as "not ready", and a plain comment no longer lifts a request for changes (spec 021 FR-055).
- **Theme CSS check is an allow-list** (spec 035 FR-015, issue #747): `test:theme` now accepts only listed paint properties in `themes.css`, so `flex`, `transform: scale`, `top`/`left`, an unknown property or a length in a custom property fail, not only `width`/`padding`. Checked by planting each case in a copy of `themes.css`; test-only, no behaviour change.
- **A custom answer to a long question is an answer, not a skip** (spec 052 FR-178, issue #689): an answer to a question of more than 300 characters was dropped before it reached the agent, so the card showed "skipped" and the agent was told you skipped. An answer that cannot be read is now refused with a message and the card stays open. Unit test and simulator check; not run on a real session.
- **A skipped question says why** (spec 052 FR-178, issue #689): the card's tooltip and the debug log name the path that ended it (you pressed Skip, the turn was stopped, the agent app ended it), by code only, never the question or an answer. Added to find out why some answers were recorded as skipped; that cause is not found yet.
- **Question cards stay true** (spec 052 FR-178, issue #689): several open questions are answered each on its own card; a card that said "answered" while the agent app was told "skipped" is corrected to skipped; a blank answer is refused instead of sent.
- **An answer over 500 characters is refused, not cut** (spec 052 FR-178, issue #689): the answer boxes and the app use one limit; several choices plus a typed answer that together are too long say so and keep the question open.
- **OS sandbox under Read-only and Ask first** (spec 087, issue #611): Durbar now also turns on each agent app's own OS sandbox as a second lock under its own checks: Claude Code's bash sandbox (a `sandbox` block with `failIfUnavailable`, no auto-approve, no unsandboxed retry, home-folder reads limited to the session's folders, network off) and Copilot's local sandbox (`sandboxConfig` with no dev-tool write grants, no bypass, no credentials, network off; local MCP servers inside it); Codex keeps its read-limited profile and its Ask first cards now say an approved command may run outside its sandbox. The mode chip says *Sandboxed*, *Sandbox: checking* or *Not sandboxed* (and why) after the mode; when the sandbox cannot start nothing is sent and the chat offers *Continue without the OS sandbox* or *Change mode…* (*Always continue on this computer* only for Claude Code on native Windows, undone in Settings > Agent apps, which shows each app's sandbox line). Simulator and fakes only: not verified on a real session (the tooltips say so).
- **OS sandbox floor checked on Linux** (spec 087 T060): real prompts, owner-approved. Claude Code's sandbox refused a Read-only `touch` in the folder ("Read-only file system"), so its tooltip on Linux Read-only no longer says "not verified on a real session" (Ask first still does). Copilot's sandbox was on, but Durbar's own Read-only refused the command first; in Ask first a Copilot command the person approved wrote a file outside the session folder: it ran inside Copilot's sandbox, whose policy allows one more writable place than Durbar asked for (K15). macOS and Windows are still unchecked.
- **Copilot Ask first approval cards say the sandbox may not hold** (spec 087 FR-021a, K15): a Copilot shell command card shows *If you approve, Copilot’s sandbox may still let this write outside the folder (seen on Linux, 2026-10-25).* on its own line, and the chip tooltip says *writes: not only this folder*. Checked with tests and the simulator.

## v0.1.0-alpha.17 ([pre-release](https://github.com/three-feathers/agent-durbar/releases/tag/v0.1.0-alpha.17), 2026-10-25)

Cut on Vikram's standing OK for a pre-release about every 8 hours (version PR #739, spec 088). Highlights: **organization policy greying** of permission modes, models and MCP tools, the **session Browser tab**, **Compacting on the desk**, an **end-of-session summary card**, git calls that never run a command from a folder's own config, and the Mac "exit 127" fix for the Copilot CLI check. Unsigned and not notarized; not marked latest.

- **Nightly macOS navigator check** (specs 008 and 074): make the simulator test macOS native text-edit shortcuts without assuming Electron simulates Cocoa editing; verify the app does not intercept them. Targeted navigator driver passed locally on macOS.
- Bound agent version parsing (spec 012) to the first 200 characters of the first line, reject error prose and oversized tokens, and preserve Codex version prefixes and build metadata handling (offline regression tests; no real agent sessions).
- **Compacting on the desk** (spec 015, issue #601): while an agent app summarises a long conversation, the agent's desk, list row, chat header and strip say **Compacting** (*Compacting the conversation…*), and the chat leaves a line where history was summarised. Claude Code (`status` and `compact_boundary`), Codex (`contextCompaction` item) and Copilot's `session.truncation` (older messages dropped, worded as such, never "compacted") now give the same normalized event Copilot's compaction already did. After a compaction whose new size the app does not report (Codex, and Claude when it sends no `post_tokens`) the context ring says it waits for the next message instead of the old figure, and the "nearly full" note does not fire on it. The gauge maths now treats `NaN`, `Infinity` (as the size in use or the window), strings and absurd sizes as unknown. Simulator and unit tests only (`test:compaction`, driver `shots-015-compact`); the Claude, Codex and truncation shapes are read from documentation and types, not seen on a real session.
- **Folder status never runs a command from the folder's own git config** (spec 063 FR-008, #719; found by Ruben's agent in the review of #718). Git honours a folder's `.git/config`, so a planted `core.fsmonitor`, filter driver (`clean`/`process`), external diff or textconv, or the `core.sshCommand` of a partial clone's lazy fetch could run while Durbar read the branch and changed files. Every git call now goes through one runner that allows only `status`, `diff`, `rev-parse` and `rev-list`, accepts only the options the app itself uses, turns those settings off on the command line (`-c`, `--no-ext-diff --no-textconv`, no transports, no lazy fetch), switches off every filter driver (the user's own too: git-lfs runs commands from the folder's config, so a touched git-lfs or git-crypt file can show as changed while `git status` says clean), passes none of Durbar's own `GIT_*` variables to git, and does not enter submodules (a moved submodule commit still shows as changed; a submodule with uncommitted work inside no longer does). Tested with real git 2.43 on Linux and through Windows CI; not verified on macOS.
- **Your company's agent-app policy, before a session fails** (spec 076 section O, issue #602): Durbar reads the agent apps' own organization policies, read-only. That means Claude Code's `managed-settings.json` and its drop-ins, Copilot's policy through the Copilot runtime (with its `managed-settings.json` as a fallback), and Codex's `requirements.toml`. It greys out the permission modes, models, MCP servers and MCP tools they block, with "Blocked by your organization's <app> policy", and main refuses them at start and on a switch. Each key is read the way the vendor documents it; what Durbar cannot read that way is shown as advice and stays selectable. Policy files are read in linear time within size caps (over a cap the area is advice, never read as permissive), and a file whose text did not change is not parsed again. Fixture tests with a hostile-input matrix, a fake Copilot runtime and a simulator driver; not checked on a real managed machine.
- **Session browser: the Browser tab** (spec 080 T012, T014, T015, T016, T018, T019, issue #556): a chat now has Chat and Browser tabs. The first time you open a Browser tab, Durbar asks once (*Not now* / *Open the browser*) and remembers the answer. The tab has an address field (web addresses only; `file:`, `javascript:` and addresses with a user name are refused with a reason), Back, Forward, Reload and Stop, a Sound on/off toggle (sound starts off) and *Open in system browser*; each session's page lives in its own throwaway profile and is shown only on its own Browser tab. Menus, dialogs, toasts and the slash list that open over the page win: the page shows as a still picture behind them. A page's `beforeunload` shows Durbar's *Leave this page?* box (Stay focused); a page dialog in a session whose chat is not showing raises a toast naming the session, brings that chat forward once and marks it *Dialog open*. Checked on the simulator (its own test pages under `durbar-sim:`) on Linux; not yet on a real desktop, Windows or macOS. Chat links and the settings line for them come with T017.
- **Copilot CLI "Could not check: exit 127" on a Mac** (spec 081 FR-042): an app started from the Dock has no `node` on its PATH, so npm (found in nvm, Volta or Homebrew) could not start. The npm check, the npm update and sign-in runs now put the program's own folder first on that command's PATH (only when the folder is not writable by everyone), a missing npm or node says "npm or node was not found" with what to do instead of a number, the card's buttons are named *Check again* and *Check for update*, and "Found at" shows the full path of a system install. Unit-tested with fakes; not verified on a real Mac.
- **End-of-session summary card** (spec 085, issue #604): ending a session adds one line to its chat: files changed and +/− lines (read-only `git diff --shortstat` against the commit the session started from, argument array, no shell), new files, commits, duration and the session's own cost ("cost not reported" when missing), with **Show uncommitted changes**. Honest limits: non-git folders, folders git could not be read in and folders that already had changes say so; pull requests only when an agent app reports them (none yet); not on the lounge bubble or the briefing yet. Checked with the simulator (`simsummary=1`), `npm run test:sessionsummary` and `scripts/check-session-summary.js` at 1440x900 and 1280x800.

## v0.1.0-alpha.16 ([pre-release](https://github.com/three-feathers/agent-durbar/releases/tag/v0.1.0-alpha.16), 2026-10-25)

Cut on Vikram's standing OK for a pre-release about every 8 hours (version PR #725, spec 086). Highlights: **resume of sessions interrupted by a crash**, a **Schedules button** in the top bar, **rename in place**, **agent-app updates** in Settings, the coffee-break Retry fix, and masking of chat-tool tokens and PowerShell hashtables. Unsigned and not notarized; not marked latest.

- **Resume interrupted sessions after a crash** (spec 006 FR-038..FR-046, issue #606): if Durbar stopped unexpectedly (crash, kill, power loss) while sessions were working, the next start shows one card, "N sessions were interrupted", with **Resume all**, **Pick…** and **Dismiss**. Resume uses the existing resume (each session's own mode and cap, its earlier chat shown, no prompt sent); the chat says the unfinished reply is lost and nothing was sent again. Nothing resumes by itself. A small marker file of session ids per Durbar process (mode 0600, no content) is removed on a normal exit, and a second Durbar running on the same profile is not taken for a crash. In a lounge corner made short by the strips, a resting agent's chat number no longer covers its name. Unit tests (`test:crashmarker`, with mutations) and a driver at 1440x900 and 1280x800 on the simulator (`DURBAR_SIM_CRASH=3`); no real crash run, not checked on macOS or Windows.
- **`check:themes` no longer hangs on macOS** (spec 008 FR-125/FR-126, #670): available memory there counts free, inactive and speculative pages from `vm_stat` (not free pages only), and a parallel worker that has waited 120 s for memory starts its job only when no other job is running, so none waits forever and the load stays at one job under low memory. Ran on Paco's Mac (14/14 shards, vm_stat 2848 MB vs freemem 186 MB); the 120 s fallback was not exercised there (unit tests only).
- **Chat-tool secrets are masked** (spec 012 FR-080, issue #551): Slack tokens and incoming webhooks, Discord webhooks and Telegram bot tokens are hidden wherever Durbar masks secrets (debug log, desks, chat, folder setup), also after a quoted-printable `=3D` or `%u003D` escape. Unit tests with fake values, false-positive and 1 MB adversarial cases.
- Tests: the edit-masking and MCP-output-masking speed checks measure CPU time against a baseline from the same run, so a loaded parallel run no longer fails them (specs 005, 019; test only).
- Tests: the OAuth-masking linearity check takes the best of three runs per size, so one GC pause or busy core cannot fail it (spec 058; test only).
- **Process: test once, then trust the record** (team decision 2026-10-22). CI is the source of truth for the full suites. Reviewers read CI for the exact sha and run locally only mutations, probes and paths CI can't cover, and agents post `reviewing at <sha>` and `checks run at <sha>` as PR comments; `scripts/pr-gate.sh` no longer counts those one-line notes as asks but still prints them as status notes, a review round is split with `taking #N` in the team chat, and a `reviewing at` note expires after 1 hour (AGENTS.md, docs/collaboration.md, docs/agent-notes.md).
- **PowerShell hashtables are masked** (spec 041 FR-053 R12, issue #502): a secret member of `@{"X-Api-Key"="..."}` (also in `powershell -Command`) is shown as `[redacted]` in MCP server lists and read tools. Unit tests with false-positive and adversarial cases (1 MB without spaces, 50k with spaces).
- **A Schedules button in the top bar** (spec 044 FR-027..FR-035, issue #523; owner's idea: scheduled prompts were hard to find). A clock with the number of active schedules, left of **Needs you** (the word shows from 1920px, 2040px on a Mac, and in Simple); it opens **Settings > Schedules** at its heading, from the office, the list, a chat (**Ctrl+Shift+S**, ⇧⌘S on a Mac) and **Ctrl+K**. A small **!** on the clock shows a run that was missed or failed since you last looked (done one-time schedules included; busy and paused skips never), and a pause mark shows **Pause all**. Hidden in the demo and in an empty office. Pure logic in `schedtop.js` (`npm run test:schedtop`); `npm run check:sched-top` at 1440x900 and 1280x800; the top bar still fits on one row at both sizes (room made at 1280 as measured in `docs/design-rules.md`). Simulator and unit tests only; not tried on a real Mac or Windows. Known gap: with the Mac title-bar room forced at 1280x800 in Expert the top bar still wraps (it already did before this change).
- **Constrain the manual MCP OAuth check to GitHub-hosted runners** (spec 058): dispatch names the original Windows + Ubuntu pair explicitly or offers one fixed runner, never a self-hosted label; YAML and selection mapping checked locally, workflow not dispatched.
- **Rename in place check steadier on Windows** (spec 059, issue #715): the focused-pencil step of `check-rename-inline` gives the window focus and reads the pencil's final opacity, not a mid-transition value. No change to the app.
- **Rename in place** (spec 059, issues #636 and #595): click the chat title, or use the pencil next to a name on a desk card, a list row, a Previous sessions row or a folder (room header and Folders panel), or press **F2** on a focused card, row, door or title, to edit the name where it is. Enter or clicking away saves, Esc cancels, empty brings the default name back; the Rename… menu items and the dialog stay. Same checks and calls as the dialog (no new IPC). Checked on the simulator (`npm run check:rename-inline`, `check:ui-text`, `check:office`, `test:inlinename`), not on a real Mac or Windows.
- **Folder names follow the session-name rule** (spec 059 FR-028, FR-029): a folder name with a control or hidden character (bidi override, zero width) or that looks like a secret is refused with the reason, in the inline field, the dialog and main (it used to reset the name silently); the shortcut sheet says Fn+F2 on a Mac.
- **Coffee break: Retry while the limit is still active no longer ends in Error** (spec 068 FR-025, #671): Claude Code answers such a Retry with the limit as words only ("You've hit your weekly limit · resets 2am"); Durbar now reads that as a coffee break again, keeps or refreshes the reset time, says in the chat that the resume found the limit still active, and sends nothing more by itself. Checked by unit tests and the simulator driver; the exact Claude wording comes from an owner report, not from a captured real run.
- **Session browser groundwork** (spec 080, issue #556): address rules for the coming per-session Browser tab (web addresses only, only `mailto:` leaves the app, loopback detection) and a simulator probe of Electron's view behaviour, recorded in the spec. No visible change yet.
- **Session browser: per-session view module** (spec 080 T012/T013, issue #556): each session's future browser view gets its own throwaway profile, a hardened set of handlers (no permissions, devices, client certificates, popups or downloads), a Chrome user agent, sound muted and a checked path for every navigation; torn down at quit. Not wired to any window yet, so no visible change.
- **Agent-app updates** (spec 081, issue #662): Settings > Agent apps shows the installed version, the install source and the latest version that source offers; Durbar checks once at every start in the background (a switch, on by default; organization keys turn it off), a new version gets a badge and one toast, and **Update…** runs the updater of the source that installed the copy (npm and winget pinned to the checked version, Homebrew, `claude update`, `copilot update`, and for the official Codex CLI `codex update` with a plain-words confirmation, otherwise the checked installer), never as root and never by itself. Open sessions are warned about (wait or end them); failures say what to do. Simulator and fake tools only: not run on a real Mac or Windows PC yet.
- **Notes store, foundation** (spec 083 T010-T014, issue 699): no visible change yet. Main gets the per-folder notes store (private files, 0700/0600, masked before they are written, atomic writes under a lock file, a file it cannot read is never overwritten), window-only notes calls and the simulator's in-memory notes; the Notes drawer and the rest of the spec come next. Checked with unit tests, two real processes writing at once and the simulator; nothing was run on Windows or macOS.
- Notes: the test helper retries a *busy* answer (up to 30 times), as a user pressing the button again would, so the four-process stale-lock test no longer depends on the Windows runner's speed (spec 083 FR-008 test; no product change).

## v0.1.0-alpha.15 ([pre-release](https://github.com/three-feathers/agent-durbar/releases/tag/v0.1.0-alpha.15), 2026-10-24)

The first release made with fold-at-tag (spec 008 FR-117..FR-120): a version-only PR (#672) landed, the tag on its merge commit wrote these notes, and this fold PR came after (spec 082; cut by Paco's agent on Vikram's OK while his agent was paused). Highlights: Copilot's **Auto routing tier**, **Codex on macOS** working in Read-only and Ask first again, a **Retrying** state while the agent app retries a model call, the **Codex coffee break** for its usage limit, clickable coffee-break agents, and two security updates (**Electron 44.9.1**, **zod 4.6.6**). Not checked by the agent on macOS or Windows; the packages stay unsigned and not notarized.

- **Auto routing tier for Copilot** (spec 007 FR-012..FR-018, issue #607): when a Copilot agent is on Auto, the model menu offers Efficiency, Balance or Intelligence, remembered per agent and used again after a restart; a higher tier asks first; a tier locked by the organization's Copilot policy is shown disabled; Undo restores the agent's own choice; a tier switch Copilot could not apply is reported with the tier still in use. Checked on the simulator and with unit tests; Balance and Intelligence were not run on a real account.
- **Release notes come from the tag** (spec 008 FR-117..FR-120, owner-approved): a tag on `main` writes the draft's notes from exactly that commit's `changelog.d/` fragments, so the notes no longer go stale while the merge queue keeps landing PRs, and no merge freeze is needed. The release workflow refuses a tag that is not on `main`, does not match `package.json`, or comes before the previous release was folded. The draft is always a pre-release, never *latest*. The version lands first in its own PR, and the fold into `CHANGELOG.md` is a doc-only PR from the tag afterwards (`changelog.d/README.md`). Checked by unit tests on throwaway git repositories and a local dry run; the workflow itself has not run on GitHub yet.
- **Test builds from branches** (spec 008 FR-121..FR-124, owner decision, #658): a tag such as `v0.1.0-alpha.15-test.1` may be on any branch and builds a draft pre-release, never *latest*, titled "(test build)". Its notes list the branch's commits that are not on `main` (subject lines, masked and shown as code, at most 100) and the PRs they name; nothing goes into `CHANGELOG.md` or the README, and a test build never counts as the previous release. Regular tags must still be on `main`. Checked by unit tests on throwaway git repositories and a local dry run; the workflow has not run on GitHub with a test tag yet.
- **Codex on macOS works in Read-only and Ask first again** (spec 012 FR-162, issue #660): Codex reached through a link (Homebrew's `/opt/homebrew/bin/codex`, the official installer's `~/.local/bin/codex`) could not re-run itself inside its sandbox (`sandbox-exec: execvp() … Operation not permitted`, openai/codex#41493). Durbar now starts Codex from its real file, without widening what Codex may read, and the macOS message tells this case apart from a sandbox inside a sandbox. Unit tests; cause confirmed on a Mac with a 0-credit `codex debug prompt-input` check, full session not re-run.
- **Team rules from the chat recorded** (spec 021 FR-050): review SLA (about 30 minutes, `taking #N`), `FINAL` settles ownership, release `freeze` / `queue open`, never check out a branch in the main checkout, delete a branch only once its PR is `MERGED`. Docs only.
- **Retrying** (spec 034, issue #600): when the agent app retries a failed model call by itself (Claude Code's `api_retry`, Codex's retried request errors, Copilot's failed model calls), the card's pill says *Retrying*, the desk and the pill's tooltip say *Retrying (attempt 3 of 10) – servers busy (HTTP 529)*, and the chat has one note that follows the attempts and then says the request went through. When the retries run out the turn's error says *Gave up after 4 retries – rate limit (HTTP 429)*. Only a fixed reason and numbers leave the adapter, never the provider's message. Simulator (`simretry=1`, `scripts/shots-034-retry.js`) and unit tests (`test:retry`); not seen on a real session.
- **Answer bar: long names end in an ellipsis** (spec 052 FR-177, issue #540): a long session name above the message box is cut with `…` and shows the full name on hover; "asks:" stays readable. Checked by `check:ui-text` on the simulator.
- **Opening a chat never scrolls the window** (spec 052 FR-176, issue #638): showing a waiting question (*Show question*, opening a chat at its request), answering or skipping it and focusing the message box no longer push the chat header off the top and leave a blank band at the bottom. Every focus and scroll into view in the app (chat, Settings, dialogs, profile editor) now moves only its own inner scroller through one shared helper. A safety net puts the page back if anything scrolls it anyway, and an office too big for the window still scrolls. Checked by `test:innerscroll` and the simulator driver `check:chat-no-scroll` at 1440x900, 1280x800, 1280x520 and 1000x560; not yet run on a real Mac.
- Tests: the OAuth-masking speed check no longer fails on a fast Windows run (a 0 ms baseline met a 40 ms floor); the floor is now 250 ms, still far below what a backtracking pattern would take.
- **Codex usage limit now gives the coffee break** (spec 068, #646): Codex spells the error `usageLimitExceeded`, the code compared a capitalised form, so a real Codex limit showed as a plain error. Both spellings are accepted; tests use the real one. Not checked on a real Codex account.
- **Coffee-break driver passes at any time of day** (spec 068, test-only): `shots-068-coffee.js` accepts the weekday form ("until Sat 12:01 AM") the reset time takes after midnight; `test:coffeebreak` checks the next-day words and the driver's patterns against `whenText`.
- **A coffee-break agent is clickable** (spec 068, owner request, #634): its entry in the coffee corner is a button that opens its chat (click, Enter or Space), and right-click or Shift+F10 offers **Open chat** and **Retry now**; the tooltip says the time left ("back in 4 min"). The chat shows a calm bar "On a coffee break · back in N min" with **Retry now**. *Try now* is renamed **Retry now**: it sends the same single message an automatic resume sends, with the session's own mode and spending limit, and a double press sends it once. Unit tests and the simulator driver `shots-068-coffee.js` (click, keys, menu, double press) at 1440x900 and 1280x800.
- Electron 44.8.0 → 44.9.1 (security: blocks compromised eMudhra intermediate certificates in Chromium networking and Node TLS, GHSA-jr5w-w6x2-84p9; Dependabot PR 659).
- **Security: zod 4.6.6** (Dependabot alert 2, high): zod below 4.6.6 could exhaust memory validating oversized constrained collections. `@github/copilot-sdk` 1.0.22 pins zod 4.3.6, so an `overrides` entry in `package.json` lifts it to 4.6.6. Remove the override once `@github/copilot-sdk` itself depends on zod 4.6.6 or later (tracked in issue #656). No behaviour change; typecheck, build, `npm test` (111 suites, including the Copilot adapter and runtime tests) and `npm run dist:dir` pass.

## v0.1.0-alpha.14 ([pre-release](https://github.com/three-feathers/agent-durbar/releases/tag/v0.1.0-alpha.14), 2026-10-23)

The MCP release, cut from `main` on owner request (spec 079) once the linked MCP pull requests had merged: **Sign in** to a Copilot MCP server from the chat's MCP dialog (#598; Claude Code servers too, #635), **MCP health** (each failed server says why and when, **Copy diagnostics** copies a masked summary, and the composer's MCP chip shows a red dot; #630), and **fewer macOS keychain prompts** (the Copilot runtime keeps GitHub's signature; #625). On a Mac, after updating you may need to press *Always Allow* on two launches, then no more prompts. It also carries the compact row under the message box and its resize, Read-only refusals that open the mode menu, Move to floor, the signed-in account per agent app, VS Code prompt files in the `/` list, token masking in the `/` list, Go to keys, rooms that use spare height, and masking and layout fixes. Unsigned and not notarized, as before.

- **The office spreads into spare height** (spec 001 FR-086..FR-088, owner report, issue #619): with few rooms and a tall enough window, the rooms first grow (up to 20%) and the rest becomes even gaps above, between and below the rows and the lobby, instead of leaving it all empty at the bottom (1440x900 with 4 one-agent rooms: 158 px at the bottom before, four 15 px gaps now). `check:office-fill` measures it in every layout that fits; simulator only.
- **Room headers keep their chips when a siren or cost arrives late** (spec 001 FR-023): the office is laid out again when a room header gains a part after the layout (siren, cost or MCP chip) and no longer holds it; this fixes the intermittent `check:office` failure "header part room-mcp sticks out" in the merge queue. Checked with a new simulator case (late siren) and its mutation.
- **A finished agent back from the Boss rolls into the lounge at once with reduced motion** (spec 001 FR-051): with reduced motion its figure was dropped without a walk and nothing drew the office, so the agent kept its empty chair for several seconds; this also fixes the Windows-only `check-office-fill` failure "Mira, Jonas and Noor are not resting before the checks". Checked with a new reduced-motion case and its mutation.
- **Screenshots match the app again** (spec 001 FR-082..FR-084): the README, website gallery and landing page pictures are retaken on the simulator after the lobby (Boss, Bank, Postbox), grown desks, chat numbers and lounge changes. `npm run shots:docs` retakes every linked screenshot in one command, with a pinned clock, time zone and locale; `npm test` fails when a linked picture has no driver. UI PRs now retake every linked picture that shows what they changed. No screenshot catches a lobby figure mid-walk any more (shared wait in the driver helpers). The website gallery's theme picture is now the theme picker with a built-in theme (issue #573).
- **Read-only refusals open the mode menu** (spec 003 FR-094..FR-098, owner request, #618): when Read-only stops an edit or a command, the chat (and its answer bar and Needs you) offers **Switch to Ask first…**, which opens the usual mode menu with Read-only, Ask first and Allow all (the current one ticked; Allow all still asks to confirm). After a switch the chat says to ask the agent again; nothing is resent. Checked on the simulator (`check-ro-ask`, mouse and keyboard) at 1440x900 and 1280x800.
- **A compact row under the message box** (spec 005, issue #627, owner request): the model chip shows only the model's name (effort and the context window choices are in its menu, only those the model has); the context is a small ring coloured by how full the window is, and a click opens a VS Code-style token summary with one bar per type the agent app reports (Copilot: system instructions, tool definitions, chat messages; Claude Code and Codex report only a total, and the summary says so) and **Compact**; MCP shows `12/16` (servers on of all) and stays in the row. `test:ctxbreakdown` (each adapter's mapper with fake events) and `check:composer-toolbar` (both sizes, 760px and 480px panels, six themes and a dark user theme); simulator only.
- **The message box grows and can be resized** (spec 005 FR-044..FR-049, issue #592): a long prompt grows the box up to 40% of the chat panel instead of stopping at 110px, and the transcript always keeps 140px visible. A thin handle on its top edge resizes it by mouse or keyboard (arrows, Page Up/Down, Home/End); double-click or Enter goes back to automatic growth; the chosen height is remembered for the whole app. Checked with `test:composersize` and `check:composer-toolbar` (simulator, 1440x900 and 1280x800).
- **Nightly watch no longer floods the Actions list** (spec 008, FR-116): `nightly-watch.yml` only starts for runs of `main`, not for every PR and merge-queue run (it was skipped anyway). Checked by YAML parse; the effect shows after the merge.
- **Team agent setup** (spec 021, FR-044): new `docs/agent-setup/` with the recommended Claude Code setup (sub-agents, `CLAUDE.local.md` and global templates, the review `/loop` and its conventions, an always-on VPS guide with example files). Docs only, no personal data; checked with `npm run specs:check`, `changelog:check` and `test:site`.
- **Label backfill applied** (spec 021 T022): every open issue and PR, plus those closed in the last 30 days, now has a type, an area and its `spec:NNN` labels; 16 items that need a human are listed on #633.
- **One label taxonomy for issues and PRs** (spec 021 FR-045, FR-046, team decision 2026-10-23): one type, at least one `area:*` (new `area:security`, `area:schedules`) and `spec:NNN` per spec touched; written in `AGENTS.md` and its mirrors. `npm run labels:backfill` proposes the missing labels (dry run by default, add-only; `--apply`, `--create-labels`), with a fixture test in `npm test`.
- **A shared review checklist** (spec 021 FR-047/FR-048, owner decision 2026-10-23): `docs/review-checklist.md` lists the mistakes reviewers found again and again in the last week (masking, stale text, untested rules, CI that never ran on the head, fixed waits, number collisions and more), each with its reason and a PR link, plus what reviewers praised and how the team reviews. Every agent runs it on its own diff before asking for review; AGENTS.md, the PR template, `docs/collaboration.md` and the reference sub-agents say so. After every review round, reviewer and author add what was new; agents pull `main` at the start of each round; a PR that changes only the checklist or the agent notes takes a fast lane (one quick approval from another side, no waiting for the full CI). Docs only.
- **Process: today's team decisions are written down** (spec 021 FR-037..FR-043, AGENTS.md): decisions from the team chat are recorded the same day; sub-agents by the size of the change; review teammates' PRs first with one list per round; unstack with a replacement PR, never by force; how to use the merge queue; and "land before building": at most 8 open PRs per side, 3 of them new features waiting for review.
- **Move to floor from the folder menu** (spec 033 FR-024, issue #590): with two or more floors, right-click a folder (or its ⋯, or the context-menu key on its door) and choose **Move to floor ▸**; the floors open in the same menu (current one ticked), the whole folder moves with its agents, and a note offers **Undo**. Also on the List view's Folder cell. With one floor the menu is unchanged. Simulator check `shots-033-move` at 1440x900 and 1280x800.
- **Fixed: a token an agent printed no longer shows on the office floor** (spec 041 FR-065, T069, issue #541): the text of a finished desk and its tooltip, helper and sub-agent cards, the tool line, the list view, the Needs you inbox and answer bar, and the previous-sessions list is now masked before it is cut, with its Markdown removed but its `_` kept (so a token split by `**`, backticks or an escape is still caught); before, `ghp_...`, `github_pat_...` and `sk-...` in a reply could appear in the start of the reply on the desk.
- **Agent Success Profiles: delete, refresh at once, save only on Save, a better structured profile** (spec 043 FR-040..FR-043, issue #570): **Delete** beside **Edit** in the Profile menu and **Delete…** in Settings remove a profile file you can write, after a confirmation (main deletes only a listed, writable plain file, no shell); a profile you create, save or delete shows in every list at once, also in a running Copilot session's Profile menu (it re-reads its agent files once after a change); **Create** now opens the template as an unsaved draft and nothing is written until **Save**, so cancelling at any step leaves no file; the Agent Success Profile and its Create with AI prompt now have Role and goal, Scope and non-goals, Inputs and context, Working method, Quality bar and definition of done, Safety limits (permission mode, no secrets), Output format and When to ask the user. Checked with unit tests, the simulator (`shots-043-manage`) and one real Copilot session with no prompt (`rpc.agent.reload` lists new, edited and deleted files and keeps the selected agent; deleting the selected agent's file returns the session to the default agent).
- **The signed-in account per agent app** (spec 051 delta 2026-10-23, issue #617, owner request): Settings > Agent apps shows which account each app is signed in with ("Signed in as o••• (GitHub, through the GitHub CLI)", "y•••@example.com · Claude Pro", "Signed in with an OpenAI API key", "Account unknown"). The name is masked and blurred until you press the eye button beside it (for that view only, never saved); the Agent app picker's tooltip and *About agent apps* show it masked. Each app reports it itself without a prompt (Copilot `getAuthStatus`, `claude auth status`, Codex `account/read`); the name stays in the window, never in logs, the activity log or messages. `test:account`, `sim:` `shots-051-account`, `real:` one no-prompt check per app on Linux. Once signed in, a card lists Plan, Account, Provider and Signed in via, one per line (only what the app reports), instead of the general plan note.
- **Mac: double-click on the top bar** (spec 052 FR-085, #591): a double-click on an empty part of the top bar, the agent strip or a screen header zooms the window, minimizes it or does nothing, as set in System Settings > Desktop & Dock. Windows and Linux are unchanged. Unit-tested mapping; not yet tried on a real Mac.
- **Fewer macOS keychain prompts for Copilot and MCP sign-ins** (spec 058 FR-160, FR-161, issue #623): the mac package keeps GitHub's own signature on the bundled Copilot runtime instead of re-signing it ad-hoc, so the Keychain sees the same program as the `copilot` CLI and *Always Allow* holds across updates; a mac build fails if the signature is lost (`scripts/check-runtime-signature.js`). The one shared Copilot runtime now starts again after a failed start instead of failing for the rest of the run. On the owner's Mac (test build with 10 MCP servers), the first two launches after the update asked about 25 times each (Always Allow), and the third launch asked nothing.
- **No extra connect attempts on a failed MCP server** (spec 058 FR-163, issue #623): opening a failed, pending or needs-sign-in server's tools no longer asks Copilot to connect it again (each attempt could show another macOS keychain prompt). Checked with fake-SDK tests.
- **Sign in again to an MCP server** (spec 058 FR-164, issue #623): Copilot rows in the MCP servers dialog can redo a saved sign-in (**Sign in again…**, or **Sign in again to all**) through Copilot's own forced sign-in. This is meant for keychain prompts that return on every launch after an upgrade. It was checked with fakes and the simulator, but not yet on a Mac.
- **MCP dialog: long lists scroll, and a word never contradicts its switch** (spec 058 FR-077, FR-078, issue #544). With many servers the rows now scroll inside the dialog and *Settings*, *Folder extras…* and *Done* stay in view below them, and the *Restart session* row and its note scroll with the list instead of sitting on the buttons (before, the last rows and their switches drew over the buttons). A server the agent app turned off itself reads "Off in <app name>" (or "Turned off by the agent app"), its switch shows off, and a hint says to turn it on in the app's own MCP settings; A server Agent Durbar itself started as off (Settings, then switched on mid-session) reads "Off until the session restarts" with a pointer to *Restart session with these servers*. "Off for this session" is only for a server you switched off here, and "Off in Settings" / "Off in folder extras" for the others. Simulator and unit tests only (`test:mcpwords`, `shots-058-mcp-words` with 14+ servers at 1440x900, 1280x800 and 1280x600); not seen on a real agent app. The footer is now its own opaque layer above the scrolling list, so toggling a switch (also the last row's) can no longer put a row or switch over *Done* or the footer buttons (second report on #544; simulator checked at 1440x900, 1280x800 and 1280x600 with the toggle). The scroller's padding now leaves room for the focus ring of its first and last control in every wide dialog (add/edit server, trust, review, theme picker, import).
- **Why an MCP server failed** (spec 058 FR-090..FR-097, issue #603): a failed or needs-sign-in server now says why in plain words (not installed, crashed on start, timed out, disconnected later, refused, needs sign-in, or "Failed (reason unknown)"; never guessed) and when Durbar saw it, in the chat's MCP dialog and in Settings > MCP servers; **Copy diagnostics** copies a masked summary for an issue report; the composer's MCP chip shows a small red dot (and "1 failed" in its name) when a server of that chat failed. Copilot's failure classification and Claude Code's skipped config entries are mapped. Simulator and unit tests; not checked on a real account beyond Copilot's "command not found".
- **MCP live status in Settings** (spec 058, issue #596, owner request): *Settings > MCP servers* now starts with *Live in your chats*, each server's state over all running chats ("Failed in 1 chat · Running in 2 chats", "Not started yet") with the chat dialog's words, icons and hints; the *Advanced: MCP servers* heading counts what needs attention. Open a server to see its chats, with **Details**, **Restart** in one chat, **Restart session with these servers** and **Open in chat**; it follows the agent apps' reports while open. The chat dialog's *Settings* button now opens this section. Simulator and unit tests only.
- **MCP Sign in for Copilot servers** (spec 058 FR-064..FR-069, FR-071, issue #364; tasks T098, T101, T102, T104): in a chat's **More > MCP servers**, a Copilot server that reads *Needs sign-in* has **Sign in**. Durbar asks Copilot's own runtime to start its sign-in (`mcp.oauth.login`, no token or callback ever passes through Durbar), shows "Sign-in page on *host*", and opens your browser only after you click **Open sign-in page** (https only, no user:password in the address, kept in memory for at most 10 minutes and dropped on Cancel, connected, End session or Restart session). The row updates when Copilot reports the server connected. **Switch off for this session** and *How to sign in from a terminal* (`copilot`, `/mcp auth <name>`, with **Copy**) are on the row; the steps show on their own where the runtime cannot start a sign-in. Claude Code and Codex rows are unchanged until their own PRs. Checked with the fake SDK, unit tests (`test:mcpsignin`, address-free replies and logs, the 10-minute limit, `http:` and user:password refused) and the simulator at 1440x900 and 1280x800 (`simmcpauth=1`, `simmcpauth=manual`); checked once on a real account (Copilot, macOS, 2026-10-23, one Microsoft-hosted server: it worked, the row was slow); the 5-second re-read, host binding, source line and address rules came after and are checked in the simulator and tests only.
  Review round: the confirm shows where the server came from and warns when the page is on a different site than the server (FR-075); Open is bound to the host shown; no localhost, IP or one-word hosts, no whitespace in the address, IDN hosts shown as `xn--`; the manual steps are worded as a fallback that may not carry over (first real check, 2026-10-23); neutral credential-store wording.
  First real check (owner's Mac, Copilot, `agent365-teams`): Sign in worked and the row reached Running, but only after a long wait for the runtime's status, so while the sign-in page is open Durbar now reads the server list again every 5 seconds (at most 10 minutes; a re-read that still says "connected" never counts as signed in for a server whose tool calls failed with the sign-in challenge) and when you come back to the window.
- **MCP Sign in for Claude Code** (spec 058 FR-175, FR-176, FR-180..FR-186, issue #364 step 5): a Claude Code server that reads *Needs sign-in* gets **Sign in** in the chat's MCP dialog, through Claude Code's own in-session sign-in (`mcp_authenticate`; no callback, client secret or redirect address passes through Durbar, and no login command is run). The page is kept only as long as Claude Code's listener waits; the row keeps **Restart** to pick up a sign-in finished elsewhere, and its terminal steps say `claude mcp login <name>`, then Restart. Each app's row now has its own honest note, a Claude user-list server's allow-listed `oauth` settings (never a client secret) reach Durbar's copy, and the "different site" line now compares with the agent app's own copy of the server, not a Copilot server of the same name (fix of #598). Simulator and test harnesses only; read from Claude Code 2.1.304, not checked on a real account (T106).
- **Debug log masks OAuth values in addresses** (spec 058, review on #635): the `DURBAR_LOG` sink now masks `code`, `state`, `code_verifier`, `code_challenge` and token values in a URL's query or fragment (also percent-encoded), so an agent app's sign-in or callback address in its error output is not written in the clear. Unit tests only.
- **VS Code prompt files in the "/" list** (spec 073, FR-012, owner request #613): every chat (Copilot, Claude Code, Codex) lists the folder's `.github/prompts/*.prompt.md` and your VS Code user prompts, with their description; sending one sends its text with `${input:…}` filled from your words, and the chat names any VS Code settings (`mode`, `tools`, ...) or editor variables it could not carry over. The Copilot CLI does not list them itself (checked at no cost on runtimes 1.0.98 and 1.0.103). Unit- and simulator-tested.
- **The "/" list masks token shapes** (spec 073 FR-013/FR-014, issue #608, security): a skill, command or prompt file whose name,
  description, argument hint or path holds a token shape (`ghp_…`, `github_pat_…`, `sk-…`, also split by Markdown) no longer shows it
  in the list, its tooltips, the screen-reader read-out or the "Sent the text of" note; main masks before it cuts, so no half token
  survives a cut. Checked with `test:slashcommands` and `check:slash-suggest` (simulator), mutation-checked.
- **The chat's "Send failed" note masks token shapes** (spec 041 FR-065, T070): an agent app's refusal that holds a token shape
  shows it as `[redacted]`. Checked with `check:done-desk-mask` (`simsendfail=secret`), mutation-checked.
- **A token glued to a word by Markdown is masked** (spec 041 FR-065, T071; spec 073 FR-014): `x**ghp_…**` read as `xghp_…` once the
  Markdown was removed, and the office's masking missed it; both passes are now combined in the window and in main. Main also runs the
  full secret masking (URL credentials too) before it cuts a "/" list text, and a prompt file's send note is masked.
- **Go to keys, after a test drive** (spec 074, issue #587): Go to opens with **Ctrl+F** (⌘F on a Mac) instead of Ctrl+J ("F for floor"; a shortcut you set yourself is kept). Opening a chat by any route (Go to, a click or Enter on a desk or list row, Ctrl+K, Needs you; not N, which keeps walking the queue) puts the cursor in its message box, never on an Allow button, and unsent text and attachments are kept per chat while the app runs (in memory only; ending or removing the session drops them), with a pencil on the desk, list row and chat strip of a chat that has an unsent draft (issue #612). **Ctrl+A** (⌘A) opens Needs you and **Ctrl+Backspace** (⌘⌫) goes back to the office, but only outside text boxes and with nothing selected, so select-all and delete still work while you type. Checked with unit tests and the simulator driver (mutation-checked); not tried on a real Mac or Windows machine.
- **Team rule: chat feedback goes on GitHub too** (owner decision 2026-10-23): feedback, review notes, design advice or owner decisions given in the team chat about an issue or PR are also posted as a comment on it. Process docs only (AGENTS.md, docs/collaboration.md); no code.

## v0.1.0-alpha.13 ([pre-release](https://github.com/three-feathers/agent-durbar/releases/tag/v0.1.0-alpha.13), 2026-10-23)

The first pre-release cut from `main` since alpha.11, on owner request (spec 078). alpha.12 was cut from the feature branch `043-profile-ai`; everything it listed has since merged to `main` and is in this build too (profile editor and Create with AI, live file edits in the chat, coffee break, the composer toolbar, previous sessions, theme tokens and theme art, MCP restart, Windows install options, escaped-quote masking), so it is listed once, under alpha.12 below; only what changed after alpha.12 is repeated here. First release built from `changelog.d/` fragments (`npm run changelog:build`, spec 025 T015). Unsigned and not notarized, as before.

- **Process: Paco and Ruben are code owners next to Vikram** for `.specify/`, `.claude/`, `.github/`, `AGENTS.md` and `CLAUDE.md` (owner decision 2026-10-22). An approval from any one of the three owners now satisfies the code-owner review, so PRs touching those paths no longer wait for the maintainer's admin merge.
- **The office uses its space when there are few agents** (spec 001 FR-041..FR-046, owner request 2026-10-22): with a handful of agents the desks grow, up to about 2.5x and as large as fits without scrolling, instead of a row of small tiles with the rest of the floor empty. A grown desk shows the full title on two lines, up to 8 lines of the live thinking or reply (newest at the bottom, older lines fade out at the top), the start of the last reply once it is done (secrets masked on the raw text, before Markdown is removed), and tokens and cost. As agents are added the desks get smaller (the largest size that fits, so a new row can drop it several sizes at once) down to the usual densities; the 15-agent layouts at 1440x900 and 1280x800 are unchanged. Grown desks now come before the `full` illustrated desks and their side panel (the owner's decision, 2026-10-22). A wide grown desk (one agent, or a row with spare width) uses the extra width for more information, not longer lines: next to the stream, the start of the last reply (masked) and the current tool, usage, context and time on task, in up to three text columns of at most about 85 characters a line, folding back to one column as the desk narrows (FR-047..FR-050). `sim:` `npm run check:office-fill` (1-15 agents in 1-3 folders, 1024x700 to 2560x1440), `test:probe`, `test:liveact`; not yet watched on a real session.
- **Boss, Bank and Postbox in the lobby** (spec 001 FR-034..FR-040, owner request 2026-10-22; replaces the office rail of the first build): agents with a question or a permission walk to the **Boss** in the middle of the lobby and queue there in the order they started waiting (badge ? or key, longest wait, click opens the chat at the request, they walk back once answered; the desk shows an empty chair with *Went to see the boss*); Durbar's own spending cap waits at the **Bank** (*Limit reached*; provider usage limits stay in the coffee corner, spec 068); the **Postbox** shows the next three scheduled runs, the runs still due today and a missed/failed flag, opens Settings > Schedules, and a letter flies to the desk when a schedule fires (none with reduced motion). Lobby places name their visitors and what they do (masked; initials when names do not fit; "+N" lists everyone); room headers count in words; the lobby is 58 px tall. Simulator driver `shots-001-lobby` (also with reduced motion), `check:office`, `check:geometry`, at 1440x900 and 1280x800; not seen on a real session or a Mac or Windows PC.
- **Boss, Bank and Postbox: review fixes (spec 001, spec 024 FR-018, review on #511).** A place of your own saved as "Bank", "Postbox", "Boss" or "Coffee corner" (now names of built-in tiles) is kept at startup as "Bank (2)" and so on, with its MCP mappings, instead of disappearing; the lobby's tooltips are short (a visitor's name, folder and activity cut with an ellipsis; a tile names up to four visitors). The Postbox opens Settings right at Schedules with that heading focused, without scrolling the whole window, and its flag also counts a one-time schedule missed while the app was closed. `test:` `test:places`; `sim:` `check:ui-text`, `shots-001-lobby.js`.
- **Desk card tooltip masks before it cuts (spec 001 FR-036, spec 041 FR-065, review on #511).** The desk card's tooltip and accessible name mask tokens and secrets in the agent's text before the tooltip is cut to 200 characters, so a token across the cut no longer shows a piece of itself. `sim:` `shots-001-lobby.js` also checks that session titles and schedule prompts with markup show as plain text at the Boss, the Bank, the coffee corner, the "+N" list and the Postbox.
- **Finished agents rest in the lounge** (spec 001 FR-051..FR-057, owner request 2026-10-22, reshaped after the owner's notes on #554): in a folder where other agents still work, a finished agent (Done, or Idle after its reply) moves from its desk to a small lounge corner at the end of the desk row, so the width goes to the working desks (grown desks included). For 10 minutes it sits on a couch, its name on the cushion, with a speech bubble saying what it did last (the start of its reply on up to three lines, masked on the raw text); then it naps in a bunk bed and a dream cloud shows its last work (several dreams take turns, about 7 s each; "+N asleep" past four). Each is the agent's own character from the theme. Hover or focus opens a card beside the lounge; a click or Enter opens the chat; screen readers hear "resting on the couch, finished 2 minutes ago". Waiting, Error, Limit and Paused agents never rest; a folder where everyone is finished keeps its desks. Calm motion, none with reduced motion. Off in Settings > Detail level (*Finished agents rest in the lounge*). `test:` `npm run test:shutters`; `sim:` `npm run check:office-fill` (2 working, 2 on the couch, 3 asleep at 1440x900 and 1280x800; the simulator's 15 agents still fit); not yet watched on a real session.
- **Fixed: in the lounge, a sleeping agent's chat number no longer covers its name** ("Noor" read "oor"). The number moves to the foot corner of the bed; `check:office-fill` now fails when a badge meets a name (spec 001 FR-054, issue 576).
- **Composer toolbar, after alpha.12** (spec 005): a screen reader hears "Working…" and "Stopped" / "Reply finished" as a reply starts and ends.
- CI only (owner decision 2026-10-22, spec 008 FR-098..FR-110, T095/T097): a pull request asks for 9 runners instead of 22. No *What changed* gate job (each job checks what changed itself), one office layout job instead of 6 shards and a summary job, one UI job per OS, drafts run nothing until ready, superseded PR runs are cancelled, the Spec Kit gate no longer re-runs on label changes, and every job has a time limit. **macOS no longer runs on pull requests**: it runs on every push to `main` (build and test), nightly (UI, installed app, packaged check) and weekly (install check), and a failed nightly run opens or updates an issue. The required checks keep their names (`Build and test (macos-latest)` reports from a short Linux job on PRs). Theme layout checks on pull requests run Clean Office and Retro only (every theme on `main` and nightly; `test:theme` now also refuses sizes in CSS shorthands in `themes.css`; `npm run check:geometry` checks Retro, `CHECK_ALL_THEMES=1` all). `npm run check:ui` stops at once with the file and the error when a driver does not parse (it used to hang until the time limit). The workflows are ready for a merge queue.
- **Theme geometry check no longer flakes on a slow first frame** (spec 008 FR-113, T100; flake on PR #535): `check:geometry` waits for a steady simulator before every capture, the Clean Office reference included (agents listed, scripts replayed, totals read, a queued frame drawn), and a timed-out wait fails the run instead of measuring. Same combinations and tolerance. Linux simulator only: 5 runs in a row, a forced frame stall and a one-theme mutation.
