- CI only (spec 008 FR-114, reported by fjcabello on PR #546): the office layout parts (`check:parallel`) and `ui-suite.js` install Electron once before they start, with the same step `npm test` uses (`scripts/ensure-electron.js`), so parallel parts no longer race to unpack it and exit 126 "Text file busy". Checked with a unit test and a local repro of the race.
- `npm test -- --bail` (or `TEST_BAIL=1`) stops at the first failing suite and stops the suites still running (their whole process group, TERM then KILL after 5 s, or tree with `taskkill /T /F` on Windows), for quick local iteration. Ignored under `CI`: plain `npm test`, PR CI and the merge queue run everything. [spec 008 FR-115]
- **The simulator can no longer spend AI credits** (spec 010 FR-022..FR-027; incident on PR #505, where a simulator driver reached the real preload API and ran 3-6 real Claude prompts). Under `DURBAR_SIM` the main process refuses every window call except the install-route list and the app version, before its handler runs, creates only inert stand-ins instead of the Copilot, Claude Code and Codex adapters, does not start the scheduler and ignores `DURBAR_OPEN`. Packaged builds are unaffected (they ignore `DURBAR_SIM`). New `npm run test:simguard` (in `npm test`) and the simulator driver `check:sim-guard` (in `check:ui`). Review on #514 (FR-029..FR-031): detection and sign-in checks ask no agent app under the simulator, main starts no child process at all (`child_process` is sealed), no file but `ipcguard.ts` may touch `ipcMain` or `webContents.ipc`, and the guard takes main's simulator decision. FR-032: a plain simulator start refuses nothing and leaves no unhandled promise rejection in the page (the version call). `test:` `sim:`
- **Security: a token after a percent-encoded byte is masked** (spec 012 FR-080 delta, T129, issue #541; review of #543): `redact` and the folder-setup window now also hide a token right after a URL escape such as `?q=token%3Dghp_…`, `%22sk-…%22`, `%2F`, `%3A` or a `%` encoded again any number of times (`%253D`, `%2525253D`), and after a backslash escape such as `\n` or `\t`, so the debug log no longer writes a token that starts a line in full, for every token kind (GitHub, `sk-`, Bearer, AWS, JWT, `Authorization:`, URL credentials). Unit tests only.
- **Security: a token after an underscore is masked** (spec 012 FR-080 delta, T127, issue #541; review of #532): `redact` no longer starts its token shapes at a word boundary, so a token in Markdown emphasis (`_ghp_…_`, `__sk-…__`) or glued to a `snake_case` word (`my_github_pat_…`) is masked wherever `redact` runs (debug log, live activity, desks, chat displays). The folder-setup window (spec 061) now uses the same shapes instead of its own copy, so it hides those tokens too. A JWT is masked in the same places (`my_eyJ…`, `MY__eyJ…`, `____eyJ…`; T128). Unit tests only.
- **Fewer merge conflicts between pull requests** (spec 025, delta 2026-10-22): a PR now adds its CHANGELOG line as a file in `changelog.d/`, which `npm run changelog:build` folds into the release entry. The specs index rows are no longer committed: each spec keeps its row in a `specs-index` block in its own `spec.md`, and the website (docs page, *Specs index*) and `npm run specs:index` build the table. Two PRs no longer edit the same lines in `CHANGELOG.md` or `specs/README.md`. `npm run changelog:check` and `npm run specs:check` run in `npm test`; both only warn until a cut-over marker is committed (#520). Unit tests (`test:changelog`, `test:specsindex`, `test:site`); no change to the app.
- Test tooling only: the masking time checks in `test-redact` and the 200,000-line flood check in `test-mcp-protocol` measure CPU time instead of wall time, so a loaded machine or runner no longer fails them at random; same sizes and limits, and a deliberately quadratic rule still fails; the worker-thread cases time their own thread's CPU, the live-activity thinking flood in `test-liveact` moved too, and a loose wall-time backstop still catches a long blocking wait (spec 041 T068, Ruben's notes on #455 and #527).
- **Edit a schedule** (spec 044 FR-022..FR-026, issue #522; owner's report): every scheduled prompt has **Edit…** in the folder's dialog and in Settings > Schedules. It fills the form (prompt, when, Send to, Limit); Save changes the row in place and keeps its last run, pause state and spend; an edit never drops a limit by accident. A done one-time schedule runs again when saved with a future time. The Settings button reads **Schedules for this folder…**, the cut prompt opens in full, and a long prompt no longer pushes Pause and Delete out of the dialog. Unit tests and simulator check `check:sched-edit` (1440x900, 1280x800); not seen on a Mac.
- **Codex write requests, after alpha.12** (spec 052 FR-172): *Allow for this session* on a change that writes in several folders names and covers every one of them, and a grant on one folder no longer approves a later change that also writes elsewhere; file paths are never shortened before that check. Unit tests.
- **The composer placeholder stays on one line** (spec 052 FR-174, issue #453): with a long session title the placeholder `Message <title>…` wrapped and showed half of a second line (seen on macOS). It is now cut from the measured width of the box and ends in `…`, with the full text in the aria-label (kept current when the title arrives or the session is renamed); `check:ui-text` gets the rule `placeholder-line` and the page address `simtitles=long`.
- **Popovers share one width pair and one padding** (spec 052 FR-167, issue #508): Defaults and Allowed grants are 420px (`--pop-w-s`), Folders and Floors 660px (`--pop-w-m`), all with the padding `--pop-pad` (12px 14px); each is the window minus 32px when the window is smaller. "Pause all schedules" in the Folders popover is on its own line (it squeezed the first folder's name to "mc..."); the border and error colours there and in 15 other rules are theme tokens, so they read in dark themes. New `check:ui-text` rules: `popover-width`, `popover-squeezed` (a cut name or path under 120px), literal light borders and outlines, long-name passes for Folders and Floors, and a Floors error shown in the dark run. Checked in the simulator only (Linux fonts).
- **MCP dialog: a word and icon per state, a hint, Details and live refresh** (spec 058 T113-T115, issue #364 step 4). Each server row reads Running, Starting, Failed, Needs sign-in, Off for this session, Stopped, Not set up for this session, Not started yet (or Unknown state with its name), with an icon so colour is not the only sign. A failed or needs-sign-in server shows a hint (a sign-in hint names the agent app) and **Details** with the masked error and **Copy**. The open dialog follows the agent app's `mcp` reports without losing focus. `agents:mcp` now carries the agent app's name and each server's `errorClass`. Simulator and unit tests only (`test:mcpwords`, `test:mcpsession`, `shots-058-mcp-words`); not checked against a real agent app.
- **A long unbroken session name stays inside its box** (spec 059 FR-019, T061, issue #536; owner's report: a first prompt with a URL ran out of the desk card). On a full desk card a name wraps to two lines at most and ends in "…", a token without spaces may break anywhere, and the full name stays in the tooltip. The other surfaces (roomy and compact cards, header, strip, list, Previous sessions, the Needs you inbox) already cut a name to one line. The simulator's `simnames=long` has one URL-like name; `check:office`, `check:rename` and the new `shots-059-long-url.js` (in `check:ui`) fail when a name leaves its box (the new driver also guards the Schedules rows). Simulator only; not run on a real session.
- **CI only: the real-account check runs daily on cheap models** (spec 062, delta 2026-10-22): the *E2E real accounts* workflow no longer runs on every push to `main`; it runs once a day and by hand. The one-line turn runs daily on Linux; macOS and Windows install, sign in and list models daily (no prompt) and run the turn on Mondays or by hand (`full_turn`). Codex runs a small listed model (`mini`, then `nano`) at effort `low`. Unit test (`test:platforms`); no real run was made for this change. No change to the app.
- **MCP servers for all my folders** (spec 069 part 1, owner request): *+ Add server* offers *All my folders (global)*, which writes the server into the agent apps' own lists (Copilot CLI, Claude Code, Codex; ticks for the installed apps, a single choice with one) after a per-file review of the exact entry, with a backup next to each file; secret values are referenced as environment variables by default. Settings > MCP shows every app's servers with source chips and *Remove…*; an agent's MCP dialog shows each server's sources; Durbar's Claude Code sessions get Claude Code's user list. Unit tests (`test:mcpglobal`) and a simulator driver (`shots-069-mcp-global`); a real check in a scratch home (0 credits) saw the Copilot CLI, Claude Code and Codex list the written entries, and found and fixed one thing: a Codex `config.toml` that does not parse is now refused instead of appended to. Copilot expanding `${NAME}` is not verified.
- **Global MCP lists: review fixes (spec 069, review on #489).** A symlinked `~/.claude.json` or `~/.codex/config.toml` is written through the link (the link stays, the backup sits next to the real file); Codex TOML strings such as `"C:\\notes\\tmp"` are decoded in one pass (before: a newline and a tab), in spec 061's folder reader too; Durbar's Claude sessions getting Claude Code's user list is now unit-tested.
- **Global MCP lists: second review fixes (spec 069, review on #489).** Every agent-app config file Durbar writes is left readable only by you (a 0644 `~/.codex/config.toml` with plain values became 0600; a stricter mode is kept); JSON files are edited in place, so indentation, big numbers such as `12345678901234567890`, `1.0` and escapes stay as written; the hash is checked again right before the rename; the review says Copilot `${NAME}` expansion is not verified; Claude's MCP JSON is passed through a private 0600 file instead of the command line; the backup's content is now tested.
- **Harmonize the agent apps' MCP lists** (spec 069 part 2): Settings > MCP lists servers that are missing or different between Copilot CLI, Claude Code and Codex (which fields differ, never the values) and copies one server to the others after the same per-file review; references stay references across formats (`${X}`, Codex `env_vars`, `env_http_headers`, `bearer_token_env_var`). Unit tests and a simulator driver (`shots-069-harmonize`).
- **Harmonize: review fixes (spec 069, review on #492).** The copy review masks a token in the source entry's command, arguments or URL (before: only env and header values); a copy keeps a server switched off, its hidden tools and its tool list where the target list can hold them and otherwise refuses that file naming the field (before: a switched-off Codex server was copied as on); the differences list names tools and on/off.
- **Harmonize: second review fixes (spec 069, review on #492).** A copy never drops a field or writes a value empty: an entry with fields Durbar does not carry (Claude Code `oauth`, `headersHelper`, Copilot `timeout`, Codex `startup_timeout_sec` or extra sub-tables) or values that are not plain strings (a number or multi-line string in a Codex `env`) is named and not copied, and replacing such an entry says what it drops; a plain secret copied into a 0644 file leaves that file 0600.
- **Turn single MCP tools off** (spec 069 part 4, owner request): in the expanded tools list each tool has a checkbox and the server a tri-state box; the choice is kept per folder and passed to the agent app's own filter at the next session or *Restart session* (Copilot `excludedTools`, Claude Code `--disallowedTools`, Codex `disabled_tools`); Durbar's permission check refuses a turned-off tool in every mode as well. Unit tests on fakes (mutation-checked) and the simulator driver; not run on a real account.
- **Single MCP tools: review fixes (spec 069, review on #503).** A tool whose name has characters other than letters, digits, `_`, `.` or `-` can no longer be turned off (its checkbox is disabled with the reason; before, Claude Code in Allow all did not block it at all); Codex hiding a tool of a server from your own `config.toml` and Copilot's exact tool names stay unverified on a real account (README, spec).
- **MCP tool switches: review fixes (spec 069 part 4, review on #503).** After a tool box is toggled the focus stays on that server's box (two servers can share a tool name) or, when the list closes, on the server's tools button; a box that cannot change stays reachable with Tab, does nothing on Space, and shows why next to it.
- **An MCP server's tools, and Remove, in the agent's MCP dialog** (spec 069 part 3): click a server's name to list its tools (loaded only then, masked, cut to one line; loading, empty, failed and not-running states), **Remove…** works for servers of an agent app's list or the folder, and each row shows its sources. The row keeps spec 058's state word, hint and *Details* / *Copy*; the switch reads "Switch off <server> for <agent> in this session". Copilot `rpc.mcp.listTools`, Claude Code `mcp_status`, Codex `mcpServerStatus/list`; unit tests on fakes and a simulator driver (`shots-069-mcp-tools`), not run on a real account.
- **MCP dialog: review fixes (spec 069 part 3, review on #499).** *Remove…* on a server that is both in an agent app's list and the folder's extras offers every place, all ticked, and removes each; the switch says "in this session" only while a session runs ("when its session starts" otherwise) and the footer gives the true scope; Codex tool lists follow `nextCursor`, so a server past the first page is found.
- **Theme tokens, after alpha.12** (spec 070): a text or button colour that would be hard to read is adjusted just enough instead of falling back to the default (the theme's surfaces never change, so a dark theme stays dark); Settings and the AI dialog say how many values were adjusted or replaced.
- **Type `/` in the message box for the folder's skills and commands** (spec 073, owner request): a list of the chat's agent app's skills, commands and prompt files in the folder or workspace, then the user's own, then an allow list of Claude Code's built-ins that run when typed; filter by typing, arrows and Tab or Enter to insert, Esc to close (an ARIA combobox). Claude Code and Copilot do not load these files in Durbar's sessions and Codex's app-server does not expand `/name`, so a folder or user entry is sent as its file's text with your words as arguments, and the chat says which file. Reads only the folder and the documented user directories (no links out, capped), cached per folder, and only when the message starts with `/name`. Unit tests and a simulator driver; the vendor behaviour was checked with 3 real prompts (Claude Code, Copilot); Codex read only.
- **Go to, a keyboard navigator** (spec 074, issue #548): Ctrl+J (⌘J on a Mac; changeable in Settings > Keyboard, with a clash check that also knows Mac text-editing Control keys, Linux IBus keys and other keyboard layouts) opens the Ctrl+K box in a Go-to mode: floor, folder, agent, or C and a number for a chat; the chat opens with the cursor in the message box. Chats are numbered (C3 on desks, list rows and the chat header). Checked with unit tests and a simulator driver at 1440x900 and 1280x800.

## v0.1.0-alpha.12 ([pre-release](https://github.com/three-feathers/agent-durbar/releases/tag/v0.1.0-alpha.12), 2026-10-22)

Cut on owner request from the feature branch `043-profile-ai` (PR #505), **not from `main`**: it carries the approved stack work below that has not merged to `main` yet, and Create with AI's last review fixes are still awaiting re-review. Unsigned and not notarized, as before.

- **View and edit profile files** (spec 043 FR-010..FR-017, owner request): *Settings > Profiles (custom agents)* and an **Edit** button in the Profile menu open a profile's Markdown file in a large editor with **Edit | Split | Preview**, a front matter check, a throttled preview through the chat's sanitised Markdown renderer, **Save** (Ctrl+S / ⌘S), an *Unsaved* mark, a question before closing with unsaved changes or overwriting a file changed elsewhere, and **New profile…** (Copilot or Claude Code, in a folder or your user folder). Owner decisions: the editor calls them **Agent Success Profiles**, and a new one starts from the default *Agent Success Profile* template (Title, People responsibility, Reporting to, Behaviors, Key Success Criteria, Skills, Tools and accountability, Key responsibilities) or a *Standard agent*; both start with read-only tools. Main owns every path: the window sends only ids from main's list (for files and, in New profile, for folders), links out of an agent folder are refused, writes are atomic and keep line endings, Claude Code files keep Claude's own 64 KB limit, and Windows device names (`CON`, `NUL`...) are refused. Applies to sessions started after saving. Unit tests and a simulator driver (`shots-043-editor`) at 1440x900 and 1280x800, light and dark; not tried on real agent files on Windows or macOS.
- **Create an Agent Success Profile with AI** (spec 043 FR-026..FR-039, owner request): in *New Agent Success Profile*, **Create with AI…** takes a short description or answers to eight optional questions, asks an installed agent app once through the AI theme maker's one-shot path (no tools, no session, nothing kept; cost shown on the button), checks the reply (front matter, all eight sections, only the read-only defaults and the tools you list in the **Extra tools** line, never a tool your text merely mentions; the draft lists every tool beyond read-only and warns about a tool you mentioned but did not list; HTML kept only as text; at most the size a save takes: 64 KB for Claude Code, 256 KB for Copilot; one `name`, whatever the reply wrote) and opens it unsaved in Split view; Save creates the file. Answers are kept on an error; after *Stop waiting*, Cancel or Escape, and in a reopened dialog, Create stays off until the first request ends, and main sends at most one request at a time, so one action costs at most one prompt. Unit tests and a simulator driver (`shots-043-ai`) at 1440x900 and 1280x800; checked once on Claude Code (haiku, 2026-10-22): the reply passed and opened as a draft. A program the agent should run is listed under Extra tools (such as `Bash(git:*)`); a word in your text never grants a tool.
- **Restart an MCP server, or the session with the current servers** (spec 058 T030-T034, T040-T044, issue #349; owner's request "I miss VS Code's start/stop/restart"). In a chat's **More > MCP servers**, each running server that is on has **Restart** (only that server, this session: Copilot `restartServer`, or off then on on an older runtime; Claude Code `mcp_reconnect`; Codex has none and the dialog says to use the next button). **Restart session with these servers** ends the session and resumes the same one with Settings, the folder's extras and this agent's switches as they are now, with no prompt sent; it asks first while the agent is busy or has session permissions or remote control, and a resume that fails for sign-in shows the chat's sign-in card. Errors are masked before they reach the window; each restart is a row in the activity log. Unit tests with fake adapters and SDKs and a simulator driver (`shots-058-mcp-restart`) at 1440x900 and 1280x800. Checked on a real Copilot session (2026-10-21, Linux, T060): Restart uses the runtime's `restartServer`, Restart session keeps the conversation and applies the new servers; Copilot starts a session's servers with the first message (the dialog now says so), and a failed restart shows the server's own reason. Claude Code and Codex not run on a real account.
- **File edits stream into the chat** (spec 005 FR-018..FR-027; owner's report "nothing is shown while the agent edits files, only the seconds grow"). The adapters dropped the argument stream of edit tools (Claude Code `input_json_delta`, Copilot `assistant.tool_call_delta`, Codex `item/fileChange/patchUpdated`), so a row appeared only after the whole edit was written, often without a file name. Now the row "Editing app.js  src  +12 −3" appears as soon as the file is named, with a masked, compact diff that grows as it is written (Show all; Simple keeps it behind *See what changes*), and the waiting line says "Editing app.js · 12s". Unit tests from SDK types and fixtures, simulator check `npm run check:edits`; real check 2026-10-21 (T036): Claude Code 2.1.304 and Copilot CLI 1.0.101 stream the edit and the row appears before the tool runs; codex-cli 0.163.0 sends the whole patch at once (its patch streaming is an unreleased feature), so a Codex row appears when the edit starts. A partial edit no longer shows the old text as a removal before the new text arrives (FR-027). After review (T049): a refused or failed edit reads "Could not edit app.js" (Simple "Not changed"), not "Edited"; the waiting line names a tool still running when a newer one ends and says "Waiting for your OK" while a permission card waits; main also masks edits with the session's MCP secret values; parsing of streamed arguments is linear (a 100,000-space write took up to 61 s before).
- **Coffee break when a usage limit is reached** (spec 068, owner's idea). An agent whose plan window, quota or rate limit is used up (Claude Code, Codex, Copilot), or that reached your spending limit, waits in a new **coffee corner** in the lobby instead of showing an error, with the reset time when the agent app gives one. It comes back by itself when the limit resets and that costs nothing extra (one fixed message to continue), asks first for a quota that may be billed, and never goes past your spending limit or resumes after a restart. Settings > *Coffee break* switches to always asking. Themes name the corner (Chai stall, The Tea Room, Snack bar). Unit tests and a simulator driver (`scripts/shots-068-coffee.js`, `simbreak=N`); not run against a real limit.
- **The chat's settings sit under the message box, and Send turns into Stop** (spec 005 FR-037..FR-043, owner request). As in VS Code: the profile, mode, model with its context window, context % (its menu has Compact) and an **MCP** chip with how many servers are on (it opens the existing list) moved from the chat header to one row under the message box, with the attach buttons. Inside the box, one button is **Send** while idle and **Stop** with a spinner while a reply runs. A narrow chat puts what does not fit under **⋯** (the mode always stays). The header keeps the name, status, spending limit, folder and its actions. Same menus and dialogs as before. `npm run check:composer-toolbar` (simulator, both sizes, a 480px panel, Simple, a dark theme); not run on a real session, macOS or Windows.
- **Previous sessions are easier to find** (spec 006 FR-033..FR-037, owner request): the *Previous (N)* button in the chat header is replaced by a collapsible section right above the conversation, a slim row with a chevron, *Previous sessions* (*Earlier chats* in Simple), the count and what it holds. Click it or press Ctrl+Shift+H (⇧⌘H on a Mac); the open or closed choice is remembered on this computer. Same lists, search, pins, hides, Rename… and Delete… as before; the search box now follows dark themes. Checked in the simulator at 1440x900 and 1280x800, light and dark; not on macOS or Windows.
- **Themes can set fonts, corners, shadows, clothes and gadgets** (spec 070; owner's request "real tokenization and full support for total customization of the theme, including fonts"). A theme pack, and the AI theme maker, can now set the body, title and code fonts (installed system fonts from an allow-list; nothing is downloaded), the title weight, corner radii, card and menu shadows, the people's clothes and hair colours (skin tones stay the app's), and its own cup and laptop drawings. Each value is checked by type; one that is unusable, or text that would be hard to read, uses the default and Settings says how many; markup or CSS in a value still refuses the pack. Sizes and spacing are not themeable, so 15 agents still fit. A new test (`test:theme-tokens`) keeps the colour literals in the renderer from growing (Phase 2 moves the rest onto tokens). Checked once on a real model (Claude haiku, 1 prompt): every new value came back valid; the maker now also keeps a colour written as `transparent` or `rgb(r g b / a)`.
- **A picked theme now changes the people and gadgets on every desk** (spec 040 FR-023; owner's report "the Barbie theme's characters were only visible in the top bar"). After a theme switch the desks kept their old drawing (colours, characters, desk and monitor art) until the agent's status changed, and figures in the lobby kept theirs; now every desk, helper tile and walking figure is redrawn. A theme's hat or headband is drawn over the hair instead of under the head, and a theme's character, desk and monitor art shows on every scene kit, not only the office one. Checked in the simulator with a sample AI-style pack, *Pink Studio* (`npm run check:theme-art`).
- **Windows install options** (spec 060 T063, owner report): Settings > Agent apps now finds winget (an App Execution Alias that Node could not `stat`, so Copilot had no install route on a PC without Node.js), and the install dialog lists the vendors' own commands with a Copy button, labelled PowerShell, PowerShell (winget) and npm (macOS and Linux: Terminal, Homebrew, npm). Each command whose route Durbar can run here also has **Run** (spec 060 FR-012, owner ask): Durbar runs that route itself after its usual confirmation (the PowerShell installers are downloaded from the vendor and run with Windows PowerShell as a file, never piped into `iex`); Copilot's winget route says it needs no Node.js. The Claude npm route no longer says "Deprecated by Anthropic": Anthropic's setup page lists it as an advanced option (read 2026-10-21). Not run on a real Windows PC.
- **Codex write requests name the file and show the change** (spec 052 FR-172, real Codex finding): the permission card for a Codex file change said only "write". It now names the file(s) and their kind (new file, delete, move) and has **Show the change** with the diff of every file, secrets masked; when Codex names no file it says "Codex did not say which file". The card's +/− counts are also right for a diff of several files (Codex, Claude Code MultiEdit). Unit tests from the generated Codex types and a simulator check (`check:codex-file-card`); checked on one real Codex 0.163 edit on Linux.
- **Secrets inside escaped quotes are masked** (spec 041 T067, #447): everywhere a command line is shown through `maskSecretCommand` / `maskSecretArgs` (the MCP read tools, live activity on the desk and the other command displays). The payload of `sh -c` / `bash -c` (also `bash -c --`, `bash -O ...`), `cmd /c` (also wrapping `powershell -Command`) and `powershell -Command` is unescaped one level with that shell's own rules (`\"`, `^"`, PowerShell's `` `" ``, `""` and `''`) before masking, so `sh -c "srv -H \"X-Api-Key: SEC\""` no longer shows `SEC`; a quote that never closes is masked to the end, and a leading unmatched quote (`"--token=SEC`) no longer hides the flag. The fuzz from #447 now finds 0 leaks in 150 shapes (10 before). Unit tests only.
- **The list keeps your place** (spec 052 FR-173): when an agent changes status while a row of the list has the keyboard focus, the focus stays on that row instead of falling to the page (J/K kept working only by luck). Checked in the simulator only (Linux).
- **Tools > Activity shows its table again** (spec 052 FR-071 fix, issue #474): two renderer scripts used the same window global, so the activity log table was replaced by the last-activity helpers and the tab failed to render. The table now has its own global (`DurbarActivityTable`); `npm run test:globals` fails when two renderer scripts share a global, and `npm run check:activity-table` opens the tab in the simulator at 1440x900 and 1280x800. `sim:`
- **UI checks no longer flake when windows overlap** (spec 008 FR-103): a simulator test run keeps drawing when another window covers it (the Windows runner runs several at once on one desktop, and a covered window stopped drawing mid-check); `check-question-options` waits for the question itself and prints what it saw on a miss. Reproduced and checked on Linux with four drivers on one display; not yet seen on the Windows runner.
- CI only: the required checks also run on merge queue entries (ready for a merge queue on `main`, an owner setting), the office layout runs in 6 shards instead of 4, and the UI checks on each OS run as three parallel jobs (the macOS one took 15 min alone); every check still runs (spec 008 FR-098..FR-102, T095).

## v0.1.0-alpha.11 ([pre-release](https://github.com/three-feathers/agent-durbar/releases/tag/v0.1.0-alpha.11), 2026-10-22)

- **Help text and UI text that fit** (spec 052 FR-165..FR-171, issue #464): prose wraps between words and is never under 10.5px, help text shares one 560px measure, dialogs have three widths (420, 560, 760px), menus one width (320px) with no one-word last line in a hint, tooltips are at most 120 characters (data tooltips such as the totals, 300 characters and 8 lines), Send to several and Tools > Models and context no longer scroll sideways or cut names without a `title`, Needs you headings give the name its own line, Models and context shows the folder name (not its id), code blocks keep a strip for their Copy button, field labels stay one line with a hint under them, the shortcut sheet aligns its columns, the streaming caret and the Copy button no longer collide, a toast sits above the composer, and the 42 old light-colour rules (chips, notes, code, sub-agent blocks) now use theme tokens, so they read in dark themes. New check `npm run check:ui-text` (in `check:ui`). Checked in the simulator only (Linux, Noto Sans and DejaVu Sans); not on Windows or macOS fonts.
- **Claude Code MCP sign-in:** a failed MCP tool call that shows a sign-in challenge is flagged for the tool card (`mcpAuth`) and refreshes the MCP server list once; the server reads `needs-auth` even if the CLI still says connected; Claude Code's `needs authentication` wording is recognised (spec 058 T096, #364). No visible change until the card step lands.
- **macOS and Windows hand-checks recorded** (spec 001 T024, spec 012 T070; issues #10, #72): the owner uses Durbar daily on macOS and looked at it on a real Windows PC with a colleague on 2026-10-21 (findings #467). Not a scripted check; Codex's sandbox on Windows and macOS stays a known gap. `real:` owner report.
- **Remote control** (spec 054, issue #298): the owner drove a real Copilot remote session on 2026-10-21 (Copilot Pro). The Claude Code channels (Telegram) wizard is out of scope for now and kept as idea #485. `real:` owner report.
- **A folder's own agent setup is shown and used** (spec 061). Hooks, permission rules and MCP servers from a folder's Claude Code, Codex, Copilot and `.mcp.json` files are listed in full when the folder is added, with secrets hidden and broad rules marked. They apply only after you accept them; hooks and broad allow rules (every `Bash` rule included) need an explicit tick, and *Explain with AI* describes each hook. The mode chip shows *+ folder rules*, and a changed file asks again.
- **Codex MCP servers that need sign-in are marked** (spec 058 FR-060, T095/T023, issue #364): a Codex MCP server whose status says it is not logged in, needs authentication or `reauthenticationRequired` reads "Needs sign-in" with an auth error class, and a failed MCP tool call whose error is the server's OAuth challenge marks that server "Needs sign-in" at once (even if the runtime says connected) and makes Durbar re-read the server list, one request at a time. Simulator and unit tests only; not tried on a real Codex.
- **Copilot MCP servers that need sign-in are marked** (spec 058 FR-060/FR-063, T094, issue #364): a failed Copilot MCP tool call whose error is the server's OAuth challenge is flagged for its card (`mcpAuth`) and turns that server to "Needs sign-in" (`errorClass: auth`) until Copilot reports its next status. No UI yet. Unit tests only; not tried on a real account.
- **MCP sign-in, first step** (spec 058 FR-061/FR-062/FR-067, T091/T092/T097, issue #364): an MCP server's OAuth challenge ("MCP OAuth challenge required (HTTP 401)") no longer reads as the agent app's own sign-in error, and every MCP tool card's output after its start (update, complete, failed) is masked in main before the window and the log get it: the session's secret values and token shapes on the whole text first, then a cut to 50,000 characters, then URL keys and flags; failed tool items in a resumed session's history too. No sign-in button yet. Unit tests, mutation-checked; no real session.
- **`main.ts` split into IPC modules** (spec 045 FR-060, T021, #411). The 95 window handlers moved out of `src/main/main.ts` into `src/main/ipc/<area>.ts` (one `register(deps)` each; `main.ts` keeps start-up, windows, stores, adapters and the `register` calls). No behaviour change: same channels, arguments, results and `fromApp(e)` checks. New `npm run test:ipcchannels` checks that every channel the preload calls is registered exactly once, that nothing in `src/main/ipc/` uses raw `ipcMain`, and that the owner-only `fromApp(e)` checks are still there.
- **Faster floor switches** (spec 001 FR-033, finding in PR #457): the office's layout search measures the same boxes with less work (desk drawings left out of the hidden trial layouts, column changes set in place), so switching a floor on or off takes about 140 ms instead of 440-530 ms on a 4-core Linux machine with 15 simulated agents. The chosen layout is the same (54 cases compared box by box). `sim:`
- **Live activity on the desks** (spec 001 FR-028..FR-032, owner request): a working agent's desk shows the latest sentence of its thinking, the tool it runs ("Running npm test") or its reply as it streams, so you no longer open the chat to see what is happening. With few agents across, a fixed panel beside each desk shows a few lines; in a busy office the line under the name shows it (the name stays). At most 4 updates a second, no layout shift, secrets masked with the shared `redact.ts` rules; Settings > General > *Live activity on desks* (on by default) turns it off. Unit tests and a simulator driver (`shots-001-live-activity`) at 1440x900 and 1280x800; not watched on a real session.
- Test tooling only: every simulator driver now takes its helpers (`js`, click, key, page load, screenshot, escaping of values put into page code) from `scripts/driver-helpers.js` instead of its own copy; no check was changed (spec 008 FR-097, T094, #414).
- **Security:** Copilot sessions no longer run a repository's own hooks (`.github/hooks/`, `.github/copilot/settings.json`) unasked. A real check found the Copilot runtime ran them by default, even in Read-only; they stay off until the folder-setup dialog (spec 061) lets you accept them.
- **Fewer Keychain prompts on a Mac** (spec 019 FR-023; owner's report "a ton of allow prompts when I open Durbar"). Durbar decrypted every saved MCP secret value at launch, one Keychain read each, even for servers that never start. Now all values are one encrypted blob that is read at most once per run, only when a server with secrets starts or you change a secret; opening the app or Settings reads nothing. Saved files are converted on that first read. "Always Allow" still has to be given again after each update while builds are unsigned (#4); Copilot's own Keychain items can also ask. Unit tests with a counting fake; not run on a real Mac.
- **MCP sign-ins kept between Copilot sessions** (spec 058 FR-070, owner's Q12 B; owner's report on Windows). Copilot sessions now ask the runtime to keep an MCP server's OAuth sign-in in the system keychain (Credential Manager on Windows, Keychain on macOS) instead of the SDK default, memory only, which dropped it when the session ended. One sign-in now serves every Durbar Copilot session; Durbar never reads it. The trust prompt for a remote per-folder extra says a sign-in may outlive the session (FR-076). On a Mac the runtime's Keychain items can ask once each (not counted on a real Mac). Checked for real on GitHub's Windows and Linux runners with a demo OAuth server (T116, 0 prompts): a new session in a new runtime connected with no new sign-in; on Linux without a keyring the sign-in is a tokens file under `~/.copilot/mcp-oauth-config/` (encryption and file permissions not checked). Not checked on a Mac or with a third-party server; sharing with a sign-in made in the Copilot CLI is not verified (T105).

- **+ Folder and + Workspace are back in plain sight** (spec 052 FR-164, owner request): two labelled buttons at the left end of the top bar, first in tab order, instead of the **+ New** menu; **Ctrl+O** / **Ctrl+Shift+O** press them, and on a Mac a **File** menu has **Add Folder…** (⌘O), **Add VS Code Workspace…** (⇧⌘O) and **Close Window**. Same code path as the buttons. To keep one row, the counters keep their words from 1920px, the Defaults chip shows only the mode below 1600px, and the app name gives way to its mark when the row would wrap. Simulator and unit tests on Linux; not run on a real Mac or Windows PC.
- **A session comes back in its own permission mode, and says so** (spec 003, 2026-10-21 delta; owner's report "a session I had set to Allow all came back in Read-only"). Durbar remembered one mode per folder, so after a restart every session of a folder took the mode last chosen in *any* of its sessions, and a session resumed from *Previous sessions* took the mode of the session you opened the list from, both without a word. Now each session's last mode is kept (ids and modes only, newest 50 per folder) and restored; the chat says which mode it is in and why, an Allow all shows *restored* on the chip, and a resumed session in another mode than its opener is logged in the activity log. Unit tests and a simulator check (`shots-003-mode-back`); not run on a real session.
- **A waiting question stays in sight, and compactions no longer pile up** (spec 052 FR-162, FR-163). While an agent's question is scrolled out of view, the answer bar above the message box shows it, and a short one is answered there (choices, an "Other" box, Skip; **Show question** goes to the card). Compactions in a row are one line that updates in place ("Context compacted 4 times · last 10:21"); before, a chat that was not open kept a "Compacting the context…" line for every compaction. Simulator-checked (`npm run check:question-visible`).
- **Plan first** (spec 066, #429): in the chat's mode menu, *Plan first (next message)* makes the agent propose a plan in its app's own plan mode (Copilot, Claude Code, Codex) before it changes anything; *Approve*, *Revise…* or *Reject* in the chat. The approved plan still runs in the folder's permission mode.
- **The question card shows all its text, and Enter sends a custom answer** (spec 052 FR-160, FR-161). An agent's question, its choices and their descriptions are no longer cut with "…" (the limits went from 300/80/200 to 2,000/300/1,000 characters; an MCP form's message from 500 to 2,000); each choice shows its description under its label; the "Other" box grows with what you type, typing in it chooses it, Enter sends the card and Shift+Enter adds a line (an empty Enter sends nothing). `npm run check:question-options` checks it at 1440x900 and 1280x800 (simulator only; not tried with a real agent question or a screen reader).
- **Undo an agent's file changes** (spec 065, #431): after a turn that edited files, **Undo these changes…** in the chat restores them after a preview and your confirmation; it refuses if a file changed since, and the undo can itself be undone. Claude Code and Copilot checked on real sessions.
- **End-to-end checks with real accounts in CI** (spec 062): on Linux, macOS and Windows, a workflow installs Claude Code, Copilot and Codex the way Durbar does, signs in with the CI account, lists models and runs one tiny turn. First run: 9 of 9. It found and fixed: Codex signed in with an API key was shown as signed out; Codex installed by OpenAI's Windows installer was not found; a plain message (and a *Change mode…* button) when Codex's sandbox cannot start.
- One shared `PATH` reader (`src/shared/pathdirs.ts`) for agent detection and "Open in VS Code": the VS Code launcher now finds a quoted Windows `PATH` entry, and repeated `PATH` folders are tried once (spec 060 FR-094, #412).
- **Packaged display names (spec 008 FR-040..FR-045, T039, issue #384).** A packaged Windows build sets its AppUserModelId (`build.appId`), so toasts and the taskbar match the Start menu shortcut; the Mac app menu item carries an `Agent Durbar` label (macOS takes the menu-bar title from the bundle name, `CFBundleName` = `Agent Durbar`, now checked on every Mac build); the Linux AppImage launcher entry no longer forces `--no-sandbox` (`AppRun` still adds it when needed). `scripts/check-names.js` runs after the fuse check on every package build, and the installed-app CI asserts the names a user sees. Checked on Linux (unit tests, `dist:linux`, the installed AppImage on the simulator); Windows and macOS names are asserted in CI (Installed app run 39561893292, all three OSes), not seen on a real PC or Mac.

- **Install agent apps without Homebrew or Node** (spec 060 FR-001). Settings > Agent apps now offers each vendor's official installer first (Claude Code, Copilot CLI on macOS/Linux, Codex): downloaded only from the vendor's own hosts after you confirm, run as you, into your home folder, no administrator rights. Routes are ordered least privilege first; winget and Homebrew say they may ask for admin.
- The simulator's Agent apps screen now shows the real install options of each OS (winget on Windows; a new Mac or Linux desktop without Homebrew or Node gets the vendor page), instead of Homebrew everywhere. README: the Linux AppImage needs FUSE 2 on Ubuntu 22.04+, MCP secrets need a desktop keyring, and the Copilot CLI needs PowerShell 6+ (spec 060).
- **UI checks on macOS, Windows and Linux in CI** (spec 008 FR-090..FR-092). A new workflow runs theme geometry, the 15-agent office layout, the Mac title bar and every asserting simulator driver (`npm run check:ui`, new) on all three runners and keeps their screenshots; before, these ran on Linux only. A failing driver now exits 1.
- **MCP read tools mask shell strings, quoted headers and paths, and more shapes** (spec 041 T064, #336). An arg with spaces (`sh -c`, `cmd /c`, `powershell -Command`) is masked word by word, quoted words stay whole, `Cookie: a=1; sid=x`, `Name:` then its value, comma pairs, JSON `name`/`value` pairs and `$env:NAME='v'` are masked, and quoted paths and paths in shell strings are reduced to `…/<base name>`; no regex in the masking is quadratic on a long run of one character (linearity rows in the tests).
- **MCP read tools mask the secret after an auth scheme word, and escaped name/value objects** (spec 041 T066, #375). `--auth Bearer SEC`, `-H "Authorization: Bearer" SEC` and a bare `Bearer SEC` mask `SEC` and keep the scheme word; `{\"name\":\"token\",\"value\":\"x\"}` inside a shell string masks the value.
- **MCP read tools bound the cost of masking** (spec 041 T065, #374). A command, arg or url over 12 288 characters is shown as `[redacted]` without being masked or cut. One tool call masks at most 48 000 characters, at most 250 server entries and about 900 env or header key names (about 200 typical servers fit with no note); past that, later values are `[redacted]`, later servers are left out, and a top-level `masking` note says so. Hostile CLI files that took seconds on the main process (250k tiny args 1.5 s, 25k entries 13 s) now measure about 200 ms per call or less. `maskSecretArgs` and `maskSecretCommand` apply the same 12 288-character limit, so a 1 MB arg is hidden in under 1 ms there too. Unit tests only.
- **Rename a session (spec 059, issue #372).** **Rename…** in the chat header's More menu and the card menu (and the Previous sessions row menu): a name of your own, 1 to 60 characters, shown on every surface instead of the first prompt, kept in Durbar's folder file and also saved in the agent app (Copilot through a prompt-free resume for a session that is not open, Codex `thread/name/set`, Claude Code `--name` at the next message), with the dialog saying where it goes and that it may sync. Fakes, a guard against unescaped names in every template and a simulator check (`npm run check:rename`) on card, list, header, strip, Schedules and Previous rows (the inbox and notifications were read, not rendered). Vikram's agent ran the raw app calls and CLI lists by hand on real accounts; Durbar's adapters ran on fakes only; VS Code was not checked.

## v0.1.0-alpha.10 ([pre-release](https://github.com/three-feathers/agent-durbar/releases/tag/v0.1.0-alpha.10), 2026-10-21)

- Agent apps installed with nvm, fnm, Volta, asdf, mise or Linuxbrew are now found, also when Durbar is opened from Finder or a desktop menu (the login shell's `PATH` is read once); Settings > Agent apps says where each was found (spec 060 FR-003).
- `npm run check:office` no longer fails at random with "a walking figure is not on a slot": it waits (up to 3 s) for a figure that is still walking and fails only if it never arrives (spec 024, #406).
- Reasoning effort **none** (offered by GPT-6 Luna and the Codex models) can be chosen and is kept; before, the menu offered it but the switch refused it (spec 007 FR-008).
- **App name and profile folder (spec 008 FR-033..FR-036, issue #353).** The Mac menu says About/Hide/Quit Agent Durbar through explicit labels; the app's identity (`agent-durbar`, so the Keychain item and saved MCP secrets) does not change, there is no `app.setName`. And the profile folder is chosen in code: an installed app keeps `agent-durbar` (nothing moves), while `npm start` and the simulator now use `agent-durbar-dev`, so a dev run no longer shares the installed app's data. To keep old dev data, run once with `DURBAR_USER_DATA=<old folder>` or copy it by hand. Unit tests and a simulator run on Linux; the dev folder and an earlier menu version were seen on a real Mac (Paco, #377), the menu, dev folder and Keychain checked on a real Mac by Paco (dev run); a packaged Mac build and Windows not run.
- **Send to several sessions** (spec 064): one message to the running sessions you pick (top bar ⋯ or Ctrl+K), each with its own mode and spending limit; busy sessions can't be picked.
- **Snappier with long chats and a busy office** (spec 052 FR-142..FR-145): a 1000-message conversation renders about 20× faster (it used to slow down with every message), the office updates only the room that changed, and the context chip keeps keyboard focus. New `npm run check:perf` measures it.
- **Folder status in the chat** (spec 063): the folder chip shows the git branch and how many files changed; a click lists the changed files with their +/− lines. Read-only.
- A GitHub account without Copilot access no longer shows as "Signed in (checked with GitHub)": the card now says the account has no Copilot access yet and how to turn it on (spec 012 FR-156).
- Packaged builds refuse debuggers: Electron fuses turn off `ELECTRON_RUN_AS_NODE`, `NODE_OPTIONS` and `--inspect`, the app always removes `remote-debugging-port` and `remote-debugging-pipe` (any spelling), `npm run pack:dir` and the `dist*` scripts fail if a fuse is wrong in the packed Linux, macOS or Windows binary (so the packaged-check workflow and the release build check it), and `npm run check:fuses-launch` probes the packed Linux app (spec 008 FR-037..FR-039, #355). Run locally on Linux only; macOS and Windows not tried by hand.
- **Continue a conversation in another agent app** (#409, spec 030 FR-042..FR-045): **More > Continue in another app…** starts a session of another ready app (Claude Code, Codex or Copilot) in the same folder from a reviewed summary that says where it came from. Checked on real accounts.
- **Copilot spending limits now stop the agent** (spec 011 FR-070..FR-074). Copilot's runtime does not stop at the limit (checked on a real Pro session), so Durbar does: a turn that crosses it is stopped and a message sent over it is held, with the usual *Raise limit / Remove limit / Stop here* choice.
- **Copilot model switching checked on a paid account** (spec 030 T041, T044, T045): switching between two real models, effort, the long-context tier and *Compact and switch* work. The model menu now shows the window the runtime really allows (for example GPT-6 Luna 272k, not 1M), Copilot prices count as credits (so the switch dialog can estimate the cost), and "not verified on a real account" is gone for Copilot. Also found: Copilot's runtime does not stop at the session credit limit (spec 011).
- **Remote control for Claude Code sessions** (spec 054 FR-060..FR-064): **More > Control from Claude (web and mobile)** turns on Claude Code's Remote Control for one session, with a claude.ai link; it is turned back on after a relaunch. Copilot remote control needs a paid plan: it started on Copilot Pro (real check), and the "not started" message says so.
- Agents read every review and comment before a merge: new rule in `AGENTS.md`, and `scripts/pr-gate.sh <N>` (verdict only) checks requested changes, open threads, unanswered comments, checks, CodeQL and alerts (spec 021).
- **Switch the model inside Claude Code and Codex sessions** (spec 030 FR-038..FR-041). The model menu in a Claude or Codex chat now changes the model and effort from the next message and keeps the conversation (Claude relaunches with `--resume`, Codex sends them with the next turn). Checked on real accounts: Haiku to Sonnet in one Claude session, `gpt-5.6-luna` to `gpt-6.1-sol` in one Codex thread.
- Claude Code sessions started in Durbar now appear in the Claude CLI's `claude --resume` list, by name if they have one (they were hidden because the CLI leaves out sessions it marks as SDK runs; spec 012 FR-159). Sessions started in the CLI were already in Durbar's Previous sessions.
- Team rules for agents written into `AGENTS.md` and its mirrors: review findings are fixed in the PR (no non-blocking notes), every review checks for an existing capability, re-approval by patch id, when admin merge is fine, OKs in the team chat, working hours; new `docs/agent-notes.md` with what agents learned, so knowledge is shared in the repo (spec 021).
- Review sweep (owner rule 2026-10-20: review findings are fixed, not left as notes): MCP server errors are now masked once for every path out of main, including the `DURBAR_LOG` debug file (spec 019 FR-022); the session search accent fold uses `\u` escapes (spec 006); spec 058 requires the sign-in confirm to show where the server came from and to warn when the sign-in page is on another site, and the extras trust text to say a sign-in may stay in the keychain (FR-075, FR-076).
- **Grant chips grouped (spec 052 FR-141, issue #367).** With three or more "Allowed for this session" permissions the chat header shows one **Allowed (N)** chip that opens a list (a revoke button per row, **Revoke all** with a confirmation); one or two stay as single chips. Labels drop a tool-name prefix that repeats the server name (`server-x › GetItem`), the full name stays in the tooltip. The header chips flow on one or two rows. New `npm run check:grant-chips`, `npm run test:grantlabel` and a `simgrants=many` simulator fixture.
- **Pin, Hide and Delete in Previous sessions (spec 006 FR-024..FR-032, issue #368).** Each row gets a **⋯** menu: **Pin to top**, **Hide** (reversible, with **Show hidden (N)**) and, for Copilot and Codex sessions, **Delete…** through the agent app's own API (red confirmation with Cancel focused; Claude Code: disabled with the reason; VS Code chats: pin and hide only). A session that still has a card in the office is refused. Pins and hides are ids only in `durbar-folders.json`; `sessions:delete` is reachable only from the window (a test pins that MCP tools, remote control and schedules cannot call it); session ids pass a strict allow-list (no `/`, backslash, `.`, `:`); the activity log gets a *Session deleted* row without the title. Checked on the simulator, a fake adapter, a fake Copilot client and a fake Codex app-server; **no real delete was run** (`[KNOWN GAP]`).
- Schedules dialog: the **Send to** and **Limit** labels no longer wrap mid-word or sit under their select, and the session's name is shown on a line under **Send to** (full name in its tooltip) instead of inside the select option, where it was cut; one shared, unit-tested text cutter (`cuttext.js`, `npm run test:cuttext`) now serves the file-name, notification and schedule-row texts too; new `npm run check:sched-sendto` (spec 044 FR-020, FR-021, issue #371).
- The options of an agent's question card wrap instead of ending in "…", and each one's tooltip shows its label plus the description; new `npm run check:question-options` and a `simquestion=long` simulator fixture (spec 052 FR-140, issue #365; simulator only, to try on a real Mac).
- An MCP server's error text is masked in main (session secret values, tokens, URL keys and `user:pass@`, `--token value`) before it reaches the window, in the MCP list and in the live status events, then cut to 300 characters (spec 019 FR-021, issue #360; unit tests, not seen on a real server error).
- **+ New in the chat header (spec 052 FR-139, issue #363).** A **+ New** button sits next to Previous in a chat (named "New session in <folder>"), so a new session no longer hides under More. It is a third visible header action by owner decision; **+ New session** stays in More and in every other path. Simulator only: `check:office` checks it at 1440x900 and 1280x800, Simple and Expert; not tried on a Mac.
- Read-only wording matches what was seen: *Start my day* and the Read-only help now say an MCP tool runs in Read-only only if its server marks it read-only; otherwise choose Ask first (spec 044).
- **Round 11 of the usability pass: one-row top bar and thinner walls (spec 052 FR-105..FR-107).** The top bar is one 48px row instead of two (about 92px): **+ New** holds Add folder and Add VS Code workspace, one **Defaults** chip ("Copilot · Read-only · Auto") opens the agent app, mode and model for new sessions, the folder filter has **Manage folders** next to it, and the counters show a dot and a number on narrower screens. The office gets the height. Room walls, lobby places and helper lines are 1px. Measured in headless Chromium on the simulator page at 1280 to 1800px; not run in Electron.
- Real-account checks (2026-10-20, about 1 AI credit and $0.004): the Copilot AI theme maker now reads token names written without `--` (Copilot wrote them that way, so every theme failed); remote control that Copilot answers "not started" is no longer shown as on; Copilot's refused-sign-in error at Send is now recognised; a folder's `.claude` hooks and `.mcp.json` confirmed not to run in Claude; read-only MCP tools confirmed to run in Read-only on Copilot (specs 012, 040, 044, 054).
- Idea research: issues #128, #130, #131, #132 and #289 answered with recommendations; a draft spec 057 (briefing follow-through: safe mail reads and proposed actions in the inbox) and a research note in spec 048 (most of the office assistant is already covered by specs 041, 052 and 054). Specs only, no code.
- **Mac: the title bar on every screen (spec 052 FR-138, #345).** A notice strip over an open chat is now the drag row with room for the traffic lights; dialogs, the palette, popovers and menus can be clicked (they no longer sit over a drag area); the chat strip keeps a 32px grab handle with grip dots on its right, and the agent strip fades at the edge that is cut off while it can scroll (the fade and the handle size are not yet seen on a real Mac); the top bar reserves the traffic-light room on its first row only; Settings or Tools over a chat keep their centred header. Mac-only CSS, new `npm run check:mac-titlebar` (simulator geometry, not run on a real Mac).

- Sign-in is checked with the service where the app allows it (Copilot: GitHub quota; Codex: OpenAI rate limits; Claude Code: local only), and a chat message refused because of the sign-in shows a calm card with **Sign in** and **Send again** instead of the raw 401 (spec 012 FR-156, FR-157; fakes and simulator, not yet on real accounts).
- After a sign-in, Copilot's shared runtime is restarted so it uses the new login; open Copilot sessions reconnect on their own ("Reconnecting Copilot…") and keep their permissions and grants (spec 012 FR-158; fakes only, not yet on a real account).

## v0.1.0-alpha.9 ([pre-release](https://github.com/three-feathers/agent-durbar/releases/tag/v0.1.0-alpha.9), 2026-10-20)

Unsigned and not notarized; only Linux was run by hand. **Steer a Copilot session from GitHub web and mobile** (remote control, behind a native confirmation, never in Allow all). **Scheduled prompts Phase 2**: weekdays and weekly rules, a daylight-saving fix, per-session schedules, a per-schedule limit. **MCP forms and URL requests** in the chat. **AI themes with Copilot.** **A new website** with a landing page and a docs page. And five more rounds of usability reviews by AI personas (rounds 6 to 10): truthful status and diffs, an Activity log, motion with meaning, plain words for people who don't code, house rules, macOS conventions, a remembered window and sessions, and a less bulky layout. Every IPC call now checks where it comes from.

- **Mac: the window can be moved from an open chat (spec 052 FR-085 fix).** With a chat open the top bar is hidden; the agent strip now is the drag area and leaves room for the traffic lights, so they no longer cover the Office button. The same for Settings, Tools and a folder screen opened from a chat. Found on a real Mac; unit test only, not re-checked on the Mac yet.
- **MCP read tools cap every string at 4096 characters** (spec 041 T054, #277). The cut comes after the masking, at a word boundary, and ends with ` [truncated]`.
- **Round 10 of the usability pass: chrome density and type (spec 052 FR-095..FR-099).** The chat header is one row with 28px chips and buttons (about half its old height), **Previous (N)** replaces the Previous sessions bar, the agent strip is 48px and the composer hint is one short line; inbox rows are about 90 to 100px with a small **Open** on the heading line; Settings and Tools sit in an 880px column with tighter sections and a fluid agent app grid; one type scale (16/14/13/12/11) and spacing scale (4/8/12/16/24) for the chrome; an optional **Spacing: Compact** in Settings > Detail level. The office, the list and the top bar keep their sizes. Not seen in a running window yet (CSS read, `npm test`).
- **Round 10 of the usability pass: office and tables (spec 052 FR-100..FR-104).** Flat desk cards with one hairline and no shadow, a 3px edge for Waiting, Error and Failing, and a thinner selection ring; Working, Thinking, Idle and Done are a dot and a word (only statuses that need you keep a filled pill); the card tooltip names the role; the top-bar counters are one segmented strip; the list and the Tools tables lose their frames and fills, with right-aligned tabular numbers, plain-text "Used by" and thinner bars. No size changes. Checked with `npm test`; not run in Electron.
- **Round 9 of the usability pass: pre-release fixes (spec 052 FR-092..FR-094).** Deny in the inbox is never pushed out by a long session button; the Thinking block is full width with no inner scroll box; "Failing" shows in the palette, chat strip and list; Cost and Tool usage both say **This session** / **All time**; agent app cards and the list's Agent app column no longer cut words; clearer mode menu lines, a calmer "What Durbar does not do", an AI theme cost line for the picked app only, and in Simple "No spending limit" and a memory chip only from 70% full. Checked with unit tests and by reading the code; not run in the app.
- **Round 8 of the usability pass: daily use (spec 052 FR-087..FR-091).** Durbar remembers the window's place and size, Office or List, the folder filter and the open chat; every open session of a folder (up to 6) comes back after a restart, not only the last; after more than 30 minutes closed a **Since you were away** strip lists missed scheduled prompts with **Run missed now**; quitting while agents work asks first, and **End all sessions** is in the top bar's ⋯ and Ctrl+K; Ctrl+K starts with your 5 recent chats and **Ctrl+`** goes back to the previous one. Unit tests only; not run in Electron.
- **Round 8 of the usability pass: macOS conventions (spec 052 FR-083..FR-086).** On a Mac: an app menu with **Settings…** (⌘,), Edit, View (zoom and full screen only), Window and Help; shortcuts use and show ⌘ (⌘K, ⇧⌘A, ⇧⌘N; ⌥⌘A/S/D/N in a chat); closing the window hides it and agents keep running (⌘Q quits); the title bar is hidden and the top bar is the drag area; one Dock bounce when an agent needs you in the background. Windows and Linux lose the default menu (Reload, DevTools). Native controls follow a dark theme. Unit tests (`test:macos`); not run on a real Mac.
- **Every IPC call checks where it comes from (spec 045 FR-008, #332).** The main process answers only the app's own page in its main frame, for every handler, through one helper; a test fails if a handler is registered without it.

- **A new project website (spec 050, FR-006..FR-010).** A short landing page (what Durbar is, the honest alpha status right under the download button, the six themes, what each agent app was checked on, safety, downloads with first-run steps) and a separate docs page with the whole README and a table of contents. The curated text in `docs/site/landing.json` is checked against the README on every build.
- **Make a theme with AI on Copilot (spec 040 FR-015, T026, #301).** Copilot is offered next to Claude Code and Codex: one prompt on a throw-away session with no tools, no MCP servers, no skills or custom agents, every permission denied, a 3-minute limit, and the session deleted afterwards; the AI credits it used are shown with the result. Unit tests on a fake SDK only: not run on a real account yet.
- **Round 7 of the usability pass: consistency and readability (spec 052, FR-076..FR-079).** One set of house rules, written down in `docs/design-rules.md`: two control heights and one Copy size, red for buttons that delete or end and amber only for those that grant power, "…" only before a step that asks for input, one word per status everywhere ("Waiting", "Idle"; also in the Durbar MCP server), one chevron for every expand arrow, and calmer hover, disabled and focus states. Easier reading: the Thinking block is no longer italic, the mode menu has one short line per mode, install options lead with Homebrew or npm, chat header chips say "Model:" and "Limit: none", list headers are in sentence case, and the inbox names its keys. Checked with `npm test`; not run in Electron.
- **Round 7 of the usability pass: plain words for people who don't code (spec 052 FR-080..FR-082).** The welcome card asks **Do you write code?** (**Yes, show me the details** / **No, keep it simple**). In Simple, a request reads "Ada wants to change companies.ts" ("in tools" in small text) or "Ada wants to run a command" with the command under **Show the command**, the same in the inbox, the answer bar and notifications; the chat shows each tool step in plain words ("Read companies.ts", "Ran a command"), the header says "Memory 74% full" and **Tidy up memory**, costs show money only, and Control from GitHub, Models and context and token counts are hidden. Expert is unchanged. `npm test` only; not run in Electron.
- **Remote control and notifications, hardened after a security review (spec 054 FR-011).** Turning on remote control from GitHub now also asks in a native window the page cannot answer; it is refused in Allow all (and Allow all is refused while it is on); it is turned off on GitHub before a session ends, is removed or the app quits; on and off are in the activity log. OS notifications name only what is needed ("Asks for your OK"), never the command.

- **MCP servers can ask for input (spec 012 FR-151..FR-155, #290).** When an MCP server asks for a small form (text, number, yes/no, a choice) or for a step on a web page, the chat shows it in the question card with **Submit / Decline / Cancel**, instead of declining. Secret-looking fields are password inputs, main checks every answer before the agent gets it, and a page opens in your browser only after you click, only if it is https. Copilot, Claude Code and Codex; checked on the simulator and fake harnesses only, not yet with a real MCP server.
- **Round 6 of the usability pass: truth and density (spec 052, FR-062..FR-066).** A working agent whose last 3 tool calls failed reads **Failing** (waiting colours) on its card, in the list and in the chat header, and every card, list row and chat header says when it last did something. An open tool call shows the whole command with **Copy**, how long it took and up to 20000 characters of output; an edit request from Claude Code (and the demo) has a collapsed **Show the change** diff on its card and in the inbox. The list has 34px rows, **Agent app**, **Last activity** and **Cost** columns, sorting by any column and J/K. Cards in an Allow all folder say **Allow all** in red, and Settings and the README list **What Durbar does not do**.
- **Round 6 of the usability pass: motion with meaning (spec 052, FR-067..FR-070).** Desk scenes cross-fade when an agent changes state, the done tick draws in and the pill pulses once, the waiting badge pings three times then rests, an inbox answer fades and the rows below slide up, the Needs you count rolls, the ambulance and siren breathe slowly for about 10 s instead of strobing red and blue, a theme change cross-fades, and an empty office shows an empty desk. Transform and opacity only; reduced motion stops all of it and Serious mode stops every looping animation. Checked with `npm test`; not run in Electron.
- **Round 6 of the usability pass: activity log and Windows first run (spec 052 FR-071..FR-075).** Tools > **Activity** lists every permission answer, automatic approval, mode change and spending-limit change (newest 5000 kept on this computer, actions only, never prompts or file contents) with **Export CSV / JSON** and **Clear…**. On Windows, CLIs installed with winget are found, the Copilot card hints at PowerShell 7, and the README explains the download, the file to pick and the hash check. A Read-only denial offers **Switch this agent to Ask first**, the Copilot sign-in says it is separate from VS Code's, the limit stop says **Raise limit**, and Cost says Copilot plans have a fixed price. Unit tests and simulator only; not run on Windows.
- **Scheduled prompts, Phase 2 (spec 044 FR-012..FR-018, #291).** Calendar rules (**weekdays at**, **weekly on** chosen days), and *every day at* no longer drifts an hour across daylight saving. A schedule can target **one session** (it pauses when that session ends) and have its own **limit** in AI credits or dollars (it pauses at the limit). Copilot's own session schedules are shown read-only. Unit tests and the simulator; no real check yet.
- **Control a Copilot session from GitHub (spec 054 Phase 1, #298).** More > *Control from GitHub (web and mobile)* turns on Copilot's remote control for one session after a dialog that says what is sent to GitHub, then shows the link (Copy link, Open on GitHub) and a *Remote* chip; *Stop remote control* turns it off. Never on by default; only `https://github.com` links are opened. Simulator and unit tests only: not run on a real account yet. Claude channels and Codex mobile are specified as planned research.

## v0.1.0-alpha.8 ([pre-release](https://github.com/three-feathers/agent-durbar/releases/tag/v0.1.0-alpha.8), 2026-10-20)

Unsigned and not notarized; only Linux was run by hand. **Set up agent apps inside Durbar**: install, update and sign in to Copilot, Claude Code and Codex from Settings > Agent apps, with a native confirmation that shows the exact program before anything runs. **Five rounds of usability reviews by AI personas** (about 20) reshaped the app: the "Needs you" inbox and answer bar, Simple and Expert views, one word per concept, calmer visuals with period textures in the themes, High Contrast and focus support, OS notifications and Alt+A/S/D answers. Also new: **Make a theme with AI**, a Cost view per folder and the project website.

- **Round 5 of the usability pass: visual and accessibility (spec 052, FR-052..FR-055).** Windows High Contrast support, a two-tone focus ring (dark and yellow in Retro Pixel), stronger control borders, disabled items readable instead of faded, more contrast when the system asks for it, and dialogs that fit the window. Primary buttons are solid in every theme, Tools and Settings are quieter, themed rooms get a faint period texture, and Settings > Agent apps opened from the welcome card no longer scrolls the top bar away.
- **Round 5 of the usability pass: plain words for newcomers (spec 052 FR-056..FR-058).** The welcome card says **Connect your AI account once** (ChatGPT through Codex, Claude through Claude Code, or GitHub Copilot); agent app cards name the account; the install dialog says what Durbar does in one sentence, with the exact command under *Show the exact command*. In Simple, a limit stop reads "Paused: reached the spending limit you set (about $0.30)", rate-limit errors say the AI service is busy, and a folder grant reads "Let Ada change files in “tools” until the session ends". The restore strip says "One folder (…) lets agents change files without asking" with **Make it ask me first** / **Keep as it is**, and the spending limit dialog says plans have a fixed price and Durbar never charges you.
- **Round 5 of the usability pass: signals for heavy use (spec 052, FR-059..FR-061).** The window title shows how many agents need you ("(3) Agent Durbar"), the app icon gets a badge (a taskbar flash on Windows), and one notification, at most every 10 s, says who needs you while Durbar is in the background (name and a short summary only; Settings > Notifications, off in the demo). In a waiting chat, Alt+A / Alt+S / Alt+D answer and Alt+N goes to the next agent. The inbox lists the oldest request first and shows how long each has waited.
- **One word per concept (spec 052 FR-051).** Copilot, Claude Code and Codex are **agent apps** (Settings > Agent apps); Copilot custom agents are **profiles**; permissions say **Allow once / Allow “…” for this session / Deny** in Simple and Expert; caps are **spending limits** and money is **cost**; the demo is **Demo**; the office says **folder** (the + Workspace button is only for VS Code .code-workspace files); End session and **Remove from office…** say what happens; mood words are plainer ("Context almost full (95%)", "Waiting 3 min for you").
- **Set up agent apps inside the app (spec 051, #299).** Settings > Agent apps shows Copilot, Claude Code and Codex with their state and one next step: **Install…**, **Update…** or **Sign in…**. Durbar runs only fixed package-manager commands (winget, Homebrew, npm), shown before they run, and never a downloaded script. Sign-in uses the vendor's own flow: Durbar shows the link and the one-time code, and never sees a password or stores a token. The welcome card starts here.
- **Agent app setup, hardened after a security review (spec 051 FR-004b/FR-004c).** An install or update now asks once more in a native window that shows the exact program (its real path) and arguments; the page alone can no longer start one. Only the app's own page may call setup; world-writable programs are not run on macOS/Linux; winget uses its own source; the sign-in code and any OAuth values are kept out of the output lines.
- **Round 4 of the usability pass: interaction polish (spec 052, FR-047..FR-050).** Buttons look pressed, disabled and busy (Stop, End session, Open in VS Code, inbox answers and Resume show a spinner while they wait). An answered inbox row shows "✓ Allowed" or "Denied" before it folds away, the empty inbox says "All clear", and closing the inbox returns focus to where you were. Dialogs ease in, install plans are cards, copy buttons say "Copied ✓", toasts are marked green or red, and an empty Tools > Cost view says so.
- **Round 4 of the usability pass (spec 052, FR-045/FR-046).** Agent app cards say which plan each one uses and the welcome card says which to pick; hints point to Settings > Agent apps instead of a terminal; a finished install goes straight to the sign-in; the restore strip's main button is readable in every theme; in the demo each waiting agent asks for its own thing and no agent in Allow all shows a permission card.
- **Round 3 of the usability pass (spec 052, five new personas).**
  - **Inbox:** it opens on a request, never on a button, and every button is described by its request. After an answer, focus moves to the next request and the outcome is announced. Keys: J/K, A, D, S, O.
  - **Announcements:** new requests are announced to screen readers.
  - **Shortcuts:** Ctrl+Shift+A (inbox) and Ctrl+Shift+N (next agent) work everywhere.
  - **Confirmations:** destructive ones start on Cancel.
  - **Simple:** tool steps fold, approvals say "Allow this once" / "Don't allow", and the top bar is lighter.
  - **The restore strip** says what the risk is, and offers "Ask me first (safer)".
  - **Cards** name their harness when several are in use.
  - **Visual pass:** a solid primary button, one segmented status bar, quieter room controls and lobby, status edges on waiting and error cards.
  - **Japanese court:** Tanmatsu-shitsu, Dōgu-beya, the Ō-hiroma, fusuma doors, a bonsai, and plain short hair.
- **Cost per folder (spec 052 FR-044, #315).** *Tools > Cost* lists each folder's ≈ money, Copilot AIU, Claude estimate, tokens and calls, for *This run* or *Since <date>*, with **Copy as CSV**. Clicking a room's cost opens it.
- **The usability follow-ups, built (spec 052, #305-#311).**
  - **Needs you inbox:** every request in one queue, answered with each request's own choices; press **I**.
  - **Command palette:** **Ctrl+K** (Cmd+K) finds an agent, a folder or an action by typing; **?** lists every keyboard shortcut (#316).
  - **Cost in money:** "≈ $0.36" in the header, rooms and cards, with AIU and Claude estimates in the tooltip.
  - **Simple / Expert:** a detail level in Settings and on the welcome card. Simple uses plain verbs ("Running a command") and hides developer details.
  - **Settings:** themes as a preview grid, one lead sentence per section, MCP under *Advanced*.
  - **Controls:** one size scale, and 24px room-header targets.
  - **Themes:** Corporate bevels, quieter Indian pillars, a lacquer Japanese desk with tatami, and a crisp Retro floor.
- **Round 2 of the usability pass (spec 052, four new personas).**
  - **Restored Allow all:** a strip under the top bar names the folders, with **Switch to Ask first** and **Keep**.
  - **Answer bar:** it never cuts a request next to Approve, and shows "1 of N", oldest first.
  - **Switching to Allow all** no longer approves waiting requests by default.
  - **Wording and glyphs:** Allow all is an open padlock in the warning ink. Session approvals name their scope. The More menu explains Stop, End and Remove.
  - **Office cards** show ⚠ when an agent is stuck or nearly out of memory.
  - **Settings read easily:** themes are a grid of cards, each with a mini room in its own colours. Each section leads with one sentence, the rest is behind *More*. MCP sits in a closed *Advanced* group.
  - **Themes:** Retro and Corporate are retuned, and the Japanese and European courts use place names from their own culture. Clean Office uses soft shadows, one radius scale and tabular numbers.
- **Calmer and clearer, from four persona reviews (spec 052).**
  - **Answering:** a waiting agent's request is pinned above the message box when its card scrolls away, with the same Approve / Deny buttons. *Previous sessions* no longer opens over a conversation. **N** jumps to the next agent that needs you.
  - **Chat header:** Open in VS Code, Stop and a **More** menu, with End and Remove last.
  - **Permissions:** the "restored in Allow all" notice stays until you close it, in plain words. The top bar's mode says **New: Read-only**, because it is the default for new sessions.
  - **Status and wording:** status counts have shapes (◆ waiting, ▲ error) and spoken labels. Muted text and the focus ring meet contrast. Plain words replace "fainted", "dizzy" and "credits not reported", and themed places keep their plain names in tooltips.
  - **Fixed:** the List view's header row nodded along with the cartoon heads.
- **Floors: clearer "all floors off" message (spec 033 FR-013, #133).** The top-bar *Floors n/m* button shows only with two or more floors; with a single floor that is switched off, the office now says "Your only floor is off. Switch it on in Settings > Floors." instead of pointing to a button that is not there. Top-bar screenshots taken before the Floors change were retaken.
- **Make a theme with AI (spec 040 FR-014..FR-021, #182).** Describe a look, or pick one of the ideas, and Claude Code or Codex designs a light or dark theme: every colour, a style, place names and art for the people and furniture. Only safe values are kept, art goes through the same sanitiser as imported packs, and readable text is checked; up to 3 passes fix what fails. Try it on the whole app before you save it.
- **Fixed: Export the active theme** (spec 040 FR-019). It exports the theme that is active now. On a built-in theme it explains why there is nothing to export, and the export picker can create a new folder.
- **Faster checks that keep the computer usable** (spec 008 FR-028..FR-031). The full office layout check runs in Clean Office, and a one-minute **theme geometry** check proves every other theme lays out identically (every box and font size) and passes the layout rules in its own fonts. The full check in every theme runs on demand. Locally, `npm run check:themes` runs a few checks at a time, at low priority, and stops a job when memory runs low. Animation is checked once, in the plain office check. `npm test` leaves a core free.
- **A project website** at <https://three-feathers.github.io/agent-durbar/> (spec 050, #293). It is generated from the README, the specs index, screenshots, the CHANGELOG and the latest release, and CI republishes it when those change.
- **Schedules are easy to find (spec 044 FR-009..FR-011).** *Schedules…* in each room's ⋯ menu, a clock chip in the room header with the next run, a *Schedules* button in the chat, and **Settings > Schedules** for all folders.
- **Team rule: check the linked issue before merging** (owner, spec 021). An issue closes only when everything in it is done; otherwise what is left is written on it, or in a follow-up issue.
- **CI on `main` runs every merge to the end** (spec 008). Only pull-request runs are cancelled by a newer push, so a release commit always has a complete CI run.
- **Team rule: merging a forgotten PR** (owner, spec 021). Any agent may merge another team's PR that is approved, green, clean on security, not WIP, and quiet for 2 hours, with a comment saying why.
