## v0.1.0-alpha.7 ([pre-release](https://github.com/three-feathers/agent-durbar/releases/tag/v0.1.0-alpha.7), 2026-10-19)

Unsigned and not notarized; only Linux was run by hand. **OpenAI Codex** is now offered next to Copilot and Claude Code when it is installed and signed in. Claude Code sessions can have a **cost cap in dollars**. A new session can start from a **reviewed summary**, and Schedules have a **starter kit** (Start my day, Plan my day) that ends by asking you what to do.

- **Reviews include GitHub's security results** (owner, spec 021). Before merging, an agent reads CodeQL / code scanning, Dependabot and secret scanning on its PR, fixes every new finding at the cause, and dismisses only documented false positives.
- **Schedules starter kit (spec 044 FR-006..FR-008, issue #128).** Templates such as *Start my day*, *Summarize my day* and *Plan my day*, plus a new **every day at** time. The briefing and planning templates end by asking you, with a question card, which suggested steps to do.
- **Team rule: agents review and merge** (owner decision, spec 021). An agent merges when CI is green and it did a final review of the whole diff as a reviewer; another team's agent may review too. Humans set the roadmap and strategy.

- **A shorter, current README.** It is organised by what you need: what you can do, harnesses, install, safety, costs, themes (new), build, limits and roadmap. The milestone and release sections moved, unchanged, to [docs/feature-history.md](docs/feature-history.md).

- **New session with a summary (spec 030 FR-034..FR-037).** Start a new session from a hand-off summary of the current conversation, made by Copilot, Claude Code or Codex without changing that conversation. You edit the summary before it is sent as the first message.
- **A cost cap in dollars for Claude Code (spec 012 FR-150).** Set it in the New session dialog or from the cap chip. When Claude's estimate reaches it, the chat shows *Cost cap reached* with Raise cap / Remove cap / Stop here. It is per session, soft, and enforced with Claude's own `--max-budget-usd`.
- **Codex with MCP servers and helpers (spec 012 FR-147..FR-149).**
  - Your MCP servers, with the folder's extras, now reach Codex sessions.
  - Their state shows in the MCP panel and they can be switched on and off (from the next message).
  - An MCP tool call asks for approval and runs once approved.
  - Codex sub-agents show as helpers.
  
  Checked on a real Codex.

- **Questions from Codex use the question card (spec 012 FR-146).** When Codex asks you to choose, the same card as for Copilot and Claude shows the options. A secret question is never shown there. Codex offers this tool only with an experimental setting, so it often asks in plain text.

- **OpenAI Codex is offered next to Copilot and Claude Code (spec 012 FR-145).** If `codex` is installed and signed in (`codex login`, for example with ChatGPT), it is a harness you can pick. It supports:
  - Read-only, Ask first and Allow all, enforced;
  - a mode switch from the next message, models and efforts;
  - previous sessions and resume; a folder continues its last thread after a restart.
  
  Checked end to end in the real app with ChatGPT Plus. On Ubuntu, Read-only and Ask first need the bubblewrap note in the README.

- **Codex groundwork: mode switch and restarts (spec 012 FR-143/FR-144).** A Codex agent's permission mode can be changed while it runs; it applies from the next message, and a stricter mode stops a running turn. An ended Codex session continues its own thread, also after a restart. Checked on a real Codex.

- **Codex groundwork: resume, previous sessions, history and models (spec 012 FR-093, FR-141, FR-142).** The Codex adapter, not offered yet, can resume a thread of the same folder, list the folder's earlier threads, replay a thread's chat, and offer Codex's models and efforts. All of this was checked on a real Codex.

- **For anyone with agents, across harnesses (spec 001 FR-027).**
  - The welcome card, the README, the app description and the repository description now say what Durbar is: a calm command center for AI agents on GitHub Copilot, Claude Code and (in progress) Codex, for code, documents, research or notes, not only for developers.
  - The header badge says **Live**; its tooltip lists the ready harnesses.

- **Codex groundwork, first real run (spec 012 FR-138..FR-140).** The Codex adapter, not offered yet, now starts on Codex 0.163 and enforces Read-only, Ask first and Allow all on a real account. A blocked Linux sandbox gives a plain message, with an Ubuntu note in the README. Stop says when a command that already started may still finish.

- **Codex groundwork: sign-in check (spec 012 FR-137).** Codex detection now runs `codex login status` after the version, so a signed-out Codex will show "not signed in" with how to sign in. It takes effect when the Codex adapter is offered (#51).

- **Open in VS Code Insiders or VSCodium (spec 009 FR-015).** "Open in VS Code" now tries VS Code, then VS Code Insiders, then VSCodium, and says which one it opened.

- **Linux: the running window shows the app icon (spec 008 FR-025/FR-026, issue #247).** The window now has the icon. The AppImage's desktop entry also matches the window (`StartupWMClass=agent-durbar`; alpha.6 had `Agent Durbar`), so docks can link the two.

- **Stop and End session in the card menu (spec 005 FR-017, #251).** Right-click a live main agent: Stop (only while it works or thinks; disabled with the reason when the kind cannot interrupt) and End session (asks first when the agent is busy or waiting), the same calls and toasts as the chat header. Checked in the simulator by `npm run check:office` at 1440x900 and 1280x800; not run against a real session.
- Previous sessions has a search box (shown with 2 or more entries): it filters Copilot sessions and VS Code chats by title, ignoring case and accents, with "N of M" per group and Escape to clear; titles only, nothing is sent anywhere (spec 006 FR-023, issue #252).
- **VS Code chat view opens at the latest message, reads two more kinds, and has Continue in VS Code (spec 009 FR-016..FR-018, issue #253).** **View** now scrolls to the end of the chat (the "only the last N" and "not recognised" notes are above it); question prompts (`questionCarousel`) are shown as read-only, sanitised Markdown (no buttons or other controls) and `mcpServersStarting` is no longer reported as unknown; **Continue in VS Code** opens the chat's folder with the same launcher as *Open in VS Code*. Simulator, unit tests on synthetic fixtures and a driver at 1440x900 and 1280x800; no real VS Code launch was run.

- **Removing a folder is easy to find (spec 006 FR-022).** Each room has a **⋯** button (or right-click its header) with New session, Rename, Hide and **Remove folder**. A folder's only session shows **Remove folder** in its chat header. Before, Remove was only in the Folders panel.

- **Faster CI and tests (spec 008 FR-020..FR-023).** `npm test` compiles the test scripts once and runs the suites in parallel, about 16 s instead of about 4 minutes. The office check runs in four parallel shards. The package smoke no longer waits for the other jobs. A pull request that changes only docs, specs or Markdown skips the build and test jobs. A daily **Branch cleanup** workflow deletes branches whose pull request merged more than a day ago.

## v0.1.0-alpha.6 ([published pre-release](https://github.com/three-feathers/agent-durbar/releases/tag/v0.1.0-alpha.6), 2026-10-19)

Unsigned and not notarized; only Linux was run by hand. Claude Code is offered when it is installed and signed in; its cost is a dollar estimate, never AIU, and it has no credit cap. An in-place upgrade from alpha.5 was not checked.

- **New app icon: a lead and a working team.** A lead with one arm raised toward a small status board, above three seated workers at laptops, on a navy rounded square (design F2a). Same file names in `build/` (`icon.svg`, 1024x1024 `icon.png`, `icon.ico` with 16-256 px, `icon.icns`), so the packaging config is unchanged. The 16 px entry of `icon.ico` and `icon.icns` is a hand-tuned pixel version (`build/icon-16.svg`: the lead and the board, no team) so it stays readable in the taskbar and title bar; the README header shows it. Asset-only change, 0 AIU.

- **MCP read tools: more secret shapes masked, outside paths hidden (spec 041 T053, issue #275).** Secrets in an MCP server's `command`, `args` and `url` are now masked in one-arg flags (`--token abc`), `:` and `/` forms, cookie and other header values, JSON, connection strings, user info without a colon, Telegram, Slack and Discord URLs and signed query parameters; the folder's stored secret values are replaced too. An absolute path outside the saved folders comes back as `…/name`. Not reachable yet (no server runs). 0 AIU.

- **MCP server groundwork: read tools (spec 041 T016).** The seven read tools (folders, agents, floors, usage totals, settings, MCP state, request status) answer from Durbar's own state with secret values masked: env and header key names only, secret flags and URL parts hidden. Not reachable yet (no server runs). No user-visible change, 0 AIU.

- **MCP server groundwork: agent state in the main process (spec 041 T014, issue #204).** The main process now keeps each agent's office word and sub-label (Working, Waiting for you, Limit reached…) with the same rules as the window, so the MCP read tools can report them later. Pausing in the window is reported to the main process. No visible change, 0 AIU.

- **MCP server groundwork: tool registry (spec 041 T013, issue #203).** The read-only tools an MCP client will see (folders, agents, floors, usage totals, settings, MCP state, request status), with argument checks and a gate before `initialize`. Change tools stay hidden until the confirmation dialog exists. Not wired yet: nothing listens. No user-visible change, 0 AIU.

- **MCP server groundwork: main-process simulator (spec 041 T015, issue #215).** A developer hook, `DURBAR_SIM_MAIN=N`, plans N simulated agents in the main process (several floors, one off, with waiting, limit, error and not-started agents) on a fake adapter, so the MCP server can be tested later. Ignored in packaged builds; not wired into the app start yet. No user-visible change, 0 AIU.

- **After a restart, each folder continues its last session (spec 006 FR-020/FR-021).** Before, a restored folder started a new session at its first message. Now opening it resumes the session it last used and shows the earlier conversation; resuming sends no prompt. Only the session's id is remembered. Checked on a real Copilot and a real Claude Code.

- **Security sweep (spec 045).** The app window now refuses to load any page but its own and refuses web permissions such as the microphone or notifications; only clipboard writes for the Copy buttons are allowed. On Windows, a `.cmd` shim is run with the system's own `cmd.exe`, not whatever `ComSpec` names. Custom place ids are drawn uniformly. The remove-folder question shows names with `&` correctly. Code-scanning alerts in test-only scripts were reviewed and dismissed with reasons, which are listed in the spec. One build-time dependency (`sprintf-js` under electron-builder) has no fix yet and is not shipped in the app.

- **Claude Code is offered next to Copilot (spec 012 FR-135/FR-136).** If `claude` is installed and signed in on your computer, Durbar offers it without any switch. When two or more harnesses are ready, the toolbar and the New session dialog have a **Harness** select; with only one, nothing extra is shown, and new folders use that one. Claude's cost stays a dollar estimate, apart from AIU, with no credit cap.

- **Claude Code: the context chip shows how full the window is (spec 012 FR-134).** It used to say "Context: unknown" for a Claude agent. The window now comes from Claude's own per-model report at the end of each turn, and the tokens in use from the last message. Checked on a real Claude Code.

- **Moods in the List view and on the walking figure (spec 034 FR-014/FR-015).** The List view has no figures, so an agent whose context is nearly full, that needs help, fainted, waits impatiently or is dizzy now says so in amber at the start of its task, and to screen readers. An agent walking to a place in the lobby keeps its mood too: an amber ring when heavy, horns when nearly full, a small dot when in trouble. Serious mode hides all of it, as on the cards.

- **A workspace's other folders reach the agent (spec 006 FR-019).** A `.code-workspace` session still runs in its first folder. Its other folders are now given to the agent as well: Copilot's `additionalDirectories`, Claude's `--add-dir`. The agent can work in all of them, with the same permission mode in each.

- **Dev scripts run on any machine (spec 010 T021).** `scripts/e2e-driver.js` takes its folders from `E2E_DIR`/`E2E_SHOTS`/`E2E_LOGS`/`E2E_HELLO`/`E2E_NOTES`, and `npm run smoke` defaults to folders under the temp folder, instead of `/workspace/...`.

- **A default agent per folder (spec 043 FR-009).** In the New session dialog, tick **Default for new sessions here** and that custom agent is preselected the next time you start a session in the folder. Untick it to go back to the default agent. Only the agent's name is saved; a repository agent still asks once.

- **Scheduled prompts (spec 044, issue #125).** In **Folders**, each folder has **Schedules**: a prompt sent to the folder's agent once at a time or every N minutes, only while the app is open. A run is skipped when the agent is busy or waiting for you, missed runs are not caught up, and **Pause all schedules** stops everything. The agent's own permission mode and credit cap apply. Checked on a real Copilot session (0.16 AIU).

- **Custom agents for Claude Code too (spec 043 FR-008).** The experimental Claude agent lists the folder's `.claude/agents` and your own `~/.claude/agents`, with the same one-time confirmation for a repository agent. Claude fixes the agent when a session starts, so it is chosen in the New session dialog or before the first message. Checked on a real Claude Code: a session started with a pirate agent answered "Arr…".

- **A welcome for new users (spec 001 FR-026).** With no folder yet, the office shows a short welcome: sign in to Copilot once, add a folder (its agent starts Read-only), send a message. It has buttons to add a folder or look around in the demo.

- **Start a new session with a custom agent (spec 043 FR-007).** The New session dialog has an **Agent** select listing the folder's custom agents. A repository agent still asks once before it is used.

- **Copy buttons in the chat (spec 005 FR-015).** Each agent reply has a **Copy** button that copies the reply as written, and each code block has its own button for the code only. They appear when you point at a reply or move to them with the keyboard.

- **Move between desks with the arrow keys (spec 001 FR-019).** In the office, the arrow keys move the focus between desks, helper tiles and closed doors: left and right along a row, up and down to the nearest one. Enter opens the desk, as before.

- **Permissions on Windows (spec 003).** "Approve for this session" on a folder now also covers the same folder spelled with other capitals or with `/` instead of `\`. Two permission requests that arrive in the same millisecond without an id no longer overwrite each other.

- **Safer attachments (spec 004).** A picked file counts as an image only if its bytes say so (a renamed `.png` is sent as a file). Empty files are left out, and the image button only offers images. A sent file has to be picked again to be sent again, and temporary attachment folders left by a crash are cleaned up at start.

- **Claude Code: switch its MCP servers on and off while it runs (spec 012 FR-133, issue #58).** The per-agent MCP switches now work for Claude agents too, using Claude's own `mcp_toggle`. A switch is remembered when the session restarts. Checked on a real Claude Code with a small local MCP server, without sending a prompt.

- **A chosen answer on a question card is visible in every theme (spec 012 FR-130).** It gets a ring and a check mark. In Retro and Japanese Court it used to look like the other options.

- **Theme swatches show their own theme (spec 037 FR-014).** In Settings, each theme's little swatch now shows that theme's colours. Before, all six were painted with the active theme's colours and looked the same.

- **Choose a custom agent per session (spec 043, issue #127).** The chat header has an **Agent** chip. It lists the session's custom agents (`.github/agents/*.agent.md` in the folder and `~/.copilot/agents`) and switches between them or back to the default agent. An agent from the folder's repository asks once before use and shows its tools, model and MCP servers. A switch made while the agent works waits for the reply to end, and Stop drops it. Your permission mode always stays. Checked on a real Copilot session: the selected agent answered in its own style, and Read-only still blocked a write.

- **Claude Code: a signed-out `claude` is shown as not signed in (spec 012 FR-132, issue #55).** The agent-kind check now also runs `claude auth status` (no prompt, nothing billed). If you're not signed in, the kind is marked "not signed in" with how to fix it, instead of failing at your first message. Only the signed-in flag is read, never your email or organisation.

- **Live verification on a real Copilot session (spec 013, issue #3).** Stop, End and resume with context, the lazy start of a restored folder, thinking, a helper agent and usage were checked in the real app (9 prompts, about 0.31 AIU) and now count as verified in specs 005 and 006. A second message sent during a turn is queued by Copilot and answered afterwards. Several models, a real cap, VS Code visibility and macOS/Windows remain open.

- **MCP server groundwork: key file and handshake (spec 041 T011, issue #180).** A per-install secret kept in a private file, and a mutual HMAC handshake so the bridge and Durbar prove the key to each other without sending it. Not wired yet: nothing listens. No user-visible change, 0 AIU.

- **MCP server groundwork: constants and per-OS paths (spec 041 T010, issue #175).** Where the MCP socket or named pipe and the key file would live on Windows, macOS and Linux, with a safety check for any folder outside the app's data folder. Not wired yet: nothing listens. No user-visible change, 0 AIU.

- **MCP server groundwork: JSON-RPC framing and protocol (spec 041 T012, issue #176).** Newline framing with a 64 KB cap, the MCP `initialize`/`ping` subset with no new dependency, a fake client and synthetic handshakes for tests. Not wired yet: Durbar does not listen for MCP clients. No user-visible change, 0 AIU.

- **Copilot can ask you questions too (spec 002 FR-058).** Copilot sessions now get Copilot's `ask_user` tool. When Copilot asks you to choose, the same question card appears in the chat. Before this, the tool was missing because the app did not answer it. Checked on a real Copilot session (0.18 AIU).

- **Questions from the agent (spec 012 FR-128..FR-131, issue #60).** When the Claude agent asks you to choose (`AskUserQuestion`), the chat shows a card with an option button for each choice (several when allowed), an "Other" field, and Answer and Skip. The agent waits with "Waiting for your answer". Before this, those questions were always refused. Checked on a real Claude Code in all three permission modes ($0.007) and in the real app. The question event is generic, so other agents can use it later.

- **Claude Code: previous sessions and resume (spec 012 FR-125..FR-127, issue #56).** A Claude agent's Previous sessions now lists the folder's earlier Claude sessions, read-only from Claude's own session files. Resume continues one and replays its chat. Checked in the real app: after a restart, the resumed Claude agent remembered the file it had created. Also, the resume note no longer doubles a title's final full stop.

- **Claude Code's dollar estimate shown apart from AIU (spec 012 FR-122, issue #194).** A Claude agent's card and chat header now show `≈ $0.012 est.` (Claude's own estimate of the API price, not a bill) instead of "0.00 AIU". Agents whose kind reports neither figure show tokens only. Dollars are never added to an AIU total. Simulator only, 0 AIU.

- **Claude Code agents are no longer called Copilot (spec 012 FR-123/FR-124, issue #196).** The first real-app run with a Claude agent worked end to end: add a folder, chat, the Ask first approval card, Stop. It also showed Copilot wording on Claude agents, now fixed: the agent's subtitle, the chat footer, Previous sessions, and Copy resume command (now `claude --resume <id>`). Claude's input token count now includes cached input, as Copilot's does.

- **Claude Code: choose the model and effort (spec 012 FR-118..FR-121, issue #57).** With the experimental Claude Code agent, the toolbar and the New session dialog list the models your `claude` offers (Opus, Sonnet, Haiku, Fable, ...) with their effort levels. The list is read from the CLI without sending a prompt and leaves no session behind. Checked on a real Claude Code 2.1.302: 13 models, and one `haiku` prompt ($0.0013). Each agent kind now has its own model list in the app.

- **Short agent names are no longer cut to one letter (spec 001 FR-025).** In the compact card sizes, a long status word ("Limit reached") now gives way before the agent's name, so "Omar" stays "Omar" and does not become "O…". `check:office` now fails if a cut name shows fewer than three characters. Simulator only, 0 AIU.

- **Claude Code: change the permission mode while a session runs (spec 012 FR-115..FR-117, issue #54).** A stricter mode applies at once: Durbar's tool check uses both the mode Claude was started with and your current choice. A looser mode applies from the next message, which resumes the same session with the new settings. Checked on a real Claude Code with `haiku` (about $0.011): switching Allow all to Read-only during a shell command blocked the write that followed. The README no longer says that there is no Claude adapter.

- **Remove one session, rename a folder (spec 006 FR-017/FR-018).** A folder with more than one session has **Remove session** in the chat header (asks first when it is working; its history stays under Previous sessions). Folders > **Rename** gives a room a display name (saved, up to 40 characters, empty = the folder name). Simulator and unit tests, 0 AIU.

- **Room headers fit with many one-agent folders (spec 001 FR-023/FR-024).** With many folders of one agent each, room headers no longer overflow or overlap and the office no longer falls back to scrolling: the folder path goes first, then the room name is cut with its full name (and folder total) in the tooltip, narrow rooms hide the plant, and the narrowest move the folder total to that tooltip. In the roomy layout the plant no longer covers the MCP button. `check:office` now checks every room header. Simulator only, 0 AIU.

- Floor names: every hidden character is now written as an escape in source, more look-alike and layout characters are refused (line and paragraph separators, soft hyphen, invisible operators, fillers, annotation marks), a saved name with a C1 control keeps its floor, and the simulator's rule is tested against the shared one (spec 033 delta, T041, issue #167; 0 AIU, unit tests).

- **Claude Code adapter, experimental (spec 012 T090-T094, issues #48, #49, #53).** With `DURBAR_CLAUDE=1` the agent-kind picker offers Claude Code. It drives your installed `claude` directly over its own stream protocol (no new dependency); the Claude Agent SDK can be used instead when it is installed and `DURBAR_CLAUDE_TRANSPORT=sdk` is set (not bundled: its licence is Anthropic's Commercial Terms). Read-only, Ask first and Allow all are enforced through Durbar's own permission broker, a read outside the session's folders is denied by a hook, the folder's `CLAUDE.md` is passed as instructions while Claude's own settings files are not loaded. Checked on a real Claude Code 2.1.302 with `haiku` (about $0.015): every mode, interrupt, stop and resume. Not offered to everyone yet (T095).

- **Codex adapter groundwork (spec 012 T043a, issue #51).** A `CodexAdapter` that talks to `codex app-server` (one child per session): handshake with the read-limited profile, start with the fail-closed refusals, send, approvals through Durbar's broker, interrupt, stop, crash handling, and the adapter contract suite, all on a scripted in-memory fake. Codex never gets other tools' tokens (`GH_TOKEN`, `COPILOT_*`, `ANTHROPIC_*`) in its environment. It is **not registered in the app**: a dev hook (`DURBAR_CODEX=1`) is planned for T043c and does not exist yet. Nothing was run on a real Codex.

- **A calm warning when you add your home folder, a parent of it or a drive root (spec 006 FR-016, issue #170).** Adding is still allowed; one line says that agents there can read everything under your home folder (or the whole drive), SSH keys and saved credentials included. Windows drive and network-share roots and macOS's case-insensitive paths are covered by unit tests. Simulator and unit tests, 0 AIU.

- **Codex reads limited to the session's folders (spec 012 T084, issue #168).** In Read-only and Ask first, the Codex mode settings now use a Codex permission profile that may read only the session's folders and Codex's minimal system files, instead of the `read-only` sandbox that could read anywhere (`~/.ssh`). Where that cannot be enforced (Codex older than rust-v0.163.0, no experimental API, Windows without the elevated sandbox) Durbar will refuse those two modes for Codex. Pure settings and unit tests only; there is no Codex adapter yet and nothing was run on a real Codex.

- **Docs: what the permission modes and usage mean per agent kind (spec 012 T028, issue #47).** Dated `## Changes` entries in specs 003 (Read-only / Ask first / Allow all for Claude and Codex, unknown mode = Read-only, Codex Read-only differs), 011 (AIU for Copilot, a labelled USD estimate for Claude never added to AIU, no money for Codex; caps stay Copilot-only) and 018 (live mode switch per kind), and a README *Safety model* note. Docs only; nothing is wired to a real Claude or Codex yet.

## v0.1.0-alpha.5 ([published pre-release](https://github.com/three-feathers/agent-durbar/releases/tag/v0.1.0-alpha.5), 2026-10-18)

Themes that change the whole office, main agents named by their Copilot session title, floors, agents that show how they are doing, custom places and usage totals kept across restarts, plus groundwork for Claude Code and Codex that is not wired yet (only Copilot works). Checked on the simulator and with unit tests; nothing here was checked on a real Copilot session. **Unsigned and not notarized; untested on real macOS and Windows hardware; an in-place upgrade from alpha.4 was not checked** (spec 042).

- **Main agents show their session title (spec 001/002 deltas, issue #126).** Copilot names every session (a title from your first prompt, later perhaps a short summary or a `/rename` name); a main agent's card, chat header, list row, agent strip and lobby label now show that title instead of the folder name, which stays in the room header and the tooltip. Sub-agents are unchanged. A resumed session shows its saved name, else its summary. Simulator, fixtures and unit tests (`DURBAR_SIM_TITLES=1`), plus one real Read-only prompt (0.15 AIU): Copilot sent the title as soon as the prompt was sent, and after a resume the app's one-time name request brought it back.
- **Claude usage counted once per message (spec 012 T025, issue #44).** The Claude event mapper counted the usage of every streamed piece of one message (text, tool call), so tokens were counted twice; now repeated figures for one `message.id` count once and only their growth is added (bounded memory, empty usage skipped). New fixture tests pin the cost delta, Codex's unknown context window and "credits not reported", and that dollars never mix into AIU totals. Unit tests only; the Claude adapter is not wired yet.

- **`check:themes` now really checks each theme (spec 036 FR-005 delta).** In simulator runs the theme never reached the page, so every per-theme office check had measured Clean Office. The theme now travels in the page address (`theme=`), the check fails when the theme on screen is not the one it asked for, and `check:themes` also runs a sample user theme (`scripts/fixtures/themes/check-user.json`, spec 040 T005). Test tooling only, no change to the app; 0 AIU.
- **Themes: Clean Office, Corporate, Retro Pixel and three Durbar courts, plus your own packs (specs 035-040, issue #129).** The office can now wear a **theme**: a data-only pack (colours, small art, scene words) picked in **Settings > Theme** and applied live — no restart. *Corporate* turns the office into glass and steel; *Retro Pixel* draws chunky 8-bit figures (palette pixel maps) and sends agents to the Console and the Modem; the three *Durbar* packs dress the office in Indian, European and Japanese court colours with a small head accessory (turban arc, crown, kanzashi pin) and court place names. Only **scene words** change (places, lobby, doors): status, permission and credit wording never do, and the layout still fits 15 agents at 1280×800 — `check:office` passes per theme. Your own themes are plain folders with a validated `manifest.json`, imported/exported from Settings (no scripts, no CSS injection; a malformed pack is refused with a toast). Clean Office is exactly today's office (scene output byte-identical) and is the fallback for anything unknown or broken. Simulator and unit tests (`test:theme` now covers every pack: tokens, words, motion presets, sanitization and pill contrast ≥4.5:1; `test:userthemes` covers import/export), 0 AIU.
- **Themes look like their names, and imported themes are untrusted (specs 035-040 deltas, PR #137 review).** A pack now names a **scene kit** that draws its own people, chairs, desks and monitors (suits at glass desks, 8-bit people at a CRT, courtiers in turbans, periwigs or kimono at carved, gilded or lacquer desks; status cues stay in place; `test:scenekits`) and a **UI style** (a fixed list; the CSS is the app's, `themes.css`) that restyles the top bar, rooms, cards, buttons, pills, lobby and display fonts without changing any size, and every pack has a stronger palette of its own. The `+N more` chip and lobby tiles are opaque, so theme art never covers text. Security: the SVG sanitizer rebuilds art instead of passing it through (an unquoted attribute could inject CSS), token keys and values are limited to plain colour values, scene words to the scene keys; import refuses bad tokens and cleans up after a failed copy; the renderer no longer gets theme folder paths; `DURBAR_THEME` no longer saves the theme. Simulator and unit tests, 0 AIU.
- **Theme art: rooms, doors, lobby and place icons per pack (specs 037-040 stage D, PR #137).** Every built-in pack ships its own room look (walls, windows, floors), door plates, lobby trim and place icons as sanitized SVG or palette pixel maps, drawn under the real layout pieces so the 15-agent layout never changes. Its people, chairs, desks and monitors come from its scene kit (line above); the stage D character and desk overlays are kept only for user themes on the office kit. 0 AIU.
- **Floor names refuse hidden characters (spec 033 delta, T040).** Besides control characters, a floor name now refuses C1 controls and invisible direction or zero-width characters (U+202A–U+202E, U+2066–U+2069, U+200B, U+2060, U+FEFF), which could make one floor look like another. ZWNJ/ZWJ and LRM/RLM stay allowed for Indic and Persian scripts and emoji. A saved name just loses them on load. Unit tests (`test:floors`), simulator rule kept equal, 0 AIU.

- **Claude sessions load no Claude settings files (spec 012 T027, issue #46; owner decision Q8).** Mode options for the future Claude adapter set `settingSources: []` and `strictMcpConfig: true` in every mode, trusted folder or not, so a repo's or user's Claude settings and MCP servers cannot approve tools before Durbar asks. A repo's own `.claude` hooks and agents do not run (known gap, T083); the repo's `CLAUDE.md` will be passed as an appended system prompt by the Claude adapter (T045). Unit tests only; not wired to a real Claude yet.

- **Secrets are redacted from the debug log (spec 012 T051, issue #59).** Every `DURBAR_LOG` line now goes through one sink that hides environment secrets (also JSON- and URL-encoded) and token-like strings (GitHub, OpenAI/Anthropic keys, Bearer, JWT, AWS keys, URL credentials) before it is written. Unit tests with planted secrets, 0 AIU.

- **Mixed-kind simulator and capability gating (spec 012 T018, issue #38).** `simkinds=mixed` in the simulator shows Claude-like, Codex-like and a minimal kind next to Copilot; room and header buttons, the mode chip, mode lists and the Permissions selects follow each kind's capabilities and never widen. No visible change with Copilot alone. Simulator only, 0 AIU.

- **Agent kind picker (spec 012 T017, issue #37).** With more than one kind of agent, the toolbar and the New session dialog get an agent-kind select before Permissions; kinds that are not installed or too old are disabled and explained in "About agent kinds". Controls an adapter cannot support are hidden. No visible change while only Copilot exists. Simulator and unit tests, 0 AIU.

- **Codex permission-mode and approval mapping (spec 012 T022, issue #41).** A pure module that turns Read-only / Ask first / Allow all into Codex sandbox and approval settings and maps its approval requests and answers; a missing or unknown mode always declines. Not wired yet, no user-visible change. Unit tests, 0 AIU.

- **Claude Code permission-mode and tool mapping (spec 012 T021, issue #40).** A pure module that turns Read-only / Ask first / Allow all into Claude Agent SDK options and decides which tools each mode allows; Read-only denies every non-read tool (MCP included), and an unknown or missing mode falls back to Read-only. No SDK dependency, not wired yet, no user-visible change. Unit tests, 0 AIU.

- **Codex event mapper (spec 012 T024, issue #43).** A pure mapper from Codex app-server notifications to Durbar's events (messages, thinking, commands, file changes, MCP tools, sub-agents, token usage, failures), tested against synthetic fixtures. Not wired yet, no user-visible change, 0 AIU.

- **Claude Code event mapper (spec 012 T023, issue #42).** A pure mapper from Claude Agent SDK stream messages to Durbar's events (text, thinking, tools, MCP tools parsed against the init server list, sub-agents closed exactly once, token usage, cost estimate as per-result deltas, errors), tested against synthetic fixtures. No SDK dependency, not wired yet, no user-visible change, 0 AIU.

- **Codex JSON-RPC client (spec 012 T042, issue #50).** A line-delimited JSON-RPC client for `codex app-server` over a child process (argv only, no shell): framing, ids, timeouts, server requests, malformed and oversize lines, child exit and a SIGTERM-then-SIGKILL close. Tested on a fake child process; not wired yet, no user-visible change, 0 AIU.

- **Docs for the Claude Code and Codex groundwork (spec 012 T019).** The README has an *Agents and adapters* table: Copilot is supported; Claude Code and Codex are planned and marked simulator/fixtures only, with no adapter and nothing run on a real account.

- **Copilot passes the adapter contract (spec 012 T016, issue #36; spec 002 T020).** The contract suite and a replay of the recorded sessions now run on the Copilot adapter through a fake SDK client. Test code only, 0 AIU.

- **Adapter contract suite (spec 012 T015, issue #35).** A fake adapter and a shared contract every adapter must pass (events, permissions, stop, capabilities), plus tests for the main-process rules that refuse settings an adapter cannot apply. No user-visible change. Unit tests, 0 AIU.

- **Agents are routed to their adapter (spec 012 T014, issue #34).** The main process now keeps one adapter per kind (only Copilot today) and routes each agent's calls to it; `listAdapters()` reports each adapter's status. A setting an adapter cannot apply to a live session is refused instead of silently saved. Copilot unchanged. Unit tests and the simulator, 0 AIU.

- **Saved folders can record their agent kind (spec 012 T013, issue #33).** A new optional `adapterId` per saved folder, never written for Copilot, so existing files stay byte-identical; read by the app since T014 (T013 itself changed only the store). Unit tests, 0 AIU.

- **Claude Code and Codex CLI detection (spec 012 T012, issue #32).** Pure code that finds the `claude` and `codex` commands (PATH, known folders, Windows npm shims through a guarded cmd.exe) and reads their version, without a shell; since T014 only the Copilot detector is used by the app (`adapters:list`), the `claude` and `codex` ones are not called yet. Unit tests, 0 AIU.

- **Adapter registry and descriptors (spec 012 T011, issue #31).** A pure list of the agents the app knows (only Copilot today), with what each can do and a way to check if it is installed; since T014 the app builds its adapter from it; no visible change. Unit tests, 0 AIU.

- **Adapter interface ready for more agents (spec 012 T010, issue #30).** The app now talks to every agent through one interface with declared capabilities; Copilot works exactly as before. Groundwork for Claude Code and Codex; simulator and unit tests, 0 AIU.

- **Custom places in the lobby (spec 024 delta, issue #115).** Add up to 3 places of your own, with a name and an icon from a fixed set, from the lobby or Settings > Places, and send MCP servers there. Rename keeps the mapping; delete sends the servers back to their default place. The lobby stays one row at 1440x900 and 1280x800. Simulator, unit tests and `scripts/shots-024-custom.js`, 0 AIU; not checked on a real session.

- **Settings > Places lists every folder's own MCP servers (spec 024 delta, issue #116).** Per-folder extras now get a row before any agent has used them, read by name only from the saved folders; choosing a place never switches a server on or trusts it.

- **One GitHub server in Settings > Places (spec 024 delta, issue #117).** `github` (your own server in `~/.copilot/mcp-config.json`) and Copilot's built-in `github-mcp-server` (the name its tool events carry) are one row, `github`, with the note "also github-mcp-server"; a place chosen there now applies to calls from both. Saving moves an old `github-mcp-server` entry to `github`. Simulator and unit tests (`test:places`, `scripts/shots-024.js`), 0 AIU; not checked on a real session.

- **Rooms show their worst agent (spec 034 delta, issue #135).** A room keeps its colour but gets a little darker when its worst agent is heavy (70-90% context) and clearly darker with a monster, an agent in Error or any trouble; a small red/blue siren blinks next to the room name (and on the door plate when it is closed) while the room has the ambulance or a fainted agent, and its label says "Alert: an agent needs help". Header text gets darker with the floor, and the folder path in every room header is a little darker (it was about 4.2:1, now 4.5:1 or more). Serious mode turns it off; reduced motion stops the blink. Simulator and unit tests, 0 AIU.

- **Agents show how they are doing (spec 034, issue #20).** An agent's figure gets heavier from 70% of its context window and turns into a grumpy green monster above 90% (time for a fresh session). Trouble gets a drawing: an ambulance by the desk when a session errors (one at a time, the latest error), a faint at the credit limit, tapping the desk after waiting 60 s for you, dizzy stars after 3 failed tool calls in a row. Card text is unchanged; the card's tooltip and accessible label name the state. **Settings > Agent mood > Serious mode** turns it all off; with reduced motion nothing moves. Simulator: `simwaitold=1`. Simulator and unit tests (`test:mood`, `scripts/shots-034.js`), 0 AIU; not checked on a real session.

- **Floors (spec 033, branch `033-floors`).** Group rooms into floors and switch a floor off: it leaves the office, the list, the filter, the strip and the lobby and is not counted in the status pills; its agents keep running (no alerts; what waits there is shown only as text on the floor's row in the Floors panel and Settings) and the office credit total still counts them. Top-bar *Floors n/m* panel and a *Floors* section in Settings with On/Off, Only, rename, reorder, delete, move folder and the floor for new folders. Simulator and unit tests only, 0 AI credits.
- **Usage totals kept across restarts (spec 011 FR-024..FR-030, issue #21).** The office chip and the folder totals now show the credits spent **since the last reset**, saved on this computer (`durbar-usage-totals.json`: numbers, folder names and a fingerprint of each folder's path, never prompts or paths); the tooltip also gives this run alone. Settings > Credit cap > *Usage totals* shows the figure and has **Reset usage totals** (with a confirmation; Tools > All time is not touched). "credits not reported" can now be seen in the simulator (`simunreported=<room>`). The multiplier estimate is left to spec 030. Simulator and unit tests (`test:usagedoc`, `scripts/shots-021.js`), 0 AIU; not checked on a real session.

## v0.1.0-alpha.4 ([published pre-release](https://github.com/three-feathers/agent-durbar/releases/tag/v0.1.0-alpha.4), 2026-10-17)

Published as a **pre-release** (built as a draft by the release workflow, then published after checking its eight files). On top of v0.1.0-alpha.3: places in the office, the VS Code MCP import, the dialog focus fix and the first steps of switching model and context window inside a session. Spec: [031, now in the release table](specs/008-packaging-and-release/spec.md#release-table). Built and tested on the simulator, on fake sessions and with unit tests; **no AI credits were spent for the release itself** (the earlier real-account checks are listed below with their cost).

### Read this first

- **Still unsigned and not notarized.** macOS: right-click (Control-click) the app, choose **Open**, or `xattr -dr com.apple.quarantine "/Applications/Agent Durbar.app"`. Windows: SmartScreen "More info", then "Run anyway". Linux: `chmod +x` the AppImage.
- **Untested on real hardware.** Only Linux has been run by hand. The macOS and Windows packages are built and unit-tested by CI, but nobody has launched them on a real Mac or Windows PC.
- Same app id as alpha.3 (`io.github.three-feathers.agentdurbar`), so it should replace alpha.3, but an in-place upgrade from alpha.3 was not checked. No auto-update. Download newer releases by hand.
- **Switching model and context window is not verified on a real account.** It works on the simulator and on fake sessions. On a real free plan only `auto` exists, so switching between two real models, the long-context tier and the confirmation path were never seen.

### What's new

- **Places in the office (spec 024, issue #18, PR #109).** A shared lobby in the middle of the office with Library, Software library, Terminal, Web and Other tools. An agent whose tool runs longer than 0.6 s walks there and back; file tools never move it. At most three figures move at once, none with *reduce motion*; the card text and the place labels say where each agent is. Settings > *Places in the office* sets the place of each MCP server (global; names and places only). With 15 agents the office keeps `card` desks at 1440x900 and uses `mini` desks at 1280x800. Custom places are not built yet (issue #115).
- **Import MCP servers from VS Code, per folder (spec 020, issue #7, PR #29).** Reads that folder's `.vscode/mcp.json`, shows a preview with nothing ticked, and adds the ticked servers as extras of that folder only, switched off and untrusted. VS Code `inputs` are never asked for, stored or resolved.
- **Keyboard focus in the Allow all confirmation (spec 003, issue #25, PR #27).** Focus stays in the dialog, Tab is trapped and Escape cancels.
- **Switch model, effort and context tier inside a session, and compact (spec 030, PRs #110 to #113).** Chat-header model menu with confirmations and Undo, a context chip with a Compact action, a start-fresh hint near the limit, and the same on real Copilot agents through the adapter (marked "Not verified on a real account yet"). Verified on the simulator and a fake session; real checks on a free plan (`auto` only, 0.56 AIU) found and fixed three things: Stop drops a queued switch, a compaction's cost is now counted in the usage totals, and runtime error text is cleaner.
- **Docs:** README pictures regenerated on the simulator, specs 012 (Claude Code and Codex adapters) and 030 researched and planned.

### Honest limits

- **Unsigned, not notarized** (signing is spec 014, a draft that needs the maintainer's approval to buy certificates).
- **Untested on real Macs and Windows PCs.** CI builds the macOS and Windows packages and runs unit tests on all three OSes, but no one has launched them on real hardware. The Windows layout of the lobby was not looked at.
- Real-account checks of spec 030 covered `auto` only; windows, prices, effort and the long tier of real models are unverified.
- Needs a GitHub Copilot login (`copilot login`); usage bills against your own plan. Independent project, not affiliated with GitHub or Microsoft.
- Known gaps are listed per spec (`[KNOWN GAP]`) and under "Known limits" in the README.

### Full entries (as logged while building, newest first)

- **Places in the office (spec 024, issue #18).** A shared lobby in the middle of the office with Library, Software library, Terminal, Web and Other tools. While a tool runs longer than 0.6 s the agent walks there (about 1 s, an empty chair stays at its desk) and walks back after the call; at most three figures move at once, none with *reduce motion*. Settings > *Places in the office* sets the place of each MCP server (global, saved; names and places only). Checked on the simulator and with unit tests (`test:places`, `check:office`, `scripts/shots-024.js`); CI runs the unit tests on Linux, macOS and Windows and the office layout check (with the lobby) on Linux; a short real-session check (3 prompts, 0.84 AIU) confirmed the walks to Terminal and Software library; the Windows layout was not looked at.
- **Real-account checks of model switching and compaction (spec 030 T041..T046, free plan, `auto` only, 8 one-line prompts + 1 compaction, 0.56 AI credits; ceiling 20, owner's limit 100).** New dev tools `scripts/phase3-real.ts` and `scripts/phase3b-real.ts` and real fixtures. Findings and fixes: **Stop drops a switch that was queued during a reply** (the simulator now does too; the UI says "dropped when you stopped" and no longer leaves "Next: ..." standing, which also clears itself two seconds after a reply ends if no model event came); **a compaction's cost is now counted** in the usage totals (it was one invisible model call, 0.19 AIU); a model the plan cannot use is refused with "not available on this Copilot account or plan"; runtime error text no longer shows the "Request ... failed with message" wrapper; a compaction that grows a tiny conversation says "too short to shrink". History survives switch calls, compaction and resume. Still unverified: switching between two real models, `confirmation_required`, the long tier's window and price (a free plan lists only `auto`). The earlier T040 entry below is part of this.
- **Real model list recorded, read-only (spec 030 T040, 0 AI credits, no session, no prompt).** New `scripts/capture-models.ts` and a sanitized fixture of what the Copilot login reports: only `auto`, with no window, price, effort or tier fields (so the price unit and the long tier are still unverified). The Phase 3 plan (which models, prompts, credit ceiling) is in the spec's research file and waits for the owner's OK.
- **Real Copilot agents can switch model, effort and context tier and compact, tested on a fake session only (spec 030 Phase 2, tasks T030..T035).** The Copilot adapter implements `setModel` (SDK `model.switchTo`: model, effort, tier, `requireAvailable`, a queued switch while a reply runs; never the settings-writing fields), `cancelQueuedModel` and `compact` (refused while the agent works). Real agents now show the same header menu, confirmations, Undo and Compact action as the simulator, each marked **"Not verified on a real account yet"**; a switch that needs compaction first compacts, then switches, and is refused (not looped) if the conversation is still too big. **Fix:** the saved model, effort and context tier are now passed when a session is created and when it is resumed, so a switched model survives an ended agent or a restart; the `auto` routing preference is only sent for a new `auto` session. `listModelOptions` fills price fields, price class, the long-context tier and its window when the runtime reports them (missing ones stay absent); their unit is unverified, shown as "(unit not verified)", compared only by ratio and never checked against the credit cap. A typed effort the new model does not support is cleared instead of sent. Not started or ended agents save the choice for their first message. Tests: new `test:copilotmodel` (fake client and session), additions to `test:modelswitch`; `check:model` has a new screenshot of the note. **No real session, 0 AIU.**
- **Switch model and context inside a session, simulator only (spec 030 Phase 1, tasks T016..T024).** Chat-header model chip is a menu (price per million tokens or "price not reported", window, effort, Long context only when offered, queued switch with Cancel); pricier, unpriced, long-tier or over-cap-headroom switches ask first, cheaper ones apply with an **Undo** toast; the credit cap never changes. The context chip shows the tier, "Window: waiting for next message" after a switch, and a **Compact** action (idle agents only, optional focus). Compaction (manual, automatic, before a smaller window, failed) leaves one chat line; a dismissible start-fresh note offers New session near the limit. A model change by the runtime (rate limit, refused request) is shown as a note and a toast and never saved as the folder's choice. **Office card menu:** right-click, then *Model and context...*. **Real Copilot agents are unchanged in what they can do:** the model chip opens a menu that says it cannot switch in place and offers New session; they do now show compaction lines and the start-fresh note when the runtime reports them. Checked by `npm test` (new `test:modelswitch`, `test:simmodel`; additions to events, folders, dialog tests), `npm run check:model` (real key events in the simulator, 1440x900 and 1280x800, screenshots `docs/screenshots/030-*`) and `check:office`; **no real session, 0 AIU**. Sim prices, windows and queue behaviour are made up; the real runtime's are unverified (`[KNOWN GAP]`).
- **Switching model and context, groundwork (spec 030 Phase 1, tasks T010..T015, simulator only).** No visible change with a real Copilot account: its agents still show the model as read-only and have no switch or compact action. Added the pure price and confirmation logic, the normalized `model` and `compaction` events and the mapper for the matching SDK events, the folder store's `contextTier`, main-process IPC with validation (`agents:setModel`, `agents:cancelQueuedModel`, `agents:compact`; real agents answer "start a new session"), and simulator support (priced fake models, a long-context tier, queued switches, runtime fallback, manual and automatic compaction). Checked by `npm test` (`test:modelswitch`, `test:simmodel`, additions to `test:events` and `test:folders`); no real session was started. The sim's prices and queue behaviour are made up; the real runtime's are unverified (`[KNOWN GAP]`, spec 030 research).
- **Docs only, no behaviour change:** spec 030 (change the model and the context window inside a session) is researched, specified, planned and split into tasks ([specs/030-switch-model-context](specs/030-switch-model-context/spec.md)). Copilot SDK 1.0.22 declares a live model switch (applies from the next message, queued while a turn runs), a manual compaction and a long-context tier, but no free window size and no clear from a host app; this was read from the SDK types and docs only. Nothing was run on a real account and no credits were spent. Not built: a session's model still cannot be changed in the app.
- **Import from VS Code, per folder (spec 020 FR-020..FR-031, issue #7).** A folder's MCP extras screen has **Import from VS Code...**: it reads that folder's `.vscode/mcp.json` (comments and trailing commas are fine), shows a preview with a checkbox per server (nothing ticked, env/header keys with masked values, ignored fields and unusable entries explained) and adds the ticked ones as extras of **that folder only**, switched **off** and not trusted, so the first switch-on shows the trust dialog. VS Code `inputs` are never asked for, stored or resolved: values with `${...}` references are not copied, and entries that need one in their command, arguments, URL or working directory (other than `${workspaceFolder}`) cannot be imported. The VS Code file and `~/.copilot/mcp-config.json` are never written, and nothing is added to the global list. Unit tests in `test:mcp`, simulator screenshots `docs/screenshots/i7-import-preview-*.png`; no credits spent, not checked on a real VS Code file, macOS or Windows.
- **Docs only, no behaviour change:** spec 012 (Claude Code and Codex adapters) is clarified, researched, planned and split into tasks ([specs/012-claude-codex-adapters](specs/012-claude-codex-adapters/spec.md)). There is no Claude or Codex adapter yet and nothing was tried on a real account.

- **Keyboard focus in the Allow all confirmation (spec 003 FR-033, issue #25).** Focus used to jump back to the mode chip behind the dialog, so Escape did nothing and Tab left the dialog. Now focus stays inside it, Tab is trapped, Escape cancels (mode unchanged) and focus returns to the chip. *Allow all* still needs an explicit confirm. Tests: `npm run test:dialog` (in `npm test`) and `npm run check:dialog-focus` (simulator, real key events).
- **Docs only, no behaviour change:** the two README hero pictures (15-agent office, full-screen chat) and the Settings/About, credit-cap and MCP pictures (`m9-office-caps`, `m9-settings-cap`, `m10-*`, `about-settings`) were regenerated on the simulator, so they show the office total, per-folder totals, the Tools/Settings buttons, *Import from VS Code...* and the alpha.3 version. New drivers `scripts/shots-hero.js` and `scripts/shots-about.js`; `shots-m9.js` and `shots-m10.js` now hide the startup toast. `AGENTS.md` no longer lists spec 012 as a backlog draft (spec 025 FR-006).

## v0.1.0-alpha.3 ([published pre-release](https://github.com/three-feathers/agent-durbar/releases/tag/v0.1.0-alpha.3), 2026-10-17)

Published by the maintainer as a **pre-release** (it was built as a draft first). It is the first published release. Small update on top of v0.1.0-alpha.2 (never published; its draft was removed in favour of this one, the [git tag](https://github.com/three-feathers/agent-durbar/tree/v0.1.0-alpha.2) remains): the permission mode rename, usage totals, Windows grant-scope tests and a docs catch-up. Spec: [026, now in the release table](specs/008-packaging-and-release/spec.md#release-table). Built and tested on the simulator and unit tests; **no AI credits were spent for the release itself.**

### Read this first

- **Still unsigned and not notarized.** macOS: right-click (Control-click) the app, choose **Open**, or `xattr -dr com.apple.quarantine "/Applications/Agent Durbar.app"`. Windows: SmartScreen "More info", then "Run anyway". Linux: `chmod +x` the AppImage.
- **Untested on real hardware.** Only Linux has been run by hand. The macOS and Windows packages are built and unit-tested by CI, but nobody has launched them on a real Mac or Windows PC.
- Same app id as alpha.2 (`io.github.three-feathers.agentdurbar`), so it should be the same app as alpha.2, but an in-place upgrade from alpha.2 was not checked. No auto-update. Download newer releases by hand.

### What's new

- **Permission mode renamed *Allow all*** (was *Allow edits*), in the top bar, new-session dialog, mode chip and menu, confirmation, toasts and chat hint. Behavior is unchanged: it approves everything. Names are now Read-only / Ask first / Allow all. Spec 003 FR-028..FR-030, PR #15.
- **Usage totals (spec 011 FR-018..FR-023, PR #17).** An office total (header chip) and a per-folder total (room header and closed door plate) in AIU, summed from the runtime's usage events since the app opened. Not saved across restarts; ended sessions stay counted; shows "credits not reported" instead of 0 when the runtime sends no figure. `npm run test:usagetotals`.
- **Windows paths in session-grant scopes** are unit-tested (spec 003 FR-031/FR-032, PR #16). Known gap: Windows grant keys are not yet compared case-insensitively (task T028).
- **Docs and process:** README, CHANGELOG and spec statuses brought in line with `main`; AGENTS.md has a "Keep docs in sync" rule; the PR template has a "Docs updated" box; a non-blocking check warns when `src/` changes without a docs change (spec 025).
- **Screenshots of the mode menu, confirmation and right-click menu** (`docs/screenshots/m7-mode-switch.png`, `m7-mode-confirm.png`, `m7-mode-context.png`) were regenerated on the simulator and now say *Allow all*; the driver is `scripts/shots-mode.js` (spec 025 FR-005, T006).

## v0.1.0-alpha.2 (never published, 2026-10-16)

> The alpha.2 draft was deleted and replaced by alpha.3, which includes everything below. The git tag `v0.1.0-alpha.2` remains.

Everything since [v0.1.0-alpha.1](https://github.com/three-feathers/agent-durbar/releases/tag/v0.1.0-alpha.1) (milestones 6 to 10 plus ownership). Spec: [008 release table](specs/008-packaging-and-release/spec.md#release-table). Built and tested on the simulator and unit tests; the real Copilot runtime was used only for small research captures (about 0.17 AIU) and free checks. **No AI credits were spent for the release itself.**

### Read this first

- **New app id: `io.github.three-feathers.agentdurbar`** (was `io.github.vikramgorla.agentdurbar`; spec 008 FR-019). The old test install is a **separate app**: it does not share settings, saved folders or tool counts with this one, and installing alpha.2 does not replace it. Remove the old one yourself if you do not want both.
- **Still unsigned and not notarized.** macOS: right-click (Control-click) the app, choose **Open**, or `xattr -dr com.apple.quarantine "/Applications/Agent Durbar.app"`. Windows: SmartScreen "More info", then "Run anyway". Linux: `chmod +x` the AppImage.
- No auto-update. Download newer releases by hand.

### What's new

- **MCP servers (specs 019, 020).** The **Copilot CLI's own list** (`~/.copilot/mcp-config.json` plus the built-in GitHub server) is read live and is the primary, global source in Settings and in the Tools view, with call counts and the exact command or URL (no env or header values). Nothing to import. Extra servers that only this app passes to a session are stored **per folder or workspace** (room-header MCP button) with a trust prompt that shows the exact command; secrets stay encrypted with the OS store. Servers from the CLI list get no trust prompt (decided by Vikram, 2026-10-16). The old app-level list and its Import buttons are gone.
- **Tool usage (specs 019, 020).** The Tools view has **This session** and **All time**. All time is kept on disk (`durbar-tool-usage.json` in the app's userData folder) and survives restarts: per tool, per MCP server, per agent. Only tool names, server names, agent labels and counts are stored; never arguments, output, prompts or paths. A second tab shows models and the context window used per agent. **Reset all-time counts** clears it.
- **Credit caps (spec 011).** Optional AI-credit cap per session (runtime minimum 30), set in the new-session dialog or from the chat header chip, with a global default in Settings. Cards and the chat header show used/cap; it is a **soft** cap (one response can overshoot). When reached: **Limit reached**, then Add credits / Remove cap / Stop here. `[UNVERIFIED]` against a real exhausted cap (it costs real money); covered by fixtures and the simulator.
- **Permissions (specs 003, 018).** Change an agent's mode (Read-only, Ask first, Allow all) any time. The saved mode of each folder is **restored exactly** after a restart, *Allow all* included, with a visible *restored* note until you change it; switching to Allow all still asks for confirmation. (The alpha.2 build itself still labels the last mode *Allow edits*; it was renamed *Allow all* right after, see alpha.3.) *Approve for this session* grants never survive a restart. Shell grants cover the **exact command** unless you explicitly choose "any arguments" (never for shells, interpreters, `sudo`, pipes or redirects). **Reads outside the folder** are asked (Ask) or denied (Read-only).
- **Three Feathers About (spec 008 FR-018).** The app names **Three Feathers** as owner and credits vikramgorla, fjcabello and rubenlupi (GitHub handles) in the OS About panel and Settings > About, `LICENSE` and the installer metadata.
- **Chat and office (specs 005 to 007, 015 to 017).** Full-screen chat with a slim agent strip, previous sessions per folder (resume, plus read-only VS Code chat history), new session per folder, model and reasoning-effort pickers, a Thinking timeline, nested sub-agent blocks, safe Markdown in chat, collapsible room doors, a layout that fits 15 agents at 1440x900 and 1280x800.
- **Safer packaging.** `DURBAR_*` developer hooks are ignored in packaged builds unless `DURBAR_DEV=1`. The Copilot SDK is pinned exactly (1.0.22).

### Honest limits

- **Unsigned, not notarized** (signing is spec 014, a draft that needs the maintainer's approval to buy certificates).
- **Untested on real Macs and Windows PCs.** CI builds the macOS and Windows packages and runs unit tests on all three OSes, but no one has launched them on real hardware. The macOS/Windows About panel and installer metadata were not looked at. Only Linux was run by hand.
- Needs a GitHub Copilot login (`copilot login`); usage bills against your own plan. Independent project, not affiliated with GitHub or Microsoft.
- Known gaps are listed per spec (`[KNOWN GAP]`) and under "Known limits" in the README.

## v0.1.0-alpha.1 (draft, never published)

First packaged build (milestone 5): office view, Copilot adapter, permission modes, attachments, unsigned dmg/zip, nsis/portable and AppImage through a draft pre-release. The draft stays in the repository's Releases list; it is not published.
